**Risks.**
- Two month lengths in one set of notes (33 vs 30.333): if the chart divides by one and a budget line by the other, every $/M figure disagrees by 8.8 % with no error shown — the ruling must be on the ledger before any $/M is rendered.
- The sheet's 'Equal' is false in the shipped parser: parseABC('3600.3600..3600') = 3601.0003, one B + one C past the period; reusing it unguarded releases $1.00 more than the deposit and marks the end of the MoT 12 minutes late.
- Perihelion jitter makes the DOWN day elastic (9.28 h in the 2026 year, 3.40 d in 2027); anything that assumes a 24-h day 365 or a 365-day year — pod-clock's MAX_YUG_PER_MIN divides by 525,600 — drifts against the calendar the operator ruled.
- CST vs the bank app's wall clock: Austin is on daylight time on 2026-10-01, so '07:00' on the phone may be 12:00Z, not 13:00Z; the difference is $4.95 of release and every mark on the chart moves an hour.
- Elapsed-time $/min shown as a wage: $3,604.49 over 728 elapsed hours is $4.95/h, below the Texas floor; printing that beside 웃 = M × T invites the exact confusion pod-clock forbids.
- The pilot's escrow is modelled, not custodial: any word implying the app holds, releases or moves money is a claim of more than happened; 'withdraw' must read as a recorded intent until a rail exists.
- A third copy of assertSource (drone, drs, financial) is the class the FIX THE CLASS law refuses; extract once or the next domain inherits three divergent validators.
- The per-device ledger pattern (pod-store) caps history at 60 entries and evicts the oldest pod when storage is full — acceptable for a pod, fatal for money; Financial-2525's store must never evict and must say so when the device refuses a write.
- Real personal financial figures — the paycheck, the bank description string, the budget — are committed to the repository's ledger by design; confirm the repository's visibility before revision 0.002 appends more of them, and keep the screenshot uncommitted as the ask already does.
- The sheet's recorded arithmetic slips (47,620 / 4,762) will tempt a renderer to 'fix' them; the persisted transcription is the source and must render the written figure beside the checked one, never replace it.
- Scope creep into the business frame: 4-4-5 months, the P&L ladder and up-front business escrow are 'test later' by the operator's own order; they may be declared as draft CRS rows in 0.001 but not built.
- The shared Cadence type has no Y and no h/min/s; widening it touches every innovation-data consumer, while a parallel financial ladder rebuilds a primitive — extend with a gate, in one place.
- The chart's Fixed/Variable budget lines pre-filled from the sheet sum to a deficit against an Income that is not the real paycheck; shown without their source and stamp they read as a false present-day statement.

**The three things revision 0.001 must do first.**
- Open the record before anything else: docs/financial-2525/financial-2525.v00.00.json at Version 00.00 · revision 0.001 kind 'ask' with handoff = the ask path and handoffSha256 = 5880abb0cbe60208c87073e24d9b7ec8d3b5ad807af6049fa4b68c2b5318dd6d; docs/traceability/financial-2525.ledger.json rev 1 carrying the verbatim ask, the five addenda and the three sheets as transcribed (slips recorded); one CRS row FIN-01 'A transaction is $, Start, MoT' with in/out/section/metric/verify; a financial-render that shares the extracted assertSource and runs --check in test:ci; the R-CORE badge fed by fromLedgerJson. Commit that artefact alone, report SHA · pushed · LIVE/UNVERIFIED.
- Put the MoT law on the record and under gates before a chart exists: one ledger decision ruling MoT = one whole unit (0 → 3600.3600..3600), the three spellings equal, month = 91 ÷ 3 with 33 carried as data, CST fixed −6, Day 1 = PERIHELION_UTC with an elastic DOWN day; the real paycheck as the only fixture — $3,604.49 · 2026.10.01_07.00..00 CST · 30.333 → $0.082520/min, $14.85 at +3 h, SoI day 271 Q3 d89, end SoI day 302 — proven by fin-abc-full-unit, fin-accrual-law, fin-calendar-perihelion, fin-ltu-roundtrip, fin-escrow-states.
- Ship the personal surface at /financial-2525 as revision 0.002+: login → one record → the phone chart (escrowed / released / withdrawable / cash on hand with the Start, +3 h, MoT-end, Q4 and DOWN-day marks), the stat tile, ⅓Q / ⅔Q / Q windows, addendum-3 budget lines with the sheet's figures tagged by source, every string via t() in 33 languages, dark ground, no machine words, badge last; then run the twelve-lens review on each revision and leave the business frame declared as draft rows until the personal pilot has received feedback.

### Asar — synthesis and outcome validation: what the person actually gets on the screen, and whether the outcome (cash on hand, in real time) is proven by replay rather than asserted.

**Readiness:** B-

**One sentence.** The person opens the phone and sees one honest number — cash on hand — rising at $0.0825 a minute from a $3,604.49 deposit whose orbit runs 0.0000..0000 to 3600.3600..3600 over 30.333 days, and every figure on that screen replays from a three-field record that is appended, gated, and never edited.

**What the notes specify (23).**
- The name and scope: "this entire session will be called Financial-2525" — "to document real-time transactions and also see financial management in 91 days for a quarter with down day 365 of year".
- The year anchor: "day resets everywhere at perihelion each year for Austin Texas as CST standard" (PERIHELION_UTC 2026 = 2026-01-03T17:16Z = 11:16 CST; fixed −6, no daylight shift).
- The unit: "we will use MoT financials in notes; to obtain $/min of an individuals financials".
- The basis and the framework: "◬ ♡ 웃 Session as a basis for R-CORE TO start our wireframe. From the get go we will have revision control using the same framework as drone 2525" (Version 00.00 · revision 0.001 · domain JSON → rendered CRS → append-only ledger → R-CORE badge → gates).
- Tenancy: "each user gets their own log in; and we will log financial deposits and assign deposit date and length of transactions for real time accrural".
- The worked example: "for example i got paid $3604.49 monthly"; bank screen: Amount $3,604.49 · Posted 09/29/2026 · Status Pending · memo PENDING DIRECT DEPOSIT · payroll, regular.
- The record shape: "Each ttansaction gets amount, Deposit day and time, and MoT length of time"; "pay check example is$ deposit, October 1, 2026 at 07:00, 30.333" (ask line 73 reads 30.333 as 91 ÷ 3).
- The escrow law: "goal is to pay escrow that releases $/min into account that one can see and deposit as needed".
- The business up-front and the hold: "for bainess they pay up frint financials of 2 weeks or one minth, and 3 hrs later individual can withdraw to their account if really in need of cash".
- Notation: "all is defaulted to A.B..C so MoT=30.33 converted to 3600.3600..3600 from Celestial-2525 scale (for earth), which will allow for financial system to convert to Mars and future planets".
- LTU: "it is important A.B..C is used for time and easily converted to Earth local time units LTU, similar to how drone positioning and planetary positioning uses A.B..C".
- Sheet timestamps and durations: `Start 2026.01.11_13.11..11 · Stop 2026.01.22_13.11..11`; `1×/mth → 0000.00.33_00.00.00 | 33 days · 2×/mth 0000.00.16_12.00..00 | 16.5 days`.
- Sheet 1 equalities: `ssu: 3600.0000..0000 · 3600.3600..3600 · 3599.3599..3599 } Equal`; `LSU 3600 – 3600.3600 · 3`; `Reference ↳ 365.25`.
- Sheet 1 trinity time measures: `♡ → M_LTU · 웃 → M_∞(33) · ◬ → M_SS`; project value `♡ = min · 웃 = × Min Wage · ◬ = min × Efficiency Gain ↳ Trade for $`; `$3/Day = $99/M∞(33)`; `Unlock 🔒 $/min System · Baseline: $/Day`.
- Sheet 3 outcome and frames: `Outcome: Cash On Hand`; `$/Balance · $/End of Mth · $ Target · 11 · 22 · 33D`; `Options – Time Frame · Real-Time 33D, 66D, 99D (30.3, 60.6, 90.9)`; `Analysis: D = Day · W = Week = 7D · M = month = 33D · Q = Quarter = 99D · Y = Year = 365D`.
- Sheet 3 iconology: `Day = D [1] · Month = M · Quarter = Q · Week = W [7] · Minute = m · Second = s`.
- Sheet 3 budget (the example, as data): `Home 700 F · Car 1,800 F · Insurance 200 F · Electric 150 V · Food 300 V · Fitness 50 F · Fun 200 V · Income 3,200 F` (F 2,750 + V 650 = 3,400 against 3,200 income in the sheet's own example).
- Sheet 3 rate ladder: `144 $/D = 0.1 $/m` · `Basis | ÷24 | ÷6` · `$/D: 144 → 138 → 137` · `Week 1,008 W7` · `14,256 $/Q99`; the ask records two slips as written (47,620 → 47,520 min; 4,762 → 4,752 $/M33) and does not silently fix them.
- Addendum 3 budget lines for the personal pilot: "Income, Fixed / Variable, from there Home, Auto, Insurance, Utilities, Fitness, Fun, Groceries, Dining Out, and other basic budgeting items … personal budget to $/min, $/sec (using MoT that converts to Hour, Min, Second)".
- Addendum 4: "all 12 AsM will contribute until simulated sufficiently so system complements Vision-2525 for personal FINANCIAL management and tracking tokenization 9999 HI TOKEN CAP" — the one ceiling is YUG_CEILING = 9999 in lib/pod-yug.ts, never a second one.
- Addendum 5 / user request: "ONCE PERSONAL FRAMEWORK RECEIVES FEEDBACK WELL TEST BUSINESS" — the business frame (`4-4-5 · [91 day] 13 · Rev – COG – [MGN] – R+D – MKT – Sales – SG+A – [OP INCOME] – DA – [EBITDA] – [EBIT]`) is declared and marked `Test Later`.
- Persistence proof: the ask file's sha256 5880abb0cbe60208c87073e24d9b7ec8d3b5ad807af6049fa4b68c2b5318dd6d matches its sidecar (verified in this review).
- The repo primitives exist and are pure: abc-3600 (toABC/format/parseABC/abcToValue, FULL_UNIT_ABC = 3600.3600..3600), ucrs-2525 (FULL_ORBIT, HU 0 = perihelion, ltu = spotu × tSec, tzFromLon → CST −6), soi-calendar (soi91: Q 91 · M 91/3 · W 7 · intercalary 1 · PERIHELION_UTC · spreadPerMin), pod-clock (append-only two-kind event log, measure by seq), pod-yug (mint = hours × M, YUG_CEILING, standing), pod-store (appendPod/replayPod), architect-economy (ratePerMin, fmtUsd, allocate), innovation-data (CADENCE_UNIT Q|M|W|D, fmtPerCadence, perMinFinancials over days × 1440).

**Ambiguities → declared default.**
- **Which month is the month: the sheets' 33 days (`M = month = 33D`, `$99/M∞(33)`, `47,520 min M33`), the paycheck's 30.333 (= 91 ÷ 3), or the calendar's 91-day quarter (3 × 33 = 99 ≠ 91)?** → The stored MoT of every transaction is its own field (the paycheck's is 91/3 d, printed 30.333) and it is the ONLY denominator of that transaction's $/min; the calendar is soi91 (Q = 91, M = 91/3, W = 7, Y = 4 × 91 + the down period); the 33D / 66D / 99D chips are VIEWPORTS on the chart (sheet 3 itself parenthesises them as 30.3, 60.6, 90.9) and never re-price a figure. — _If a frame re-priced, the same $3,604.49 deposit would read $3,604/M on one chip and $3,921/M33 on the next — two numbers for one paycheck, which is the panel claiming more than happened. A viewport changes what you look at, not what you earned._
- **What does "3 hrs later individual can withdraw" permit: only what escrow has released by then ($14.85 on the example), or the whole up-front deposit as an advance "if really in need of cash"?** → Released-only. withdrawable(t) = 0 for t < start + 3 h; afterwards withdrawable = released(t) − Σ withdrawn. A withdrawal above that refuses and is recorded as refused. An advance is a separate decision to take on the ledger, not an unstated default. — _Every released dollar is then backed by escrow it has already left; withdrawn can never exceed released, so escrow + released = deposit holds at every minute and the chart cannot show a negative escrow. The operator can widen it later on the record; nothing can un-pay an advance._
- **Which instant starts the accrual: the operator's declared `October 1, 2026 at 07:00` or the bank's `Posted 09/29/2026 · Pending`?** → Start = the operator's declared deposit stamp 2026.10.01_07.00..00 CST (13:00Z). The bank's posted date is stored as `postedAt` metadata and drawn as a hollow, uncounted pre-deposit tick labelled pending. — _He wrote the record himself in the three-field form the system defines; the bank screen still says Pending. Two days of accrual is $237.66 — the record must name which date accrues, and the person's own stamp is the one he can check._
- **Is `07:00` CST always, or the phone's local time?** → Always CST −6 with no daylight shift; the stamp is written `2026.10.01_07.00..00 CST` and stored as the UTC instant 2026-10-01T13:00Z; the phone's zone is never consulted (no getTimezoneOffset / toLocale* on the calendar or record path). — _"day resets everywhere at perihelion … for Austin Texas as CST standard" fixes the clock for every device; a traveller's paycheck must not shift by hours because the phone crossed a zone._
- **Does $/min accrue on the down day (day 365) and across the perihelion reset?** → Yes, every minute. The down period only lacks a quarter/month label; a MoT that spans it releases exactly $ ÷ MoT-minutes on those minutes too. — _Released money is never lost. Pausing release on an unlabelled day would under-release a 30.333-day paycheck by a day's pay ($118.83) with no transaction to show for it._
- **Is Day 1 the perihelion INSTANT (11:16 CST in 2026) or midnight CST of the perihelion day; and how long is the down period when perihelion-to-perihelion is 364.16 d (2025→26), 364.39 d (2026→27) or 367.40 d (2027→28)?** → Day 1 begins at the perihelion instant from PERIHELION_UTC; days are uniform 1,440-minute blocks from that instant; 4 × 91 = 364 days are the four quarters; whatever remains until the next perihelion instant is the down period, labelled "day 365 · down" (9 h 17 min in the 2026 year, 3.40 d in 2027). The seed table's own note — confirm annually — is carried onto the screen as a badge until confirmed. — _It reuses soiYearStartUTC unchanged and keeps every day the same length, which is why the operator asked for CST standard; the jitter is astronomical fact, not a modelling choice, and hiding it would be inventing a figure._
- **Does a paycheck mint 웃? At $3,604.49 / 728 elapsed hours = $4.95/h that is 0.68× the Texas floor; at 173.3 worked hours it is 2.87×.** → No mint in Financial-2525. The pod is the mint ("웃 is minted only for TIME CLOCKED BY THE PLATFORM"). The screen shows one read-only equivalence — $ ÷ the stamped local floor = hours at 1× — capped for display at YUG_CEILING imported from lib/pod-yug, and the source contains no `mint(` and no literal 9999. — _A paycheck is not clocked time witnessed by a pod of three; minting from it would either be 0.68× (wrong by elapsed hours) or trust a hand-typed hours figure, the exact failure pod-clock exists to prevent. Tracking the 9,999 cap means reading it, not re-declaring it._
- **"each user gets their own log in" — is 0.001 an Auth0 login with a cloud ledger, or the per-device pattern the pod uses?** → 0.001 keys the ledger by the login id (Auth0 sub when signed in) in the pod-store append/replay shape on the device, `exel-fin:<login>`, WITHOUT pod-store's eviction; the cloud half appends to the same shape later without changing the contract. One device shows one login's record. — _It is the operator's own real paycheck on his own phone; the record must be true beyond the phone's memory, but the one thing 0.001 can guarantee today is that the shape never changes and nothing is evicted._
- **Where does a withdrawal go — is there a rail to the person's bank account in 0.001?** → No rail. A withdrawal is a transaction the person records here and moves by hand; the panel says so in one sentence in the person's terms and never names a vendor, a database or a migration. — _The signer never meets the machine; the ledger's job in 0.001 is to be the honest record the rail will later reconcile to, exactly as architect-economy defers to the audited Cube 5/8 ledger._
- **Does 0.001 carry the budget lines (Income · Fixed / Variable · Home … Dining Out) and the Swings chart?** → 0.001 carries the three-field transaction, the escrow chart and cash on hand; the budget lines and the swings overlay are FIN-02 at 0.002, seeded with the sheet's example figures as data (F 2,750 · V 650 · Income 3,200) and the corrected minute count (47,520). — _Addendum 3 names the lines but gives no per-user entry flow; the outcome the operator named is cash on hand, and the first screen must prove that one number before it decorates it._
- **The sheet's 4-4-5 / 13-week business ladder and the two-week up-front — in or out of 0.001?** → Out. The business frame is a declared, dated, `Test Later` row (FIN-09 placeholder, status draft) and no P&L code ships until the personal pilot has received feedback. — _The user's request in this run is the law: "ONCE PERSONAL FRAMEWORK RECEIVES FEEDBACK WELL TEST BUSINESS". Building it now is rework by definition._

**MoT law, as this lens read it.** Asar reads the MoT law as: A MoT is the money's orbit, and the deposit is its perihelion. ONE number is stored per transaction — the MoT in Earth LTU (for the paycheck, 91 ÷ 3 days exactly, printed 30.333: 728 h = 43,680 min = 2,620,800 s; the three-decimal 30.333 × 1440 would be 43,679.52 min, 0.48 min short, so the rational is stored and the decimal is the print). Every screen figure derives from it: $3,604.49 ÷ 43,680 = $0.0825204/min · $4.9512/h · $118.83/day · $0.001375/s. A.B..C over the period (the operator's \"30.33 converted to 3600.3600..3600\"): the whole MoT period is ONE unit, exactly as UCRS-2525 makes one orbit 3,600 HU with HU 0 = perihelion and prints completion as FULL_ORBIT 3600.3600..3600 (FULL_UNIT_ABC in lib/abc-3600). So for the 30.333-day paycheck: A = whole MoT periods elapsed since the deposit (0 throughout the period, 1 at the next deposit); B = 1/3,600 of the period = 12.1333 min = 728 s, worth $1.00125 of release; C = 1/3,600 of B = 0.2022 s, worth $0.000278. Deposit = 0.0000..0000 (2026.10.01_07.00..00 CST). The 3-hour hold ends at 180 min ÷ 12.1333 = 0.0014..3007 with $14.85 released and $0 withdrawable before it. The next deposit = 1.0000..0000, which the celestial convention prints as 3600.3600..3600 — the sheet's three \"Equal\" writings are the same instant seen three ways: 3600.0000..0000 (3,600 B-ticks = one whole A), 3600.3600..3600 (every field at its ceiling, the UCRS full-orbit stamp), 3599.3599..3599 (the last tick before rollover, one C short). Conversion to Earth LTU is one multiply, the same line ucrsAt already uses (ltu = spotu × tSec) with the orbit period replaced by the pay period: elapsed seconds = abcToValue({a,b,c}) × 2,620,800; then LTU → D · W · M · Q · Y through soi91 (D = 1,440 min, W = 7 D, M = 91/3 D, Q = 91 D, Y = 4 × 91 + the down period) and h · min · s (3,600 · 60 · 1). The same MoT also has a unit-anchored print in abc-3600's hours grammar (A = hour, B = second, C = 1/3,600 s): 728.0000..0000 — used when the person asks \"how long\", while the period print answers \"how far along\". The deposit's position in the SoI year is a third, calendar reading: 270.822 days after the 2026 perihelion = day 271 = Q3 day 89 = HU 2669 of the Earth orbit; the paycheck crosses into Q4 (day 274) 2.178 days in, on 2026.10.03_11.16 CST, and ends on day 301 (Q4 day 28). Mars changes nothing in the record: A.B..C and the $/tick stay, only the LTU table swaps (a sol of 88,775 s) — which is what \"convert to Mars\" means.

**Chart.** axes: x = time within the MoT period, from the deposit (0.0000..0000 · 2026.10.01_07.00..00 CST) to the next deposit (3600.3600..3600 · 2026.10.31_15.00..00 CST), double-labelled: A.B..C ticks above (0000 · 0900 · 1800 · 2700 · 3600 B) and Earth LTU below (D1 … D30 of the period, with the SoI day number 271 … 301 on tap). y = USD from $0 to the deposit ($3,604.49); the axis ceiling IS the deposit and is never exceeded, so the chart cannot claim more than was paid. · series: Escrow remaining — falls linearly from $3,604.49 to $0 at $0.0825204/min (dim fill; state: escrow). · Released — rises linearly $0 → $3,604.49, derived as min(now − start, MoT) × rate (primary line; state: releasing). · Withdrawn — cumulative step, one riser per withdrawal transaction with its stamp (neutral line; state: withdrawn). · Withdrawable = Released − Withdrawn, zero before start + 3 h — the accent band between the two lines; this band is CASH ON HAND from escrow and is the headline number. · Budget burn (0.002, off in 0.001) — fixed + variable spread at their own $/min as a dashed reference, the sheet's Swings overlay; only once the person has entered lines. · marks: Deposit dot with the three-field stamp: $3,604.49 · 2026.10.01_07.00..00 CST · MoT 30.333 (728.0000..0000). · Hold band, hatched, 0.0000..0000 → 0.0014..3007 (0 → 3 h): released $14.85, withdrawable $0, labelled 'held'. · NOW cursor with four readouts: released · withdrawn · withdrawable · the tick ($0.0825/min · $0.00138/s), plus the A.B..C position and the LTU elapsed. · Quarter boundary Q3 | Q4 at 2.178 days in (2026.10.03_11.16 CST, SoI day 274) as a thin vertical rule. · Each withdrawal as a labelled riser with its stamp and A.B..C position; a refused withdrawal as a hollow mark with the word refused. · Period end at 3600.3600..3600 with the next deposit's due stamp. · The bank's 'posted 09/29 · pending' as a hollow pre-deposit tick, never counted. · Down period, when the viewport contains it, as a hatched column 'day 365 · down' — accrual line continues through it. · Perihelion / year reset as a heavier rule labelled 'Day 1 · perihelion' with the confirm-annually badge until the table is confirmed. · states: escrow — deposited, held by escrow, not yet released (dim). → releasing — leaving escrow at $/min; visible every second (primary). → held — released but inside the 3-hour hold: withdrawable $0 (hatched). → withdrawable — released − withdrawn after the hold: cash on hand (accent). → withdrawn — moved to the person's account by a recorded transaction (neutral); the panel never says 'complete' while escrow > $0. · cadence: Released and withdrawable are re-derived every second on screen from (now − start) × rate — nothing accumulates in state; the chart re-plots once a minute and on every transaction; the ledger is written only on a deposit or a withdrawal (two event kinds, as pod-clock has two); on reopen the screen replays the ledger and lands on the same cent. · phone: One column at 375 px with 16 px gutters and no horizontal scroll: cash on hand first (≥ 40 px, the outcome), the $/min · $/s tick under it, the chart at 100% width × ~220 px with tap-to-scrub (the compare-panel scrubber pattern), then the 33D · 66D · 99D viewport chips (never re-pricing), then the transaction list (each row: $, Start stamp, MoT in both prints), then the R-CORE badge last in normal flow; dark ground by default; every label through t() in all 33 languages.

**Invariants.**
- Each transaction gets amount, Deposit day and time, and MoT length of time — a record missing any of the three refuses before it is written, and a written record is never edited. — `scripts/financial-render.mjs assertSource + frontend/tests/financial-record.test.mjs`
- Escrow releases $/min into an account one can see: released(t) = min(t − start, MoT) × ($ ÷ MoT-minutes), never decreasing, and escrow(t) + released(t) = the deposit at every minute. — `frontend/tests/financial-escrow.test.mjs`
- 3 hours later the individual can withdraw: withdrawable is $0 before start + 3 h, then released − withdrawn; a withdrawal above it refuses and the refusal is on the record. — `frontend/tests/financial-escrow.test.mjs (hold section)`
- Outcome: Cash On Hand — the headline number is derived by replaying the ledger, and any device replaying the same ledger at the same instant shows the same cent. — `frontend/tests/financial-replay.test.mjs`
- Day resets everywhere at perihelion each year for Austin Texas as CST standard — day n = floor((t − PERIHELION_UTC[y]) ÷ 1,440 min) + 1, fixed −6, no daylight shift, quarter = 91 days, the remainder before the next perihelion is the down period; no phone-zone call on the calendar path. — `frontend/tests/financial-calendar.test.mjs`
- All is defaulted to A.B..C and easily converted to Earth LTU — every printed duration or position comes from lib/abc-3600, parse(format(x)) round-trips within one C, and there is no second Base-3600 implementation in financial code. — `frontend/tests/financial-abc.test.mjs`
- 9999 HI TOKEN CAP — the only ceiling is YUG_CEILING imported from lib/pod-yug; the literal 9999 and any mint( call are absent from Financial-2525 source. — `frontend/tests/financial-yug.test.mjs`
- Revision control using the same framework as drone 2525 — one editable JSON, generated views that --check refuses on drift INSIDE test:ci, revisions strictly increasing, the traceability ledger append-only under a chain hash. — `test:financial-render (node ../scripts/financial-render.mjs --check) + frontend/tests/financial-ledger.test.mjs`
- Each user gets their own log in — a ledger is keyed by the login and a device never shows another login's record; no transaction is ever evicted to make room. — `frontend/tests/financial-tenant.test.mjs`
- The panel never claims more than happened — on the glass withdrawable ≤ released ≤ deposited, a pending bank posting is shown as pending and never counted, and the screen names no database, migration or vendor. — `frontend/tests/financial-glass.test.mjs (data-fin-* attributes) + frontend/tests/sign-lexicon-voice.test.mjs pattern`

**Risks.**
- Perihelion jitter: the seed table gives perihelion-to-perihelion years of 364.16 d (2025→26), 364.39 d (2026→27) and 367.40 d (2027→28), so with Day 1 at the instant the 'down day 365' is 9 h 17 min long in the 2026 year and 3.40 days in 2027; the table itself says confirm annually and nothing in the repo does.
- Two months in one ask: every $/M figure on sheet 3 is on 33 days, the paycheck and the quarter are on 91/3 and 91; if a viewport re-prices, one paycheck reads $3,604/M on one chip and $3,921/M33 on the next.
- Sheet arithmetic slips (47,620 → 47,520 min; 4,762 → 4,752 $/M33) collide with the published-figure-is-read law if seeded verbatim; the seed must carry the corrected values with the slip noted, as the ask already does.
- Real money on a phone: pod-store's evictOldestPod and MAX_ENTRIES = 60 would silently drop the oldest transaction on a full device; reuse the append/replay shape, never the eviction.
- '3 hrs later … withdraw' read as an advance lets withdrawn exceed released and breaks escrow + released = deposit; the default refuses, and widening it is a ledger decision, not a code default.
- 'Withdraw to their account' implies a rail; 0.001 has none, and the screen must say in one sentence that the withdrawal is recorded here and moved by the person — the signer never meets the machine.
- The Drone precedent's `drone-render.mjs --check` is not in frontend/package.json at all despite the script's own comment; copying the framework verbatim copies the gap unless the Financial gate is wired into test:ci and seen to fail on drift.
- Start-date ambiguity (declared 2026-10-01 07:00 vs bank posted 09/29 pending) is worth $237.66 of accrual; storing both without naming which accrues is the panel claiming more than happened.
- A phone in another zone typing 07:00: without an explicit CST stamp on the record the paycheck's orbit shifts by hours per trip.
- Lexicon: five states, three prints and a three-field form add roughly forty keys; each must exist in all 33 languages or tests/lexicon-coverage.test.mjs fails the build.
- The 웃 relationship is unstated; a mint from a paycheck is 0.68× on elapsed hours or a hand-typed worked-hours claim — both are the failure pod-clock exists to prevent; keep the mint in the pod and show a read-only equivalence.
- Business frame creep: any P&L, 4-4-5 or two-week-up-front code in 0.001 is rework against the user's own request in this run ('ONCE PERSONAL FRAMEWORK RECEIVES FEEDBACK WELL TEST BUSINESS').

**Ambiguities → declared default.**
- **Which month is the money's month — the paycheck's 30.333 days (91 ÷ 3) or the sheet's M = 33D, whose own parenthetical reads '(30.3, 60.6, 90.9)'?** → A transaction's MoT is exactly what was typed (30.333 for the paycheck) and money accrues on that; the personal frames are 33 / 66 / 99 REAL days as sheet 1 states ('33 days'), and every frame label prints its pay-MoT equivalent beside it (30.333 · 60.667 · 91) — never a silent conversion between the two. — _Sheet 1 says '33 days' verbatim and the paycheck says 30.333; the SoI law (soi-calendar.ts) is elapsed calendar minutes, so money must ride real time; keeping both numbers visible lets the operator rule instead of the build guessing._
- **In the MoT grammar is A the whole unit (abc-3600.ts: format(1) = 1.0000..0000 is full) or 3600ths of the whole (FD-01: 3600.3600..3600 is full)?** → FD-01 — A = 3600ths of the MoT, B = 3600ths of an A, C = 3600ths of a B, mirroring UCRS HU 0..3600; FULL is a marker string (isFull), never an arithmetic value; abcToValue is never applied to it; the surface says which grammar a number is in. — _The operator's sentence '30.33 converted to 3600.3600..3600' is satisfied only by the 3600ths reading; the sheet's three 'Equal' writings are equal only as a marker (as numbers 3599.3599..3599 < 3600.0000..0000 < 3600.3600..3600)._
- **Is '07:00' fixed CST (UTC−6, 13:00Z) or the phone's civil time, which in October is CDT (12:00Z)?** → Fixed CST −6 all year (FD-02): the record stores the instant, prints the stamp with 'CST standard', and the bank's civil 07:00 CDT (one hour earlier) is provenance only. — _'Austin Texas as CST standard' — a standard has no daylight shift; ucrs-2525.ts tzFromLon already carries −6 CST; the gate proves an October date still maps to −6._
- **Posted 09/29 (Pending) vs the deposit 'October 1, 2026 at 07:00' — which instant starts release?** → The operator-ASSIGNED deposit day and time (2026.10.01_07.00..00) starts release; the posted date and 'PENDING DIRECT DEPOSIT' are memo provenance on the same entry. — _'we will log financial deposits and assign deposit date' — the person assigns the start; a bank's posting date is evidence, not the rule._
- **Is the 3-hour hold once per deposit or a rolling hold on each released minute?** → Once per deposit (FD-03): from Start + 3 h everything released to date and thereafter is withdrawable; before it, released money is visible but held. — _'3 hrs later individual can withdraw' names the deposit event; a rolling hold would be a second law nobody stated._
- **Does day 1 begin at the perihelion INSTANT (2026: 11:15 CST on Jan 3) or at 00:00 CST of that date?** → The instant; day N = whole 24 h periods from it (as the in-flight calendar does); the CST civil date is printed beside every day number. — _'day resets everywhere at perihelion' — only the instant is the same everywhere; soi-calendar.ts anchors Day 1 to the instant too._
- **What happens when the next perihelion arrives before day 364 completes — FY 2028 → 2029 is 363.24 days on the in-flight table, so four 91-day quarters do not fit and the down day is −0.76 d?** → A quarter is never cut: the year resets at the perihelion in every year where the four quarters fit (2024–2027 and 2029 on), and in a short orbit the year runs to the end of day 364 with 'next perihelion arrived during Q4 day 91 · deficit 0.76 d' DECLARED on the record; the operator's own 'Reference ↳ 365.25' (a fixed mean year) is the alternative and needs his word as FD-10. — _Financial management on a 91-day grid cannot tolerate a 90.24-day Q4; nothing in the notes allows truncation; the sheets cite 365.25 twice; the in-flight gate tests only 2026 → 2027 and would pass while 2028 breaks._
- **Does money accrue on the down day?** → Yes — the calendar grid (quarter, month, week) is suspended on the down day; the $/min clock is not. — _Rent and pay run on elapsed time; soi-calendar.ts states period math in ELAPSED calendar minutes; the paycheck's MoT is in real days._
- **Duration grammar — '0000.00.33_00.00.00' (no double dot) or '0000.00.16_12.00..00'?** → Canonical 0000.00.DD_HH.MM..SS; the parser accepts both spellings, the formatter emits only the double-dot form (as the in-flight mot.ts does). — _A.B..C puts the double dot before the finest unit; one grammar for stamps and durations means one regex and one gate._
- **What do the MM and YYYY places mean inside a duration string?** → M = 33 d and Y = 365 d inside a DURATION (the sheet's iconology), while a MoT typed as a decimal (30.333) is taken literally; the surface never routes a typed MoT through the 33-day month. — _The sheet defines M = 33D and Y = 365D; the paycheck's MoT is typed as days; mixing the two would move a pay period by 2.67 days silently._
- **What unlocks the 'Unlock 🔒 $/min System · Baseline: $/Day'?** → $/D is the default rung; the $/min and $/s rungs unlock the moment one real deposit with a MoT is on the record — a display unlock, never a paywall. — _The sheet names $/Day the baseline and $/min the unlock; only a real transaction with a length gives $/min meaning._
- **Which login for revision 0.001, and is the record isolated per login in the cloud?** → The app's existing Auth0 gate on the route; the record is per device under exel-fin:<owner>; the cloud half is declared honest (FD-07: per-login Supabase isolation waits on wiring the login into Supabase) and the surface says so in one sentence in the person's words. — _The operator's own phone is the pilot; pod-store.ts is already the per-device append-only pattern; a faked isolation would be the signer-meets-the-machine defect._
- **Are sheet 3's budget figures (Income 3,200, Home 700 …) the operator's budget?** → No — they are seed DATA labelled 'sheet example' and editable; the only real money on the surface is the one paycheck; nothing else is invented. — _'treat figures as data, never invent others'; the sheet's Income 3,200 differs from the real 3,604.49, which proves it is an example._
- **Does the paycheck mint 웃 against the 9,999 cap?** → No — a paycheck is settlement, not mint; revision 0.001 imports YUG_CEILING from pod-yug.ts as the only cap and mints nothing; any $ → 웃 reading waits on the twelve-lens loop. — _pod-yug.ts: 'NO WAGE ENTERS THE MINT' and 웃 = M × hours is the only mint; a $ ÷ rate = 웃 formula would repeat a published defect._
- **How is a released cent rounded, and does the sum of released ever miss the deposit?** → releasedCents = round(amount × fraction), monotonic by construction, clamped at the amount, and at FULL the residual is released so Σ released = deposit exactly; two overlapping deposits are rounded each, then summed. — _A cent lost between escrow and balance breaks 'released ≤ deposited' from the other side; the in-flight accrual.ts already rounds this way and the +40 d assertion covers the clamp._
- **Which perihelion table is true — soi-calendar.ts PERIHELION_UTC (2024 00:39Z · 2026 17:16Z · 2028 12:11Z) or the in-flight financial table (00:38Z · 17:15Z · 12:28Z)?** → ONE table: extend soi-calendar.ts PERIHELION_UTC to 2035 with provenance rows and import it from lib/financial-2525/calendar.ts; until then a gate asserts the two agree to the minute or names the year. — _NO-REWORK — the primitive exists; three copies drifted once already (defect 27); the JSON records only the 2028 discrepancy while 2024 and 2026 also differ._

**MoT law, as this lens read it.** Through this lens the MoT law is a PROGRESS coordinate, not a duration: the MoT is one whole (the pay period), and any instant inside it is its elapsed fraction f = (t − Start) ÷ MoT written in Base-3600 exactly as the celestial UCRS-2525 writes an orbit (HU 0 = perihelion, HU 3600 = a full orbit). For the paycheck, MoT = 30.333 d = 91 ÷ 3 d = 43,680 min = 2,620,800 s. A = 3600ths of the period, so one A = 728 s (12 min 8 s), A runs 0..3600; B = 3600ths of an A = 0.2022 s; C = 3600ths of a B = 56.2 µs. '30.33 converts to 3600.3600..3600' means the period's own length is the full scale: the deposit instant reads 0.0000..0000, halfway reads 1800.0000..0000, +3 h reads 14.3006..2136, +1 day reads 118.2452..2690, and the end (2026.10.31_15.00..00 CST) reads 3600.3600..3600 — the FULL marker that the sheet's three 'Equal' writings (3600.0000..0000 · 3600.3600..3600 · 3599.3599..3599) all denote; it is a marker, never an arithmetic value. Money rides the same scale: $3,604.49 ÷ 3600 = $1.00125 per A and $0.000278 per B. Conversion to Earth LTU is ltu_seconds = f × MoT_seconds (the mirror of ucrsAt's ltu = spotu × tSec), then s → min → h → D, and D × 7 = W, D × 33 = M33, D × 99 = Q99, D × 365 = Y365 for the sheet's analysis ladder — while the pay MoT's own month is 30.333 d, printed beside M33, never folded into it. On that ladder the paycheck is $118.83/D, $4.951/h, $0.08252/min, $0.001375/s. Because f never names a planet, a 30-sol Martian MoT reads the same A.B..C at every fraction and only the LTU seconds differ. Two grammars now coexist in the repo and must never share a screen unlabelled: abc-3600.ts where A is the whole unit (1.0000..0000 is full) and this MoT reading where A is a 3600th (3600.3600..3600 is full).

**Chart.** axes: X = time inside the current MoT, from the deposit instant to Start + MoT (2026.10.01_07.00..00 → 2026.10.31_15.00..00 CST), ticked in CST stamps (MM.DD_HH), with the A.B..C position of NOW in the header (e.g. 14.3006..2136 · 3 h of 30.333 d) and the year's orbit A.B..C beside it (2675.2232..0614 on deposit day 271 · Q3 day 89); Y = dollars, 0 → the deposit amount ($3,604.49), cents exact; the slope IS the rate, so a right-hand rung labels the same axis in $/min so the eye reads 0.0825. · series: released — cumulative, monotonic, slope = amount ÷ MoT-minutes (the line a person watches rise every second) · escrowed — amount − released, the descending mirror, drawn as the remaining band · withdrawable — 0 until Start + 3 h, then equal to released (a step at the hold mark) · available — withdrawable − withdrawn: steps down at each withdrawal, never below 0 · rate $/min — a numeric tile, the sum over deposits still releasing (not a line) · net $/D swing (revision 0.002) — the budget's net per day over the 33/66/99 frame: sheet 3's 'Swings' and '144 → 138 → 137', outcome Cash On Hand · marks: ▲ deposit at 2026.10.01_07.00..00 with $3,604.49 and the memo (posted 09/29 · PENDING DIRECT DEPOSIT as provenance) · hold mark at Start + 3 h (2026.10.01_10.00..00) labelled 'withdrawable from', with an hh:mm:ss countdown before it (pod-clock hhmmss) · ▼ each withdrawal with amount and stamp; a refused one is not drawn but said in the person's words (HOLD · INSUFFICIENT · AMOUNT) · NOW cursor, ticking, carrying the A.B..C and the LTU beside it · end-of-MoT line labelled FULL 3600.3600..3600 · the Q3 → Q4 boundary the real paycheck crosses: day 274 opens 2026.10.03_11.15..00 CST · 33 / 66 / 99 frame boundaries and the perihelion when they fall inside the window (labelled with both the frame day and its 30.333-equivalent) · a second ▲ when deposits overlap (rate tile shows the sum) · states: pending — before Start: flat 0, escrow shows the full amount as arriving, memo visible → releasing · held — Start → +3 h: released rises, withdrawable 0, a HELD badge with the countdown → releasing · withdrawable — +3 h → end: withdrawable tracks released, available nets withdrawals → released — after end: flat at the amount, escrow 0, FULL badge, rate 0 unless another deposit is releasing → stacked — two deposits overlapping: rate = sum, both ▲ marks, both on the view → refused withdrawal — HOLD / INSUFFICIENT / AMOUNT in one sentence in the person's words, never a code → unsaved or tampered record — one sentence: this phone could not keep or verify the record, the figures shown are from memory; the operator's door only at ?diag=1 → example — an unsigned visitor sees the worked paycheck labelled EXAMPLE, never another person's record · cadence: Number tiles (released · withdrawable · available · A.B..C · $/min) tick at 1 Hz from a pure balanceAt(now); the path re-samples once per minute, because one $/min step is 8.25¢ and on a 220-px phone chart 1 px ≈ $16.38 ≈ 199 minutes, so the line cannot visibly move faster; the whole-MoT series is sampled hourly (728 points = one A per hour) from series(); redraw on every appended transaction; no clock read inside the pure modules — the surface passes now. · phone: 390 px wide, 16-px gutters, no horizontal scroll: header (title · v.000_r.001 · FY day and quarter · A.B..C now with its LTU), a four-tile row (Released · Withdrawable · Available · $/min), the chart ~220 px tall as strokes only on the black ground in the 13 Trinity colours (vector law), the deposit/withdrawal form below, the budget ladder collapsed ($/D default; $/min and $/s rungs unlocked by the first real deposit), touch targets ≥ 44 px, every string a fin.* lexicon key, and the R-CORE badge mounted LAST in normal flow reading the Financial-2525 ledger.

**Invariants.**
- Money released is never more than money deposited. — `test:financial-accrual (exists, green; add a random-t sweep so the clamp is proven at every instant, not one)`
- A withdrawal never makes my balance negative, and when it is refused it says why. — `test:financial-accrual (exists, green)`
- The rate is my amount ÷ my MoT-minutes and nothing else. — `test:financial-accrual + test:financial-mot (exist, green)`
- A transaction is three fields — $, deposit day and time, MoT — and it accrues from those three alone. — `test:financial-accrual (exists, green)`
- What is released I can see at once; I can take it out only three hours after the deposit. — `test:financial-accrual (exists, green: $14.85 at +3 h, held a minute earlier)`
- My MoT starts at 0.0000..0000, ends at 3600.3600..3600, and the three 'Equal' writings all read FULL. — `test:financial-mot (exists, green)`
- 07:00 typed in Austin is 07:00 CST all year — the clock never shifts under my money. — `test:financial-mot (exists, green on an October date)`
- Day 1 is the perihelion, day 364 closes Q4, day 365 is down, and no quarter is ever cut short. — `fin-year-fits (NEW, in test:financial-mot): for every consecutive pair in the table assert 364 ≤ gap or a DECLARED deficit — fails today on 2028 → 2029 = 363.24 d`
- There is one perihelion table in this repo and it agrees with itself to the minute. — `fin-one-perihelion-table (NEW): soi-calendar.ts PERIHELION_UTC vs lib/financial-2525/calendar.ts PERIHELION — 2024, 2026 and 2028 differ today`
- A recorded transaction is never edited; a correction is a new entry; a tampered record says so. — `test:financial-accrual (verify/brokenAt exists) + test:financial-surface (MISSING: the unsaved/tampered sentence is on the glass)`
- My record reopens on this phone to exactly what happened, at any revision. — `test:financial-accrual (replay exists) + test:financial-surface (MISSING: loadRecord on mount, saveRecord false is said)`
- Every A.B..C on my screen has its Earth time beside it; no number stands alone. — `test:financial-surface (MISSING)`
- The chart draws the pure series; the screen never computes money itself. — `test:financial-surface (MISSING: the component imports balanceAt/series and contains no ÷ 1440 or × 60 arithmetic)`
- No screen I can reach names a database, a migration, a storage quota or a vendor. — `test:financial-i18n (MISSING: reuse the BANNED regex of tests/sign-lexicon-voice.test.mjs over every fin.* key)`
- Every word I read is in my language — all 33. — `test:lexicon-coverage with fin.* staged in AFTER_FILL (in flight) + test:financial-i18n (MISSING: zero hardcoded English in the component)`
- The generated pages never disagree with the one source; the ladder only goes up; a release names its commit; the stamp reads v.000_r.001. — `test:financial-crs (MISSING) running scripts/financial-render.mjs --check and its assertSource (exists)`
- There is one 9,999 cap and it is the POD's. — `test:financial-crs (MISSING: no literal 9999 in lib/financial-2525 or the component except the YUG_CEILING import)`
- Every figure on the surface is mine or the sheet's, labelled by origin; nothing is invented. — `test:financial-crs (MISSING: every seeded number carries a label or source — budget.ts labels and example.note exist)`
- The badge at the bottom is R-CORE and it reads my ledger. — `test:rcore-revisions (exists for Drone; extend to FINANCIAL_LEDGER via frontend/scripts/gen-rcore-revisions.mjs and the component mounting RCoreBadge last)`
- The twelve-lens review of this revision is on the record before the next revision opens. — `test:financial-crs (MISSING: reviews[] has an entry for project.revision whose record file exists on disk — docs/financial-2525/reviews/r001_twelve_lenses.md does not today)`

**Risks.**
- The build is moving under review and nothing is committed: between two reads the routes, five npm scripts, 66 lexicon keys and the generated views appeared, all untracked (??) — the exact exposure of AAR 2026-08-28, where thirteen uncommitted files were destroyed; at last read test:ci names three test files and both routes import a component that do not exist, so test:ci and tsc are red until they land.
- FY 2028 → 2029 spans 363.24 days on the in-flight table: four 91-day quarters do not fit, the down day is −0.76 d and Q4 is silently cut; the existing gate exercises only 2026 → 2027 and passes — a time bomb two years out unless fin-year-fits lands now and the operator rules on 'Reference ↳ 365.25'.
- Two perihelion tables in one repo (soi-calendar.ts vs lib/financial-2525/calendar.ts) differ in 2024, 2026 and 2028; the domain JSON records only 2028 — defect 27's drift pattern, and a NO-REWORK breach.
- Two month lengths on one surface: a frame labelled 33D beside a paycheck at 30.333 will read as wrong to the operator unless both numbers are printed together; parseDurationDays turns 0000.01.00 into 33 d while a typed 30.333 stays 30.333 — a silent 2.67-day divergence if a duration string is ever used for pay.
- CST −6 vs the bank's CDT: the operator's 07:00 is one hour off his banking app's civil 07:00 in October; the first side-by-side comparison disagrees by an hour unless every stamp says CST standard.
- B and C digits change 5× and 18,000× per second in a 30.333-day MoT; a live readout of C is fake precision — sample at the 1 Hz tick or freeze C.
- Rounding: each deposit rounds independently, so available can flicker by 1¢ around a withdrawal when deposits overlap; the invariant holds but the sweep gate must prove it.
- Real money on a personal device with a 16-hex FNV chain is tamper-EVIDENT, not tamper-PROOF; the disclaimer says 'a record, never a bank' and the voice gate should ban bank-grade claims ('verified', 'secured') on the glass.
- Login isolation is declared honest (FD-07) but two people signing in on one phone are isolated only by key, and the cloud mirror is not per login — FIN-08 stays draft and the surface must say so once, in the person's words.
- 66 fin.* keys staged in AFTER_FILL satisfy the 33-language law only as a listed debt (×32 fills owed); the fill must never be reported as done.
- The domain JSON cites this twelve-lens review as PENDING at a record path that does not exist and assertSource does not check reviews[] — the review can be forgotten while the JSON claims it.
- The ◬ ♡ 웃 Session basis is declared in the ask, but the in-flight code touches pod-yug only for the cap: a deposit Start is a typed claim, not a witnessed event; pod-clock's law 'time claimed ≠ time clocked' applies to money too, so the record will need a witnessedBy (the bank posting) before the business frame.
- The business P&L ladder rendered in README is fine as declared data, but any business control on the surface before feedback breaks addendum 5.
- Ledger and revision text must stay free of model identifiers — the rcore-revisions gate redacts on render and should be extended to FINANCIAL_LEDGER.

**The three things revision 0.001 must do first.**
- PERSIST what is already built before anything else: commit the in-flight footprint as-is in its own commit (the domain JSON, the ledger, the five modules, the two green gates, scripts/financial-render.mjs, the generated views, both routes, the 66 fin.* keys, package.json) — then make test:ci honest in the next commit by landing tests/financial-crs.test.mjs, tests/financial-surface.test.mjs, tests/financial-i18n.test.mjs and components/financial-2525/command-ux1.tsx, or removing their names from test:ci until they exist; report SHA | committed | pushed | LIVE/UNVERIFIED with the site URL on every update.
- Close the two calendar holes with gates before drawing the chart: fin-one-perihelion-table (extend soi-calendar.ts PERIHELION_UTC to 2035 with provenance rows, import it from lib/financial-2525/calendar.ts, assert equality to the minute) and fin-year-fits (for every consecutive pair four 91-day quarters fit, else the deficit is DECLARED on the record — 2028 → 2029 = 363.24 d fails today); put 'reset at each perihelion vs a 365.25 reference year' to the operator as FD-10 and append it to the ledger.
- Build the phone chart from the pure series only, then its gates: components/financial-2525/command-ux1.tsx renders series()/balanceAt(now) for the worked paycheck before sign-in and the person's record after, every A.B..C with its LTU beside it, the +3 h hold, FULL and Q4 marks, strokes only, the R-CORE badge mounted last reading FINANCIAL_LEDGER via frontend/scripts/gen-rcore-revisions.mjs; tests/financial-surface.test.mjs (no money arithmetic in the component, the unsaved/tampered sentence present, the BANNED voice regex over fin.*), tests/financial-crs.test.mjs (financial-render --check, 13 parents, /crs matrix rows, reviews[] record on disk), tests/financial-i18n.test.mjs; persist this twelve-lens round at docs/financial-2525/reviews/r001_twelve_lenses.md and append ledger rev 3 before r.002 opens.

### Christo — consensus and user-flow validation: where the operator, first time on his phone, gets lost, and what one screen must say

**Readiness:** B-

**One sentence.** The notes are enough to build from and the pure law already runs green on his real paycheck, but revision 0.001 exists only as uncommitted files with no screen, two months sharing one letter, and a badge that claims a chart nobody can open — so persist it, settle M, and put one plain sentence above one chart on his phone before the twelve lenses see anything else.

**Ambiguities → declared default.**
- **Which "month" does a person see? The paycheck MoT is 30.333 days (91 ÷ 3); sheet 3 says "M = month = 33D" but its own options line writes "33D, 66D, 99D (30.3, 60.6, 90.9)" — 30.3 IS 91/3. The build (FD-04, LTU_DAYS.M = 33) keeps both, so perUnit($3,604.49 over 30.333 d, "M") prints $3,921.37 "per M" for a $3,604.49 monthly paycheck.** → One letter, one month: M = the pay MoT = 91/3 = 30.333 days (CALENDARS.soi91.monthDays already in lib/soi-calendar.ts). "33D · 66D · 99D" are NAMED FRAMES (the trinity names of 30.3 / 60.6 / 90.9 days), never a second month; the sheet's 33-day arithmetic stays recorded as the sheet's. Every $/mo a person reads = $/min × 43,680. — _The operator's own parentheses equate 33D with 30.3; 3 × 30.333 = 91 = Q closes the calendar; and a monthly paycheck that displays as $3,921 per month is the first place a first-time user stops trusting the screen._
- **"October 1, 2026 at 07:00" — 07:00 on his phone (Austin is on CDT that day, so 12:00Z) or 07:00 in the fixed CST frame he declared (13:00Z, which the build stores)?** → Typed times are read in the fixed CST frame (UTC−6, no daylight shift), so 07:00 → 13:00Z, and the screen prints both whenever they differ: "07:00 CST · 08:00 on this phone". — _He declared CST standard as the law; a release schedule must never jump an hour twice a year; but a phone that shows 08:00 beside a record that says 07:00 with no explanation reads as a bug._
- **Withdraw WHAT after 3 hours — only what escrow has released so far ($14.85 at 10:00), or early access to the whole $3,604.49 "if really in need of cash"?** → Only released money, net of prior withdrawals, and only from deposit + 3 h (FD-03 as built: HOLD then INSUFFICIENT). No advance against escrow. — _"escrow that releases $/min" is the whole mechanism; an advance is a loan and needs a rule the notes do not give. But the screen must SAY it, or he will see $14.85 against a $3,604.49 deposit and think the money is missing._
- **Is the 3-hour hold measured from the deposit instant once, or does each released minute wait 3 hours?** → Once, from the deposit day and time (FD-03). — _"3 hrs later individual can withdraw" names one event; a rolling hold would make Ready-to-withdraw permanently lag Released by $14.85, which no one could explain on a phone._
- **Which deposit date is the Start — the bank's posted 09/29 (pending) or his stated Oct 1 07:00?** → Start = what the person assigns (Oct 1 07:00 CST); the bank's posted date is a memo on the same transaction. — _"assign deposit date" is his verb; the record must show both so the two never look like two deposits._
- **Where does a DAY roll — at the perihelion clock-time each day (the build: 24 h periods from 17:15Z, so days change at 11:15 CST) or at 00:00 CST?** → Keep the build's rule for revision 0.001 (whole 24 h periods from the perihelion instant) but never print a SoI day number without its CST date beside it; put the question to the operator for 0.002. — _Both readings satisfy "day resets at perihelion"; only he can say whether his personal day changes at midnight. A person watching Q4 open at 11:15 in the morning with no date will be lost._
- **How long is a financial year — perihelion to perihelion (364.39 days in 2026, so day 365 barely exists) or a fixed 365-day grid?** → Perihelion to perihelion (as built): 4 × 91 = 364 grid days, then DOWN until the next perihelion — 0, 1 or 2 down days, never a drifted grid. — _"day resets everywhere at perihelion each year" outranks "day 365"; the grid must not drift off the anchor that names it._
- **Release curve — linear $/min, or front-loaded / arrears?** → Linear, amount ÷ (MoT × 1440) per minute, from the deposit instant (FD-05, spreadPerMin's own law). — _"releases $/min" and the sheet's 144 $/D = 0.1 $/m are one straight line; nothing in the notes bends it._
- **Is 30.333 the exact rational 91/3 (43,680 min) or the decimal typed (30.333 d = 43,679.52 min, a 29-second difference at the end of the period)?** → Store MoT as integer minutes (43,680) and display 30.333; never store the decimal or a rendered string as truth. — _The last cent must land on a definite minute; a period that ends 29 s early or late is a rounding dispute the person cannot see._
- **What is the login in the personal pilot — the app's Auth0 sign-in (FD-07/FIN-08) or a code like the pod's, with the record device-local?** → Sign-in on the route, record device-local under exel-fin:<owner>, cloud honestly "best effort"; no screen names Supabase, RLS or a migration. — _"each user gets their own log in" plus the signer-never-meets-the-machine law; the pilot has one user and his phone must work offline._
- **Do the sheet's budget lines (Income 3,200 · Home 700 …) load as his numbers?** → They are an editable template shown as "from your sheet", never merged into his balance; the only real figure on the surface is $3,604.49. — _Sheet income 3,200 ≠ real paycheck 3,604.49; blending them would show a net that is nobody's._
- **Does a paycheck mint 웃 (addendum 4's 9,999 cap)?** → No: revision 0.001 records dollars only; 웃 stays M × hours in lib/pod-yug.ts and only ever settles ONTO this record at a stamped rate (FIN-10 draft). — _unit.mintsettle: no wage enters the mint; dividing dollars by minimum wage would be a second mint and a second ceiling._
- **What is the stop rule for "until simulated sufficiently" (FIN-09)?** → The operator uses the personal surface for one full MoT (Oct 1 07:00 → Oct 31 15:00 CST) and reports; his feedback is the sufficiency gate, and only then is FIN-12 (business) opened. — _Addendum 5 makes feedback the trigger; a loop with no measurable stop runs forever._

**MoT law, as this lens read it.** A MoT is ONE WHOLE — the pay period — and a moment inside it is its ELAPSED FRACTION written in Base-3600, exactly the celestial UCRS-2525 convention (HU 0 = perihelion, HU 3600 = one full orbit = 3600.3600..3600). For the paycheck, MoT = 30.333 days = 91 ÷ 3 = 43,680 minutes = 2,620,800 seconds, and A = 3600ths of the period (one A = 728 s = 12 min 8 s), B = 3600ths of an A (0.2022 s), C = 3600ths of a B (56 µs). So "30.33 converts to 3600.3600..3600" means: the whole period is the ruler; the deposit instant (2026.10.01_07.00..00 CST = 13:00Z) reads 0.0000..0000; the 3-hour withdrawal gate at 10:00 is 10,800 ÷ 2,620,800 = 0.0041209 of the whole → 14.3006..2136; halfway (Oct 16 11:00 CST) reads 1800.0000..0000; completion (Oct 31 15:00 CST) reads 3600.3600..3600, which the sheet calls Equal to 3600.0000..0000 and 3599.3599..3599 because the last tick and the rollover name the same instant (24:00 = 00:00). Money rides the same ruler: released = $3,604.49 × fraction, so one A is worth $1.00125 and the whole is $3,604.49 — at 10:00 exactly $14.85. Earth LTU conversion is one multiplication: seconds = fraction × 2,620,800; minutes = fraction × 43,680; and the rate $3,604.49 ÷ 43,680 = $0.08252/min = $4.951/h = $118.83/D = $831.80/W(7) = $3,604.49 per pay-month (30.333 d) = $10,813.47 per 91-day Q = $0.001375/s. In the sheet's own duration spelling the length is 0000.00.30_08.00..00 (30 days 8 hours). Mars: the fraction never names a planet — only the period's local-second count changes (a 30-sol MoT = 30 × 88,775.244 s), so a quarter of it reads 900.0000..0000 on both worlds, and the gate already proves it. The one place this law breaks consensus today is the letter M: the sheet's 33-day analysis month multiplies the same $/D to $3,921.37 "per M" for a $3,604.49 monthly paycheck — two months may never share one letter on one screen.

**Chart.** axes: X = time, from the deposit instant (Oct 1 07:00 CST, 0.0000..0000) to deposit + MoT (Oct 31 15:00 CST, 3600.3600..3600); tick labels in CST civil dates on top, the A.B..C reading on ONE line beneath the plot, never as the only x label; the SoI day and quarter (day 271 · Q3) as a secondary strip. Y = dollars, 0 to the deposit ($3,604.49), no money above the deposit ever drawn. Default window on the phone = the whole current MoT with a 'now' cursor; a two-finger pinch steps the frame 33D → 66D → 99D (30.3 / 60.6 / 90.9 days) and a tap on 'now' zooms the last hour, where one point per B is visible. · series: In escrow — deposit minus released; a straight line falling from $3,604.49 to $0 (the payer's pot emptying) · Released to you — cumulative, rising straight at $0.0825/min ($118.83/day); the line the eye follows · Ready to withdraw — zero until 10:00, then tracking Released minus every withdrawal; a step down at each withdrawal · Withdrawn — cumulative steps, one per withdrawal transaction, drawn as the gap between Released and Ready · Budget burn (revision 0.002, not 0.001) — the fixed + variable $/D line from the budget lines, dashed, so Cash On Hand = Released − Withdrawn − burn is readable at a glance · marks: Deposit mark at T0: 'Oct 1 · 07:00 CST · $3,604.49 in escrow · 0.0000..0000' (with 'posted 09/29 · pending' as its memo) · Hold-lift mark at T0 + 3 h: 'Oct 1 · 10:00 · $14.85 ready · 14.3006..2136' — the first thing a first-time user asks about, so it is labelled before he asks · The 'now' cursor with the live counter beside it: 'Released so far $X.XX · +$0.0825 a minute' · Quarter line where Q3 becomes Q4 (SoI day 274, ≈ Oct 3 11:15 CST under the built day rule) — printed with its CST date, never as a bare day number · End mark at T0 + MoT: 'Oct 31 · 15:00 CST · all $3,604.49 released · 3600.3600..3600' · A withdrawal mark for every withdrawal (down-tick on Ready with its $ and CST time); a refused withdrawal (HOLD / INSUFFICIENT) is a one-sentence toast in the person's words, never drawn · When the frame contains it: the next perihelion line (2027-01-02 20:33 CST) labelled 'Day 1 · new year' · states: IN ESCROW — deposited, not yet released; the payer's money, greyed → RELEASED — released by the clock; yours, visible, counting every second → READY TO WITHDRAW — released AND past 10:00, net of withdrawals; the only number the withdraw button reads → WITHDRAWN — moved to your own account; each one a line on the record with its day and time · cadence: The Released counter recomputes once a second from the pure balanceAt(now) — never stored, so a reload shows the same cents. The plotted series is sampled once per A (728 s → exactly 3,600 points over any MoT, the Base-3600 grid itself), re-sampled per B only inside the last-hour zoom. The record is written only on events (a deposit, a withdrawal), never on a tick. The three state tiles update with the counter; the chart redraws at most once a minute. · phone: At 375 px, top to bottom, one column, no horizontal scroll: (1) the sentence — '$3,604.49 arrived Oct 1 at 07:00 CST (08:00 on this phone). It releases to you at $0.0825 a minute for 30 days 8 hours — finished Oct 31 at 15:00.' (2) the live number 'Released so far $14.85' ticking, with 'Ready to withdraw $14.85 — from 10:00' under it; before 10:00 it reads 'Ready to withdraw from 10:00 · $0.00 now'. (3) three tiles: In escrow · Released · Ready. (4) the chart, strokes only on the black ground in the 13 colours, one accent. (5) 'Log a deposit' — amount · day and time · length, prefilled with the worked example; 'Withdraw' — amount only, refused in one sentence. (6) the R-CORE badge v.000_r.001 at the bottom centre. The A.B..C reading is one tappable line under the chart; no screen names a ledger, a hash, a database, a migration or a vendor.

**Invariants.**
- Every minute of a deposit adds up to the deposit, to the cent — the clock releases the money, never a hand, and never more than was put in. — `tests/financial-accrual.test.mjs (exists, 28 pass) — add the conservation case: Σ per-minute increments over 43,680 min === 360,449 cents, last tick absorbs the remainder`
- Nothing is ready to withdraw before 10:00 on the deposit day, and never more than has been released minus what was already taken; a refused withdrawal says why in one sentence. — `tests/financial-accrual.test.mjs HOLD / INSUFFICIENT cases (exist) + financial-voice: the refusal strings pass the signer-voice lexicon gate`
- A transaction written is never rewritten; the record replays to any earlier revision exactly, and a changed cent breaks the chain where it was changed. — `tests/financial-accrual.test.mjs §4 (exists) + a device-half case: saveRecord false → the screen says the record is on this phone only, and never evicts another person's record`
- One M: what a person sees as 'per month' is his pay MoT (30.333 days); the 33-day frame is a named window, never a second month. — `tests/financial-mot.test.mjs — new assertion: the worked example prints $3,604.49 per month on the surface and $3,921.37 appears nowhere`
- One clock, two spellings that always agree: the CST stamp and the A.B..C reading name the same instant, and the A.B..C position converts to Earth minutes and back within one second. — `tests/financial-mot.test.mjs round-trip block (exists, 41 pass) + parseStampCST(fmtStampCST(t)) === t for a DST-season instant`
- 07:00 means 07:00 CST all year; a daylight change never moves a release, and the phone's own time is shown beside it when they differ. — `tests/financial-mot.test.mjs cstMs case (exists) + financial-surface: both times rendered when offsets differ`
- Day 1 is the perihelion in CST; 4 × 91 days fill the grid; whatever is left until the next perihelion is DOWN and belongs to no quarter; no minute belongs to two quarters. — `tests/financial-mot.test.mjs §4 (exists) + one-source case: PERIHELION[2026] equals lib/soi-calendar.ts PERIHELION_UTC[2026] or the difference is on the record (today 17:15Z vs 17:16Z is not)`
- The 9,999 웃 ceiling is read from the pod, never written a second time. — `tests/financial-crs.test.mjs (cited by FIN-10, does not exist yet): source grep — no literal 9999 in lib/financial-2525/ or components/financial-2525/; YUG_CEILING imported`
- The only real money on the surface is the operator's — $3,604.49 · Oct 1 07:00 CST · 30.333; every other figure is typed by the person or marked as the sheet's. — `tests/financial-crs.test.mjs: example block equals the ask file's fields and project.handoffSha256 equals the sidecar hash`
- A generated view never disagrees with the one editable source, and revision 0.001 is the last line of revisions[] — appended, never edited. — `node scripts/financial-render.mjs --check in test:ci (script exists; --check currently FAILS on 4 missing views; not wired)`

**Risks.**
- MOTION WITHOUT RECORD, again: revision 0.001 is 13 untracked + 3 modified files, uncommitted and unpushed, in the same kind of container that destroyed thirteen files on 2026-08-28. Every hour it stays that way is the AAR repeating.
- The JSON says FIN-07 (the chart) and FIN-11 (33 languages) are 'implemented' while components/financial-2525/command-ux1.tsx does not exist (both route pages import it — the build cannot compile) and 0 fin.* keys exist: the panel claims more than happened.
- Two months in one repo: LTU_DAYS.M = 33 (mot.ts) vs CALENDARS.soi91.monthDays = 91/3 (soi-calendar.ts); a monthly paycheck can render as $3,921.37 per month. This is the first trust break a first-time user will hit.
- Two perihelion tables: calendar.ts PERIHELION[2026] = 17:15Z, soi-calendar.ts PERIHELION_UTC[2026] = 17:16Z; the JSON records the 2028 and 2027 discrepancies but not this one. One fact, two sources, silent drift.
- 07:00 CST-fixed is 08:00 on the operator's phone on Oct 1 (CDT): without both times on screen the record looks one hour wrong from its first line.
- $14.85 ready at 10:00 against a $3,604.49 deposit will read as missing money unless the sentence explaining the release is the first thing on the screen.
- The 3-hour hold in the code is per deposit; a second deposit re-arms nothing, but a person reading 'ready from 10:00' on day 20 may be confused when a new deposit shows $0 ready for three hours — say it per deposit.
- Rounding: releasedAt uses Math.round(amount × fraction); nothing yet proves the per-minute increments sum to 360,449 cents, and the decimal 30.333 vs 91/3 moves the end of the period by 29 s.
- record.ts stores real payroll data in localStorage under a bare owner key: anyone holding the unlocked phone reads it; a full phone makes saveRecord return false and the screen must say so (the sign-store lesson) — and never evict another record to make room.
- Sample-per-minute charts over 43,680 points on a phone will drain the battery; sample per A (3,600 points) as the chart spec says.
- FIN-08's cloud half is a bearer-key model, not identity; if it is ever labelled 'your account' the signer meets the machine.
- Scope creep into the business frame (4-4-5, P&L ladder) before the operator has used the personal surface for one full MoT — addendum 5 forbids it, and FIN-12 is rightly draft.
- The twelve-lens review record docs/financial-2525/reviews/r001_twelve_lenses.md is cited by the JSON and does not exist; this review is part of that record and must be persisted beside the revision, not left in a transcript.

### Enki — diversity and edge-case discovery: the leap fraction of a perihelion year, the reset instant in CST, the daylight hour, a deposit before it clears, a withdrawal before 3 h, a backdated deposit, a second deposit overlapping the first, the 31-day gap and the February overlap, an impossible stamp, and a 3-hour mark on a 30-day phone axis.

**Readiness:** B

**One sentence.** The notes were enough to build revision 0.001 and it has been built and gated green (235 cases), but every odd case this lens probed — a perihelion year that runs 363 to 368 days so the leap year 2028 has no down day, a fixed CST an hour off the bank in October, monthly paychecks that gap 16 h and overlap 2.3 days on a 30.333-day MoT, a hold a backdated entry lifts at once, a February 31 that rolls to March 3, and a 3-hour mark 1.4 px wide on a phone — is answered today by a declared default the operator has not yet seen, and the whole of it is still uncommitted.
