**Ambiguities → declared default.**
- **How long is a Financial-2525 year: 365 fixed days from perihelion, or perihelion to perihelion? The sourced table gives 363.24 d (2028), 363.35 (2031), 363.84 (2034), 364.16 (2025), 364.39 (2026), 365.67 (2029), 366.44 (2030), 367.28 (2032), 367.41 (2027), 367.54 (2024). Under the built law (4 × 91 from the instant, down from day 365 until the next perihelion) three years have NO down day and a Q4 whose day 91 lasts 4–18 h, and three years have ~82 h of down days (365–368).** → Perihelion to perihelion, as built (calendar.ts positionInYear). 'Resets everywhere at perihelion each year' is the stronger word; 'day 365' is the ordinal NAME of the down span, not a promise of 24 h. The surface prints the down span's real length (e.g. '9 h 18 m' for 2026, 'none — Q4 ends 5 h 45 m early' for 2028) and the gate walks all twelve years, not only 2026. — _The two operator sentences cannot both be exact; choosing the instant keeps the year a true orbit (the celestial A.B..C the surface already shows) and makes the irregularity visible instead of hiding it in a fixed count._
- **Does a financial DAY roll at the perihelion's clock time (2026.01.03_11.15..00 CST) or at CST midnight?** → Whole 24-h periods from the instant, as built (calendar.ts line 61 'CST-independent'); the civil CST stamp is printed beside the orbit day so both are visible. — _The reset is an instant, not a date; snapping to midnight would make day 1 begin 11 h 15 m before perihelion in 2026 and 20 h 33 m before it in 2027 (perihelion 2027 = 2027.01.02_20.33..00 CST — a January 2 reset in Austin)._
- **Is A.B..C for a MoT a POSITION (0..3600 of one whole, the celestial HU grammar) or a QUANTITY (N.mmmm..ssss whole units, the tokenomics grammar in abc-3600.ts)? As a quantity, 30.333 days formats to 30.1198..2880 and 728 h to 728.0000..0000 — neither is 3600.3600..3600.** → Position, as built (FD-01): the whole reads 3600.3600..3600 at completion. The quantity grammar stays for 웃 and money and the two never share a line. — _The operator wrote '30.33 converted to 3600.3600..3600' and named drone and planetary positioning as the model; only the positional reading produces his number._
- **'Austin Texas as CST standard' — fixed UTC−6 all year, or Austin's local clock (CDT, UTC−5, from March to November)? The pilot deposit 'October 1, 2026 at 07:00' is a summer-time wall clock at the bank: 12:00Z on the bank, 13:00Z on the record.** → Fixed UTC−6, as built (FD-02, CST_OFFSET_MIN = −360); the entry field is labelled 'CST standard' and the surface says once that a summer bank time is one hour earlier on this record. — _He said 'standard'; and perihelion is always in January, so the anchor is exact under either rule — only Mar–Nov deposits carry the hour, and a stated one-hour convention is safer than a silent DST table._
- **Which instant is the deposit: the bank's posted date (09/29/2026, Pending) or the operator's typed 'October 1, 2026 at 07:00'?** → The typed day and time (the three-field law); the posted date lives in the memo as provenance — as built (example.postedDate vs example.depositStamp). — _Accrual must read from the three fields alone (FIN-03); the bank's pending date is a fact about the bank, not about when the money is his._
- **Is the 3-hour hold measured once per deposit (from its instant) or after each minute's release?** → Once per deposit, from its instant — as built (FD-03). — _'3 hrs later individual can withdraw' names one event; a rolling hold would make the withdrawable line lag the released line by 3 h for ever, which no sheet draws._
- **Is the hold measured from the CLAIMED instant (tx.atMs, what the person typed) or the RECORDED instant (entry.at, when the platform wrote it)? Probe: a deposit typed with a time 4 h in the past is withdrawable the moment it is recorded.** → From the claimed instant (his words), but the entry keeps both numbers and the surface prints the recorded instant whenever it differs from the claim by more than the hold — never blended, the pod-clock's own rule ('time clocked, not time claimed'). — _The pilot is the operator's own money and a record, not a bank; the honest fix is visibility, not a second hold. A witnessed rail is FIN-04's stretch._
- **What MoT does a monthly paycheck carry: 30.333 (91 ÷ 3), the sheet's 33, or the calendar span to the next paycheck (28–31 days)? Probe: Oct 1 07:00 + 30.333 d ends Oct 31 15:00 CST, so the Nov 1 07:00 deposit leaves a 16-hour gap at $0/min; Feb 1 + 30.333 d ends Mar 3 15:00, so the Mar 1 deposit overlaps 2.3 days and the live rate doubles to 16.50 ¢/min.** → 30.333 per deposit as typed (FD-04); overlaps sum, gaps read zero, and the chart draws both — never auto-stretch a MoT to the next deposit. — _A MoT that depends on a future deposit breaks 'accrues from its three fields alone'; the gap and the doubling are true statements about a 30.333-day law applied to a Gregorian payroll, and the person should see them._
- **Business escrow: 'two weeks' (14 days) in the addendum or the sheet's '2×/mth 0000.00.16_12.00..00 | 16.5 days' (33 ÷ 2)?** → Test later (addendum 5); both figures declared as data; the payer types the MoT. — _Nothing in the personal pilot depends on it, and the operator has ordered the sequence._
- **How is a deposit that the bank reverses (a pending direct deposit that never clears) recorded on an append-only record with only 'deposit' and 'withdrawal' kinds?** → Not in 0.001: a deposit is recorded when the person is satisfied it cleared; a 'reversal' kind that names the id it reverses is a 0.002 item; until then the surface refuses to record a reversal rather than editing a row. — _A withdrawal cannot stand in for it (the hold refuses it and it would be the wrong word), and editing is forbidden by the record's own invariant._
- **In a pilot with no escrow rail, what does 'withdrawable' mean?** → A budgeting number — 'yours to spend now' — that moves no money; the disclaimer says so (project.disclaimer). — _The pilot models the paycheck as if an escrow released it; the number is the law's output, not a bank balance._
- **Is the sheet's 'Swings' chart ($/D stepping 144 → 138 → 137 across the range, each decision a step) part of 'the real-time chart' the operator asked for?** → Yes, as a second strip in 0.002; 0.001 draws the three series and the marks. — _It is the only chart the operator drew by hand; FIN-07 lists the series but not the step._
- **Three months share the letter M: 30.333 (pay MoT), 33 (personal analysis month), 30.44 (Innovation's CADENCE_UNIT on the Gregorian calendar).** → On this surface the day count is always printed beside the letter (M33, Q99, MoT 30.333); a bare 'M' never appears. — _The sheet's own 4,752 $/M33 vs 4,762 slip shows how easily a month drifts by a day._
- **Which figures govern where the sheet's arithmetic slips (47,620 vs 47,520 min; 4,762 vs 4,752 $/M33)?** → The arithmetic (33 × 1440 = 47,520; 0.1 × 47,520 = 4,752), with the slip recorded beside it — as built (budget.sheetSlips). — _The quarter figure 14,256 = 3 × 4,752 shows the operator's own intent was the corrected month._

**MoT law, as this lens read it.** A MoT is ONE WHOLE (a pay period, a budget month, a financial year) and A.B..C is a POSITION inside it on the celestial UCRS-2525 convention (HU 0 = perihelion, 3600 = the full orbit), not a count of days. For the pay period 30.333 days = 91 ÷ 3 = 728 h = 43,680 min = 2,620,800 s: A = one 3600th of the MoT = 728 s (12 min 8 s), running 0..3600; B = one 3600th of an A = 0.2022 s; C = one 3600th of a B = 56.17 µs. The start reads 0.0000..0000 and completion reads 3600.3600..3600 — 'MoT = 30.33 converted to 3600.3600..3600' means the whole, whatever its local length, is written FULL at its end; the sheet's three Equal writings (3600.0000..0000 · 3600.3600..3600 · 3599.3599..3599) all mean FULL (mot.ts isFull). Concrete positions in this MoT (probed): +3 h = 14.3006..2136 (the hold), +1 day = 118.2452..2690, +1 week = 830.2769..0831, half = 1800.0000..0000, one second before the end = 3599.3595..0198. To Earth LTU: seconds = (A + B/3600 + C/3600²) ÷ 3600 × 2,620,800; then ÷60 = min, ÷3600 = h, ÷86,400 = D, and the sheet's frame D1 · W7 · M33 · Q99 · Y365 (mot.ts ltuOfMotABC, LTU_DAYS); back again: f = elapsed ÷ 2,620,800, A = ⌊3600 f⌋, B = ⌊3600 · frac⌋, C = round(3600 · frac) — round-trips within 1 s (gated). Money rides the same fraction: $3,604.49 ÷ 43,680 min = $0.08252/min = 8.252 ¢/min = $0.001375/s = $4.95/h = $118.83/D; at the hold (A = 14.3006) $14.85 is released and withdrawable. The other grammar in abc-3600.ts (A = whole units) would write 30.333 days as 30.1198..2880 and 728 h as 728.0000..0000 — not the operator's number — so it is kept for 웃 and money only. Mars: a 30-sol MoT (2,663,257 s) reads the same 0 → 3600.3600..3600; only the LTU multiplier changes (FIN-13, gated). The year is also a MoT: 0.0000..0000 at 2026.01.03_11.15..00 CST, FULL at the next perihelion — and its whole is not 365 days but 363.24 to 367.54 depending on the year (the leap fraction this lens exists for); the calendar day is a 24-h step from that instant, and the stamp is fixed CST (UTC−6): 2026.10.01_07.00..00 = 13:00Z.

**Chart.** axes: x = the focus deposit's MoT position in A (ticks 0 · 900 · 1800 · 2700 · 3600) with the CST stamps of start and end printed under 0 and 3600 and an LTU row above (+3 h · day 1 · day 7 · day 15 · day 30.333) so a person reads both grammars at once; y = dollars from $0 to the deposit amount, four gridlines at 25/50/75/100 % LABELLED IN $ (the built chart has none). Phone-first defect found: on a 30.333-day axis at 390 px the 3-hour hold sits 1.4 px from the origin and the first day is 11 px — the operator's hold is invisible; add a window toggle 3 h · 1 D · 7 D · MoT (default 1 D while the deposit is younger than a day, else MoT) rather than a second chart. · series: escrowed — hairline, falling from the amount to $0 over the MoT (intelligence colour) · released — accent stroke, rising 0 → amount, linear at $/min, the one line that is always monotonic · withdrawable — consciousness colour, $0 until the hold then equal to released · available = withdrawable − withdrawn — the ONE number a person acts on; it is computed (accrual.ts Balance.availableCents) but NOT drawn: draw it · live $/min · $/h · $/D · $/s — numbers, not a line (balance panel, built) · the sheet's Swings — $/D as a step function across the range, each transaction a step (144 → 138 → 137); missing from FIN-07, 0.002 · on overlap, the second deposit's released line in the same window; on a gap, a flat $0 rate segment after the MoT end until the next deposit · marks: deposit instant at x = 0 (stamp printed) · the 3-hour hold — built as a 10-px bottom tick; make it a full-height dashed hairline labelled '3 h' so the withdrawable line's start is legible at every window · NOW — blank hairline, moves every second (built) · every withdrawal — top tick at its instant WITH its amount (built without the amount) · MoT end at 3600 with the end stamp; when the next deposit starts inside this window, its start as a second deposit mark (overlap); when it starts after, the gap length in hours · the perihelion when it falls inside the window (2027.01.02_20.33..00 CST) — the year resets mid-MoT · the recorded instant when it differs from the claimed instant by more than the hold (claim vs record) · states: PENDING — before the deposit instant: everything in escrow, lines flat at $0, header 'pending from <stamp>' (built) → RELEASING · HELD — 0 to 3 h: released > 0, withdrawable = 0, 'visible, not yet yours'; a withdrawal is refused by name HOLD (built) → RELEASING — 3 h to the end: withdrawable = released; available = withdrawable − withdrawn; a withdrawal above available refused INSUFFICIENT (built) → RELEASED — fraction = 1, escrow $0, position 3600.3600..3600, money still withdrawable (built) → GAP — no deposit releasing: rate $0/min, flat line, 'next deposit expected' (probe: 16 h after every 31-day month on a 30.333 MoT) — NOT surfaced → OVERLAP — two deposits releasing: rate = sum (probe: 16.50 ¢/min for 2.3 days after February) — computed, not drawn → BEFORE ANY DEPOSIT — a withdrawal is refused INSUFFICIENT (right verdict, wrong word: nothing is deposited yet) — name it → TAMPERED — chain broken: the record reads empty and says so (built: fin.chain_broken); the chart draws nothing rather than a wrong number → UNSAVED — the device refused the write: the entry stays in memory and the banner says so (built: fin.save_failed) · cadence: One 1 Hz clock feeds `now` (built, gated). At $0.0825/min a cent lands every 7.27 s, so the balance in cents looks frozen 6 seconds in 7; the visibly moving element must be the A.B..C readout (B advances 4.95 per second — built in the chart header) and the $/s figure to 4 decimals (built). Resample the series at every appended transaction and once per minute, not per second; and sample at the EVENT instants (deposit, +3 h, each withdrawal, MoT end) as well as the 120-point grid — today's uniform step is 6.07 h, so the withdrawable line rises at +6.07 h on screen instead of +3 h. NOW and the header tick every second. · phone: viewBox 360 × 150, width 100 %, strokes only on the black ground in the 13 colours (built, gated by the vector-law test). At 390 px: panel 366 px wide, one A-tick of 900 = 85 px, hold tick 1.4 px from the origin (invisible), first day 11 px — hence the window toggle above. Legend wraps under the chart (built). The year panel prints day · quarter · n/91 and 'down day' (built) — add the down span's real length. Touch targets: the four window buttons at least 34 px high (the input style already uses minHeight 34). Nothing on the glass names a database, a migration or a vendor (the signer law) — the built copy passes (fin.device_only says 'this device').

**Invariants.**
- Each transaction gets amount, deposit day and time, and MoT length of time — a deposit missing any of the three is refused before it is written, and it accrues from those three fields alone. — `financial-accrual (built) + financial-record-shape (new: append() refuses a deposit without amount > 0, a parseable stamp and motDays ≥ 0)`
- Escrow releases $/min: what is released is amount × elapsed ÷ MoT, never more than what was deposited, and never goes down. — `financial-accrual (built: released clamps at the amount, monotonic over the series)`
- Three hours after the deposit the individual can withdraw — before that the released money is visible but not movable, and a withdrawal is refused by the name HOLD. — `financial-accrual (built: +3 h − 1 min held, +3 h withdrawable, HOLD refusal)`
- One can deposit and withdraw as needed but never more than is theirs — the available balance is never negative and a second withdrawal cannot take what the first left. — `financial-accrual (built: INSUFFICIENT at $14.86 against $14.85; the second withdrawal refused)`
- Day resets everywhere at perihelion each year — at every sourced perihelion 2024–2035 the year reads day 1 · Q1 · 0.0000..0000, including 2028 (a leap year with NO down day and a Q4 that ends 5 h 45 m early) and 2027 (82 h of down days). — `financial-calendar-all-years (new: extend financial-mot § 4 to walk all twelve years and assert the down span's real length; today it asserts 2026 only)`
- 91 days for a quarter with down day 365 — day 364 always closes Q4, day 365 onward belongs to no quarter, and the down span is shown at its true length, never asserted as 24 h. — `financial-calendar-all-years (new, same gate) + financial-surface (built: fin.down_day; extend to print the span)`
- All is defaulted to A.B..C: a MoT starts at 0.0000..0000, completes at 3600.3600..3600, the three Equal writings all read FULL, and every position converts to Earth LTU and back within one second — on Earth and on Mars. — `financial-mot (built: 41 cases including the 30-sol MoT)`
- Austin Texas as CST standard — every stamp is UTC−6 with no daylight shift (07:00 on 2026-10-01 is 13:00Z), and a stamp is accepted only if it prints back as itself (2026.02.31 is refused, not rolled to March 3). — `financial-mot (built for the offset) + financial-stamp-roundtrip (new: fmtStampCST(parseStampCST(s)) === s or null)`
- From the get go we have revision control using the same framework as Drone-2525 — one editable source, rendered views fresh, revisions strictly increasing, a release citing a real commit, project.revision the last entry, thirteen parents one per Vision section, the handoff hashed. — `financial-crs (built: 130 cases, runs financial-render --check)`
- Each user gets their own login — an unsigned visitor sees only the worked example and a sign-in, never another person's record; the record is keyed to the signed-in person. — `financial-surface (built, source-level) — a live probe on the exported route is FIN-08's DTM, still draft`
- The 9,999 HI token cap is the POD's YUG_CEILING and nothing else — no second ceiling on this surface. — `financial-crs (built: yugCeiling 9999 and YUG_CEILING present in lib/pod-yug.ts)`
- Once the personal framework receives feedback we test business — no business route, P&L code or 4-4-5 surface exists before a feedback record does. — `financial-crs (built: business.status starts TEST LATER, tenant personal) + financial-business-gate (new: no EBITDA/COGS/4-4-5 identifiers in components/financial-2525 until reviews[] carries a feedback record)`
- A record is a record — the claimed instant (what was typed) and the recorded instant (when the platform wrote it) are both on every entry, never blended, and the surface says when they differ by more than the hold. — `financial-claim-vs-record (new; the entry already carries both `at` and `tx.atMs`)`
- A finished entry survives a full phone — when the device refuses the write the entry stays in memory, the person is told, and the oldest OTHER record is evicted before giving up, the way the pod store does. — `financial-surface (built: fin.save_failed) + financial-record-eviction (new: reuse pod-store's evict-oldest loop in saveRecord)`

**Risks.**
- The entire revision-0.001 surface (23 paths: five libraries, the domain JSON, the render script, the ledger, the chart component, five gates, the lexicon and launcher edits) is UNCOMMITTED in the working tree while the ask is committed — the exact failure of AAR 2026-08-28 (thirteen uncommitted files destroyed); 235 green tests exist only on this container until the commit lands.
- The perihelion year is not 365 days: 363.24–367.54 d across the sourced table. 2028 (a leap year), 2031 and 2034 have NO down day and a Q4 whose day 91 lasts 4–18 h; 2024, 2027 and 2032 have days 365–368 down (~82 h). The gate asserts the down day only for 2026; the year panel will show 'Q4 · 91/91' at the reset in 2028 with no explanation.
- Two perihelion tables in the code (soi-calendar.ts seed of 5 vs calendar.ts sourced 12) disagree on 2028 (12:11 vs 12:28 UTC); the discrepancy is recorded in the JSON but the code has two sources of truth — a NO-REWORK breach.
- Fixed CST vs the bank's CDT: every deposit copied from a bank screen between March and November is one hour later on the record than on the bank; the pilot's own Oct 1 07:00 is one (12:00Z at the bank, 13:00Z on the record). Declared, but not yet said on the glass.
- Monthly payroll on a 30.333-day MoT: a 16-hour $0/min gap after every 31-day month and a 2.3-day doubled rate (16.50 ¢/min) after February — the person sees their $/min halve, vanish and double at month ends; the balance panel shows the number, the chart shows nothing.
- The hold runs from the CLAIMED instant: a deposit typed with a time 4 h in the past is withdrawable at once; the recorded instant is on the entry but never compared.
- parseStampCST accepts 2026.02.31_07.00..00 and silently rolls it to 2026.03.03 — an impossible date becomes a real deposit instant.
- Phone-first chart: on the 30.333-day axis the 3-hour hold is 1.4 px from the origin and the first day 11 px; the series is sampled every 6.07 h so the withdrawable line rises at +6 h instead of +3 h; `available` — the one actionable number — is not drawn; y has no $ labels.
- The refusal before any deposit exists is INSUFFICIENT — correct verdict, wrong word; a person who has typed nothing is told they have insufficient funds.
- No reversal kind: a pending direct deposit the bank reverses cannot be recorded on the append-only record except as a withdrawal, which the hold refuses; the only path is a new deposit of the wrong sign, which the type forbids.
- The sheet's Swings chart ($/D step 144 → 138 → 137) — the operator's own drawing — is not in FIN-07.
- Three months share the letter M (30.333 pay, 33 personal, 30.44 Innovation); the sheet's own 4,762/4,752 slip shows the cost of a bare M.
- saveRecord has no eviction on a full device where pod-store has an eight-try evict-oldest loop — the 'phone storage full' member of the AAR 2026-09-09 class.
- The record is per device only (FD-07, declared): a second phone shows an empty record with the same login — honest, but the operator will test on his phone and his desktop.
- docs/financial-2525/reviews/r001_twelve_lenses.md is named by reviews[] and does not exist; FIN-09's metric ('a review record exists for the revision') is unmet until this round is persisted.
- The worked example runs live for every unsigned visitor: the operator's real paycheck ($3,604.49, PAYROLL memo) is on a public route before sign-in — a record he chose, but worth his explicit confirmation before the push.

**The three things revision 0.001 must do first.**
- Commit the revision-0.001 surface ALONE — the 23 untracked/modified paths — after `npm run test:ci` (the five financial gates are already wired), push, and report `SHA | committed | pushed | cloudflare LIVE/UNVERIFIED` with the URL; nothing else touches it first (PERSIST FIRST). Then persist this twelve-lens round as docs/financial-2525/reviews/r001_twelve_lenses.md so FIN-09's metric holds, and append revision 0.002 as a `decision` — never edit 0.001.

### Enlil — implementation and build verification: the exact files, functions and gates revision 0.001 needs, mirroring Drone-2525's framework (one editable source → rendered CRS → append-only ledger → R-CORE badge → gates in test:ci).

**Readiness:** B

**One sentence.** Revision 0.001 is not a plan to build from but a build to commit — 1,764 lines across 14 new paths and 10 touched files, wired into test:ci and green (454 checks, four rendered views match their source, tsc clean) and every one of them untracked, the state the PERSIST FIRST law names as the failure — so the first act is a commit and an appended release, then the FY2028 short-year and the duplicated perihelion table, before the phone.

**Ambiguities → declared default.**
- **Which instant starts the escrow clock for the worked example: the bank's posted date 09/29/2026 or the operator's "October 1, 2026 at 07:00"?** → 2026.10.01_07.00..00 CST is the deposit day and time (as built: example.depositStamp); 09/29 is the bank's pending post, carried as data/memo, never as the accrual start. — _The operator spoke the deposit stamp together with the MoT in one line; a bank's "pending" post is not the payer's deposit into escrow._
- **"MoT=30.33 converted to 3600.3600..3600" — which A.B..C convention: abc-3600's tokenomics one (A = a whole hour, B = seconds) or the celestial one (A = 3600ths of the whole)?** → The celestial fraction-of-one-whole reading (FD-01 as built): the MoT is the unit; A = 3600ths of it, B = 3600ths of an A, C = 3600ths of a B; completion reads FULL. abc-3600's toABC/fmtABC are reused only for the digits. — _The operator said "from Celestial-2525 scale", and only that reading maps 30.33 days onto 3600.3600..3600 — under the hour convention 30.333 days is 728.0000..0000 and 3600.3600..3600 would be about 150 days._
- **The sheet's "3600.0000..0000 · 3600.3600..3600 · 3599.3599..3599 } Equal" — numerically these are 3600, ≈3601.0003 and ≈3599.9997; equal in what sense?** → All three READ FULL (isFull: a ≥ 3600, or a = b = c = 3599); the canonical print is 3600.3600..3600, ucrs-2525's FULL_ORBIT. — _The sheet marks them Equal as writings of the whole, the way 0.999… and 1 are one number; the gate already asserts this and nothing on the record contradicts it._
- **Is the 3-hour hold counted once from the deposit day and time, or 3 hours after each minute's release (a rolling hold)?** → Once, from the deposit instant (FD-03): before +3 h released money is visible but not movable; from +3 h everything released is withdrawable. — _"3 hrs later individual can withdraw" names one event after the deposit; a rolling hold would make the first withdrawable cent appear 3 h after the deposit anyway and every later one late for no stated reason._
- **Does the paycheck release forward from the deposit (advance) or does it cover the 30.333 days already worked (arrears)?** → Forward and linear from the deposit instant at amount ÷ (MoT × 1440) per minute (FD-05); arrears is a memo, not a rule. — _"releases $/min into account that one can see" describes money becoming available over the coming period; that is the mechanism the chart must draw._
- **Which month: the pay MoT 30.333 (91 ÷ 3), the sheet's M = 33 days, or sheet 1's "1×/mth 33 days / 2×/mth 16.5 days"?** → Each deposit carries its own MoT as typed (30.333 for the paycheck); the personal ANALYSIS month is 33 days (M33, Q99); 33 and 16.5 are the two pay-cadence presets for the MoT field. — _Both numbers are verbatim and serve different jobs — the record's third field is per transaction, the analysis ladder is the sheet's frame — so neither may overwrite the other._
- **"CST standard" — fixed UTC−6 all year, while Austin is on daylight time until 2026-11-01: is 07:00 on Oct 1 13:00 UTC (fixed) or 12:00 UTC (phone-local)?** → Fixed UTC−6, no daylight shift (FD-02): 2026.10.01_07.00..00 = 13:00 UTC; every stamp on the surface is labelled CST and the input placeholder shows the current CST stamp. — _"as CST standard" is the operator's explicit fixed rule; the cost is that a person typing phone time is an hour late until November, so the label must be visible, not assumed._
- **When perihelion-to-perihelion is not 365 days — FY2026 = 364.387 d, FY2027 = 367.413 d, FY2028 = 363.240 d — what happens to "4 × 91 + down day 365"?** → Perihelion wins: everything past day 364 until the next perihelion is down (0.39 d in FY2026, 3.41 d in FY2027); when the next perihelion arrives before day 364 (FY2028, Q4 short by 0.76 d) Q4 closes at the reset and the surface prints the short quarter. Gate FY2027 and FY2028 explicitly — today only FY2026 is tested. — _"day resets everywhere at perihelion" is the stronger, unconditional word; the 91-day grid is a convention laid on the orbit and must yield where the orbit is shorter — silently pretending day 364 exists in FY2028 would be a wrong date on a person's money._
- **How is a financial year named when its perihelion falls on Jan 2 in Austin (2027: 2027.01.02_20.33..00 CST)?** → By the UTC calendar year of its perihelion instant (positionInYear as built), so day 1 of FY2027 begins the evening of Jan 2 CST and the surface says so. — _Deterministic and identical to the soi-calendar table's keys; any CST-based naming would fork the year id from the celestial one._
- **Where does a person's record live and what isolates it per login in 0.001?** → Device-only, keyed to the Auth0 user.sub (exel-fin:<sub>), declared on screen as device_only; no cloud half in 0.001 (FD-07). — _The app's Supabase isolation is a per-browser bearer key, not identity (migration 030); declaring cloud per-login isolation now would be the fake the signer-never-meets-the-machine law forbids._
- **What, if anything, mints 웃 from money under the "9999 HI TOKEN CAP"?** → Nothing in 0.001: the cap is referenced from lib/pod-yug.ts YUG_CEILING (FIN-10 draft, gated as the only constant); no money→웃 rule exists until the operator states one. — _Addendum 4 names the cap, not a rule; inventing a conversion would be exactly the "STOP MAKING UP MATH" failure on the record._
- **Sheet-1 lines with no safe reading: "LSU 3600 – 3600.3600 · 3", "◬ = ATX = AI", "$3/Day = $99/M∞(33)", "Unlock 🔒 $/min System · Baseline: $/Day", "Idea + | • · – | •• · ∞ | •••", "Analysis: Backward · MoT ↔ MoT".** → Recorded verbatim in the ask; not built; raised to the operator as an open question list in revision 0.002. — _The photographs never reached the container and the transcription is from the message; a guess here would become law on a person's money._
- **Rounding: released cents are Math.round(amount × fraction) and the $/sec rung is sub-cent (0.1375 ¢/s) — floor, round, or fractional cents?** → Integer cents on every balance (round-half), 4-decimal dollars on rates (as built). — _Round-half can show a cent up to half a cent early but never a whole cent; the clamp f ≤ 1 keeps released ≤ deposited regardless._
- **What does "real-time chart" mean in ticks?** → One 1 Hz clock on the surface (null until mounted), every number recomputed from the pure law each tick. — _The $/sec rung is the finest visible motion; faster ticks cost a phone battery for no legible change, slower would hide the seconds the sheet asks for._
- **Is any business-frame element (4-4-5, P&L ladder) shown on the personal surface?** → No — declared as data in the domain JSON only, no business UI until the personal framework has received feedback. — _Addendum 5, the one user-voiced line of this task, orders the sequence._

**MoT law, as this lens read it.** Through the convention the operator named — the celestial UCRS-2525 orbit, where HU 0 is perihelion and 3600.3600..3600 is one full orbit — a MoT is ONE WHOLE and a moment inside it is its elapsed fraction in nested Base-3600: A = 3600ths of the MoT, B = 3600ths of an A, C = 3600ths of a B (abc-3600's nesting 1 A = 3600 B, 1 B = 3600 C, with A itself bounded at 3600 like HU). For the pay period 30.333 days (= 91 ÷ 3 = 2,620,800 s): one A = 728 s (12 min 8 s), one B = 0.2022 s, one C = 56.2 µs. Readings, computed with lib/financial-2525/mot.ts: deposit 2026.10.01_07.00..00 CST → 0.0000..0000; +3 h hold → 14.3006..2136; +1 day → 118.2452..2690; +7 d → 830.2769..0831; halfway → 1800.0000..0000; +30 d → 3560.1582..1503; completion 30.333 d later (2026.10.31_15.00..00 CST) → 3600.3600..3600. So \"30.33 converts to 3600.3600..3600\" means: the period is the unit, and at its completion the reading is FULL, exactly as a planet back at perihelion reads FULL_ORBIT; the sheet's three writings 3600.0000..0000 · 3600.3600..3600 · 3599.3599..3599 are Equal in that sense (all print FULL). Earth LTU: seconds = (A + B/3600 + C/3600²) ÷ 3600 × 2,620,800; ÷ 60 → minutes, ÷ 3600 → hours, ÷ 86,400 → D; then W = 7 D, M = 33 D, Q = 99 D, Y = 365 D (the sheet's units; the pay MoT itself is 30.333 D); the round trip holds within 1 s and is gated. Money rides the same fraction: released = $3,604.49 × fraction, i.e. 8.252 ¢/min = $118.83/D = $4.9512/h = $0.001375/s, $14.85 at the +3 h mark, $3,604.49 at FULL. Mars: a 30-sol MoT (30 × 88,775.244 s) reads the same digits at the same fraction (a quarter = 900.0000..0000); only the LTU seconds differ, so the ledger never names a planet. The other shape on the sheets — YYYY.MM.DD_HH.MM..SS for stamps and 0000.00.DD_HH.MM..SS for durations (0000.00.33_00.00..00 = 33 d, 0000.00.16_12.00..00 = 16.5 d) — is the Earth-local writing, always in CST standard (UTC−6, fixed). The financial YEAR is itself a MoT: perihelion → next perihelion, 0.0000..0000 at 2026.01.03_11.15..00 CST and ~FULL the minute before 2027.01.02_20.33..00 CST.

**Chart.** axes: x = the focused deposit's MoT in A.B..C, 0 → 3600 left to right, ticks 0 · 900 · 1800 · 2700 · 3600 (the quarter marks UCRS puts on an orbit), the deposit's CST stamp printed at the left end and the completion stamp at the right; y = dollars 0 → the deposit amount ($3,604.49 for the example), so the released line runs from the bottom-left corner to the top-right corner over exactly one MoT. A separate row below carries the YEAR's own A.B..C (perihelion orbit, day n · Q n · n/91 or DOWN) and the 33-day frame position — never mixed onto the money axes. · series: escrowed — falling diagonal from the amount to 0, hairline, intelligence colour (money not yet released) · released — rising diagonal 0 → amount, normal stroke, abundance (the accent): the sum the escrow has let go so far · withdrawable — flat 0 until +3 h, then identical to released, consciousness colour · available = withdrawable − withdrawn — the cash-on-hand line (sheet 3: "Outcome: Cash On Hand"), steps DOWN at each withdrawal; NOT drawn in the build today, must become the bold series · ratePerMin — printed in the title, not plotted (constant per deposit; summed over overlapping deposits in the balance panel) · marks: NOW — full-height hairline (blank colour) with the A.B..C reading in the title, e.g. 14.3006..2136 at +3 h, and the elapsed h/min beside the stamp · the 3-hour hold — a short tick on the baseline at x(deposit + 3 h), temporal colour, legend "| hold" · each withdrawal — a tick at the top edge at its instant, evolution colour; its amount and chain-hash short id in the record list · FULL — when elapsed ≥ MoT the title reads 3600.3600..3600 and the escrowed line has reached 0 · pending — when NOW is left of the deposit stamp: "pending from <stamp>" and nothing released · quarter gridlines at 900 / 1800 / 2700; frame border hairline; no fill anywhere · states: pending (t < deposit stamp): all escrowed, released 0, withdrawable 0, NOW outside the frame → releasing · held (0 ≤ elapsed < 3 h): released visible and rising at 8.252 ¢/min, withdrawable 0, a withdrawal is refused HOLD → releasing · withdrawable (elapsed ≥ 3 h, fraction < 1): withdrawable = released, available = withdrawable − withdrawn, $14.85 at exactly +3 h → released (fraction ≥ 1): escrowed 0, released = amount, reading FULL, the rate drops out of the live sum → withdrawn: each withdrawal steps available down, never below 0 — a larger request is refused INSUFFICIENT with the available figure → record states always printed: device_only · chain_ok / chain_broken · save_failed; before sign-in the example badge, after sign-in the person's own record · cadence: 1 Hz — one setInterval on the surface, null until mounted (no hydration mismatch); every number recomputed from balanceAt(txs, now). The series is currently 120 even samples over the MoT (≈ 6.07 h apart on 30.333 d), so the withdrawable step is drawn ≈ 3 h late — insert breakpoints at the deposit, +3 h, each withdrawal and NOW; the balance numbers are already exact. · phone: 360×150 viewBox at 100% width (≈ 366 px wide at 390 px), one column: balance (five numbers + the $/h·$/day·$/s ladder) first, then the chart, the year row, the forms (34 px inputs, inputMode=decimal, CST placeholder), the budget ladder, the record, the R-CORE badge last; legend wraps; every string a fin.* key; dark ground, strokes only, colours from the 13. Verified at source level only — no 390 px screenshot yet.

**Risks.**
- UNCOMMITTED BUILD: all 14 new paths are untracked and 10 tracked files are modified and unstaged (120 insertions) — the exact state AAR 2026-08-28 was written against ("thirteen uncommitted files destroyed"); the ledger's rev 2 and the R-CORE compare panel point at work no commit holds, and `npm run status` cannot name a SHA for it.
- The calendar law breaks in FY2028: perihelion 2028-01-05 12:28Z → 2029-01-02 18:13Z = 363.24 days, so day 364 never comes and Q4 is 0.76 d short; FY2027 is 367.41 d with 3.41 down days. The gate tests only FY2026 (0.39 d down) and the JSON's downNote assumes the orbit is always longer than 364 d.
- Two perihelion tables now exist (lib/soi-calendar.ts PERIHELION_UTC vs lib/financial-2525/calendar.ts PERIHELION) and disagree at 2024 (00:39 / 00:38), 2026 (17:16 / 17:15) and 2028 (12:11 / 12:28); the JSON records only 2028 — a NO-REWORK fork on the one anchor every 2525 surface shares.
- Deposit id `d-${when}-${cents}`: two deposits of the same amount at the same typed stamp collide and the second is silently dropped by the idempotent append, with no "already on the record" message.
- The deposit form accepts MoT = 0 (`Math.max(0, Number(dMot) || 0)`), which releases everything at once — the operator's third field can be empty in effect.
- CST-fixed stamps read one hour behind an Austin phone until 2026-11-01; a person typing phone-local time deposits an hour late; only the placeholder hints at it.
- The chart samples at len/120 (≈ 6.07 h): the withdrawable step is drawn ≈ 3 h late and `available` (cash on hand, the sheet's stated outcome) is not drawn at all.
- FIN-09's review record docs/financial-2525/reviews/r001_twelve_lenses.md does not exist (no reviews/ directory) and the gate checks only that the string is named.
- The fin.* ×32 fill is owed (66 keys staged in AFTER_FILL) against the all-33-languages law; the surface ships English-only to 32 languages until it lands.
- FIN-08 isolation is source-level only: a localStorage record keyed by user.sub on a shared device is readable by anyone holding the device; declared device_only, but no runtime test proves two owners never read each other.
- Math.round on released cents can show a cent up to half a cent early (the ≤ deposited invariant still holds via the clamp).
- Outside 2024–2035 the perihelion is DECLARED Jan 3 12:00 UTC and says so — correct — but the annual ephemeris confirmation both calendars ask for is scheduled nowhere.
- drone-ledger.gen.ts was regenerated (47 → 48 entries) as a side effect of re-running gen-rcore-revisions — benign, but it must ride in the same commit as the Drone ledger it reflects or the Drone gates will read a stale module.

**Ambiguities → declared default.**
- **Which A.B..C law does "MoT = 30.33 converted to 3600.3600..3600" mean — the elapsed FRACTION of one whole period on the 0…3600 scale (as built, FD-01), or lib/abc-3600's own literal law (A = whole unit = a day, B = 3600ths of a day), under which 30.333 days writes 30.1200..0000?** → FD-01 as built: the period is the whole; a moment is its fraction; completion = 3600.3600..3600. The abc-3600 literal form (30.1200..0000, one B = 24 s) is never shown for a MoT. — _The operator's words map the period onto the FULL writing, exactly the UCRS full orbit; only a dimensionless fraction reaches Mars without naming a planet's day. The transaction's third field (MoT length) is what turns the fraction back into LTU, so nothing is lost._
- **Which 'month' is THE month: the personal sheet's 33 days, the pay MoT 30.333 (= soi91 monthDays 91/3), or the Gregorian month CADENCE_UNIT.M already uses on /innovation (≈30.44 days)?** → Three named units, never blended: pay MoT = 30.333 (the deposit's own length), personal analysis M = 33, and any $/mo shown beside innovation figures says which. The surface prints the unit's days beside every $/M. — _The same $3,604.49 reads $118.83/day at 30.333 but $109.23/day at 33; a $/M on one surface would silently disagree with another. Naming the days is cheaper than choosing one._
- **Is the 3-hour hold measured once from the deposit day and time (FD-03), rolling 3 h behind each released minute, or 3 h after the first release?** → Once, from the deposit stamp: withdrawable = released when t ≥ atMs + 3 h (as built and gated: $14.85 at +3 h, $0 a minute earlier). — _The operator said "3 hrs later individual can withdraw" of the deposit event; a rolling hold would make the withdrawable line lag the released line for the whole MoT, which he did not describe._
- **The business up-front period: "2 weeks" (14 days) in the message versus the sheet's "2×/mth 0000.00.16_12.00..00 | 16.5 days" (33 ÷ 2)?** → Business: 14 days or 30.333 (test later, declared only). Personal sheet frame: 16.5 = 33 ÷ 2 kept as the sheet's own figure. — _The two figures belong to two tenants; the business frame is explicitly deferred, so nothing is built on the choice yet._
- **Which instant is the deposit's Start: the operator's "October 1, 2026 at 07:00" (2026.10.01_07.00..00 CST) or the bank's Posted 09/29/2026 (pending)?** → The operator's stated stamp is the Start; postedDate 2026-09-29 is a memo on the record. — _He gave the stamp in the record's own grammar; the bank date is provenance, and the record keeps both so nothing is invented._
- **With day 1 = the TRUE perihelion instant, the financial year runs 364.2–367.4 days (2025→26 is 364.16 d; 2027→28 is 367.4 d) — is 'down day 365' one day, or whatever remains until the next perihelion (0.16 to 3.4 days)?** → Whatever remains after day 364 is DOWN (as built: quarter 0, day ≥ 365, including 366/367 in a long orbit), shown as "down · day 366" honestly. — _"day resets everywhere at perihelion" is the stronger instruction; a fixed 365 would drift off the perihelion within a year. The variance is real and must be visible, not smoothed._
- **Is the year's A.B..C the celestial position (UCRS HU is an ANGLE on the orbit — Kepler's second law makes it non-uniform in time) or time-elapsed since perihelion?** → Time-linear (as built, motABC over the orbit's ms), labelled 'position in the financial year', not 'orbital angle'. — _ucrs-2525's own ucrsAt() is also linear (ltu = spotu × tSec), so the repo's celestial reading is already this simplification; money accrues on clock time, not on true anomaly._
- **Austin observes daylight time; the operator wrote "CST standard". Fixed UTC−6 all year?** → UTC−6 fixed (FD-02); stamps carry 'CST' and never shift. — _A shifting zone would make one wall-clock hour appear twice a year on an append-only record; a fixed offset keeps stamps monotonic and matches tzFromLon's −6._
- **What is 'tokenization' of a paycheck under the 9,999 웃 cap, when 웃 = M × hours is currency-free and no dollar may enter the mint?** → Nothing is minted in the personal pilot (FIN-10 draft). When wired, hours come from the POD clock (pod-clock measure) and the cap/carry from pod-yug standing(); dollars only ever settle (settle(yug, stampedRate)). — _pod-yug.ts is explicit that a mint with currency in it is a published defect; the only honest bridge is time witnessed, not money received._
- **"each user gets their own log in" — the login is Auth0, but Supabase today isolates by a per-browser bearer key (030) or service_role (035); is a person's record per login across phones or per device?** → Per device under the login's key (FD-07), said on the surface (fin.device_only); the cloud half is declared, not faked, until Supabase third-party auth carries the login. — _The signer-never-meets-the-machine rule forbids a screen that pretends to sync; the honest sentence is the right default until the migration exists._
- **Which pieces of /soi-session are 'the shell' (addendum 7): the header (SoiGlobe + TrinityGlyphs, page.tsx:877-881), the PodPhaseRail (:891), the big clock (data-testid pod-clock, :1339) and the phone strip (pod-strip, :1797) — or the whole Tailwind card chrome?** → Those four, extracted once into a shared component and mounted by both pages; the financial cards keep the vector-law dark ground inside it. — _The four are what a person recognises as the Session; extracting them is reuse, re-styling the whole financial page as Tailwind cards would be a rebuild._
- **The sheet's slips (47,620 min; 4,762 $/M) — record or correct on the surface?** → Compute the correct figures (47,520; 4,752) and keep the slips on the record (budget.sheetSlips), never printed as values. — _PERSIST FIRST keeps his sheet verbatim; a gate must still hold the arithmetic._

**MoT law, as this lens read it.** A MoT is ONE WHOLE — the pay period is the unit. For the operator's 30.333-day period (91 ÷ 3 = 2,620,800 s = 43,680 min): A = 3600ths of the period, so one A = 2,620,800 ÷ 3600 = 728 s (12.133 min); B = 3600ths of an A = 0.2022 s; C = 3600ths of a B = 56.2 µs (mot.ts motUnits, gated). The start reads 0.0000..0000; completion reads 3600.3600..3600 — the sheet's three 'Equal' writings (3600.0000..0000 · 3600.3600..3600 · 3599.3599..3599) all read FULL (isFull). That is what '30.33 converts to 3600.3600..3600' means: whatever the period's length, it is laid on the same full scale UCRS-2525 uses for one orbit (HU 0 = perihelion … 3600 = full orbit; FULL_ORBIT in ucrs-2525.ts = FULL_UNIT_ABC in abc-3600.ts), so the notation is dimensionless progress and never names a planet. Converting to Earth LTU needs the MoT's own length — the transaction's third field: seconds = (A + B/3600 + C/3600²) ÷ 3600 × motSec. Worked: +3 h = 10,800 s → fraction 0.0041209 → ×3600 = 14.8352 → reads ≈ 14.3006..2135; back: 14.8352/3600 × 2,620,800 = 10,800 s. Then days = fraction × 30.333 (here 0.125 d), and D · W · M · Q · Y divide by LTU_DAYS {1, 7, 33, 99, 365}; h · min · s multiply by 24 · 1440 · 86400. Money rides the same fraction: released = amount × fraction, so $3,604.49 × 0.0041209 = $14.85 at +3 h, and the rate is 360,449 ¢ ÷ 43,680 min = 8.252 ¢/min = $0.0825/min = $4.95/h = $118.83/day = $0.001375/s. The other readable law (abc-3600's own header: A = whole unit, B = 3600ths of it) would write 30.333 days as 30.1200..0000 with one B = 24 s; it is not what the operator wrote and cannot reach Mars without a planet-named day, so FD-01 stands and the sheets' duration grammar 0000.00.33_00.00..00 stays a separate LTU writing. Mars: a 30-sol MoT (30 × 88,775.244 s) reads the identical A.B..C at every fraction; only motSec differs (gated, FIN-13). Addendum 8 rules the glass: A.B..C stays behind the scenes; the chart's default axis is day · hour · minute.

**Chart.** axes: x = elapsed time inside the focused deposit's MoT, from its stamp (left edge: 2026.10.01 07:00 CST) to stamp + MoT (right edge); tick labels in DAY (D1…D30, thin gridline per day, heavier every 7), with the current position spelled 'day 3 · 14 h · 07 min' (addendum 8); the A.B..C reading (≈14.3006..2135 … 3600.3600..3600) appears once in the caption under the title, never on the axis (replaces today's 0 · 900 · 1800 · 2700 · 3600 labels). y = dollars, 0 → the deposit amount ($3,604.49); the live $/min is a stat tile above the plot, not an axis. A second thin strip beneath: the perihelion year (day n/364 · Q k, or DOWN) and the 33-day frame, from calendar.ts positionInYear / frameOf. · series: escrowed — falling line from amount to 0 (thin, intelligence colour) · released — rising line at amount ÷ MoT-minutes (accent, the money the person can SEE) · withdrawable — 0 until +3 h, then equals released (consciousness colour) · available — withdrawable minus every withdrawal, a step down at each one (the money the person can MOVE) · withdrawn — cumulative steps (evolution colour) · $/min — numeric tile, the sum over every deposit still releasing (accrual.ts balanceAt.ratePerMinCents), with $/h · $/day · $/s beneath · marks: NOW — a vertical hairline that moves (1 Hz) · +3 h HOLD — a vertical tick at stamp + 3 h labelled '3 h' (accrual.ts HOLD_MS) · each withdrawal — a vertical tick at its stamp with its amount · deposit stamp — left edge label in CST · MoT end — right edge; 'released' state once the line reaches the amount · day gridlines (heavier every 7 = W) · year strip: perihelion day 1 · Q1–Q4 boundaries at 91 · DOWN after 364 · a 'last hour' inset that visibly advances: at $0.0825/min the inset climbs $4.95 per hour · states: pending — now < stamp: everything in escrow, lines flat at 0, cursor left of the frame, 'pending from <stamp>' → releasing · held — 0 to 3 h: released rising, withdrawable 0, the hold mark still ahead of the cursor → releasing · withdrawable — 3 h to MoT end: withdrawable = released; available = released − withdrawn → released — fraction = 1: escrow 0, released flat at the amount; the A.B..C caption reads 3600.3600..3600 → withdrawn — each withdrawal a step down in available plus a mark; refusals shown by name in one line (HOLD · INSUFFICIENT · AMOUNT) → chain broken / not saved — one sentence in the person's terms (fin.chain_broken · fin.save_failed); never a storage or database word · cadence: One clock on the surface: setInterval 1000 ms (command-ux1.tsx:58) drives every number, the NOW cursor and the caption; the polyline is re-sampled from accrual.ts series() at 120 samples per MoT on the same tick (120 × balanceAt, cheap). A 30-day line moves about 1 px per 6 h at 340 px, so motion must come from the tile, the cursor and the last-hour inset, not from the main line; no clock is read inside the pure libraries. · phone: 390 px wide, 16 px gutters, no horizontal scroll; SVG viewBox 360×150 scaling to width; legend wraps beneath; the balance tile and the $/min sit above the fold; any day/hour/min toggle is a 44 px target; dark ground by law (VECTOR_LAW.ground), strokes only, one accent from the 13; the operator's own deposit runs live before sign-in so the first paint is never empty; the shell around it is the ◬ ♡ 웃 Session's (globe + glyphs header, phase rail, big clock, phone strip) once addendum 7 is folded.

**Invariants.**
- Each transaction gets amount, Deposit day and time, and MoT length of time — nothing else is needed to accrue. — `test:financial-accrual (FIN-03; FinTx shape) — exists, 28/28`
- Escrow releases $/min: money released is never more than money deposited, and the rate is amount ÷ MoT-minutes and nothing else. — `test:financial-accrual (FIN-04; releasedAt clamp, ratePerMinCents) — exists`
- 3 hrs later the individual can withdraw: before +3 h nothing is withdrawable, a withdrawal never makes the balance negative, and a refusal says why. — `test:financial-accrual (HOLD · INSUFFICIENT · AMOUNT; $14.85 at +3 h, $0 a minute before) — exists`
- A deposit once recorded is on the record for good; a changed amount breaks the chain where it was changed; the record replays to any revision. — `test:financial-accrual (record.ts verify/brokenAt, replay) — exists`
- Revision control from the get go, the same framework as Drone-2525: one editable source, rendered views never drift, revisions strictly increase, a release cites its commit, ids are FIN-##.## two digits never letters. — `test:financial-crs (financial-render --check, assertSource) — exists, 130/130`
- The day resets everywhere at perihelion, Austin as CST standard (UTC−6 fixed): the perihelion instant is day 1 · Q1 · 0.0000..0000. — `test:financial-mot (calendar section) — exists, 41/41`
- 91 days for a quarter with down day 365: day 364 closes Q4; day 365 (and any remainder to the next perihelion) is owned by no quarter. — `test:financial-mot — exists`
- MoT = 30.33 converted to 3600.3600..3600: start 0.0000..0000, completion FULL, the three Equal writings all FULL, A.B..C ↔ LTU round-trips within one second, and a Martian period reads the same A.B..C. — `test:financial-mot (FIN-01, FIN-13) — exists`
- 9999 HI TOKEN CAP: one ceiling, the POD's, never a second constant on this surface. — `test:financial-crs today (weak: checks the string in pod-yug.ts) → propose test:financial-reuse asserting `import { YUG_CEILING } from "@/lib/pod-yug"` the moment FIN-10 wires`
- Max R-CORE REUSE (addendum 8): no financial file redeclares a number the repo already exports — 1440 (MINUTES_PER_DAY), 91 and 91/3 (CALENDARS.soi91), −6 (tzFromLon CST), the perihelion table — and there is one perihelion table in the repo. — `NEW test:financial-reuse (source-level, in test:ci)`
- Behind the scenes is A.B..C but the UX is defaulted in day · hour · min for the financial tracking chart. — `extend test:financial-surface: axis tick labels match day/h/min; A.B..C appears only in the caption`

### Odin — predictive and future-proof testing: Mars and other planets, business after personal, tokenization under the 9,999 웃 cap, what breaks in a year

**Readiness:** B

**One sentence.** Revision 0.001 is on the record with green gates and the money law is sound, so what breaks within a year is the calendar it hangs on — a perihelion that jitters three days and does not fit the 364-day grid in FY2028, a CST stamp an hour off the Austin phone for eight months, a 30.333-day MoT that runs a day ahead of the paydays it models, and two perihelion tables and two 9,999 constants already drifting apart — and every one of those must be declared on the glass and gated before the business frame is tested.

**Ambiguities → declared default.**
- **Is "October 1, 2026 at 07:00" 07:00 CST standard (13:00 UTC, as shipped) or 07:00 on the operator's phone, which in Austin on 1 October runs CDT (12:00 UTC)?** → 07:00 CST standard = 13:00 UTC, exactly as the operator ruled and as cstMs() ships; every stamp on the glass carries the zone word (a fin.* key), and a person typing a time is told once that the record keeps CST standard all year. — _For roughly eight months a year the record's 07:00 is 08:00 on an Austin phone; if the rule is not printed beside every stamp the operator will report the release starting 'an hour late' and the fix will be argued at the instance, not the class._
- **When the next monthly paycheck lands on a Gregorian 1st (31 days after 1 October, 28 after 1 February) while each MoT is 30.333 days, does the next MoT start at the actual deposit instant (gap of ~23 h at rate 0 in October→November; overlap in February) or does the MoT chain run continuously grid-to-grid?** → The MoT starts at the actual deposit day and time (FD-05) and the glass shows the truth: a gap reads rate $0/min with a 'next deposit expected' mark, an overlap reads the summed rate (balanceAt already sums). — _12 × 30.333 = 364 days = the 4×91 grid, so the pay MoT is grid-aligned, not month-aligned; by the twelfth deposit the chain is a day ahead of the paydays. Hiding the gap would fake money; declaring it keeps the record honest and hands the business frame its first real requirement (the employer funds on the grid, not on the calendar)._
- **Which year does the 9,999 웃 payout cap count in — the Financial-2525 year (perihelion to perihelion), the calendar year, or the tax year — when FIN-10 is wired?** → The Financial-2525 year: day 1 = perihelion, because the operator said the day resets everywhere at perihelion; the cap and the carry (pod-yug standing()) are rolled at that instant. — _pod-yug's standing() has no notion of which year; the moment two calendars exist there are two caps, and a person near 9,999 in late December can be paid twice or not at all depending on which one is read._
- **Does "3 hrs later individual can withdraw" mean one hold that lifts 3 hours after the deposit (FD-03, shipped) or a rolling hold in which each released dollar becomes withdrawable 3 hours after it was released?** → One hold, lifted at deposit + 3 h; after that, released money is withdrawable the second it is released. — _The operator's sentence attaches '3 hrs later' to the up-front escrow payment, not to each minute; a rolling hold would need a second series on the chart and a second invariant, and nothing in the notes asks for it._
- **Does "MoT = 30.33 converted to 3600.3600..3600" mean the POSITION inside the period (start 0.0000..0000, end 3600.3600..3600 — FD-01, shipped) or the LENGTH written in day-based A.B..C (30.1200..0000 by lib/abc-3600 format(91/3))?** → The position law (FD-01): the whole period is FULL, exactly the UCRS-2525 orbit convention; the day-based length is a reveal beside the duration stamp 0000.00.30_08.00..00, never the axis. — _The operator equates the MoT itself to 3600.3600..3600 and names Celestial-2525 as the scale; only the position reading makes Mars trivial (the same fraction reads the same figure on any planet)._
- **On Mars, is $/min per SI minute (60 s) or per a Martian minute (a sol ÷ 1440 = 61.65 s)?** → Per SI minute; a sol is 88,775.244 s and only the MoT length changes; the LTU table gains a planet-scoped minutesPerDay row read from UCRS-2525 PLANETS.rotDays, never a second unit system. — _A rate is amount ÷ seconds; giving the minute a planet-dependent length would make one paycheck two rates and break the invariant 'the rate is amount ÷ MoT-minutes and nothing else'._
- **Is "reset at perihelion each year" the instantaneous perihelion (which the Moon jitters by ±1.5 days: years of 363.24 to 367.53 days on the shipped table) or the mean anomalistic year (365.2596 days) counted from one epoch?** → The sourced instantaneous perihelion, as shipped (the operator's 'each year' and a date he can look up); a year shorter than the grid DECLARES that Q4 is cut short and a long year declares its extra down days — never a silent day 1. — _FY2028 is 363.24 days, so 'day 364 closes Q4' cannot hold that year; if the glass does not say so, the first person paid across 2 January 2029 sees a quarter vanish with no explanation._
- **Is the pilot deposit an escrow FUNDED by the employer at the stamp (as the accrual models) or a bank posting the person records after the fact (posted 09/29, pending)?** → A record the person types: the deposit stamp is the escrow instant, the bank's posting date is a memo; in the pilot the person is both payer and payee. — _There is no employer integration and no money moves; the disclaimer already says 'a RECORD of what a person typed, never a bank', and the business frame is where the payer becomes a second login._
- **What does Financial-2525 tokenize under the 9,999 cap — does a dollar deposit mint 웃 ($3,604.49 ÷ $7.25 = 497 웃), or only POD-witnessed hours?** → Only witnessed hours: 웃 = M × hours from the POD clock, settled onto the person's record at the stamped rate; a dollar never mints a 웃. — _pod-yug rule 2: the mint is currency-free; dividing a paycheck by a minimum wage is a mint with a currency in it, the exact defect the 47.9× Nigeria/Washington spread came from._
- **Do 'expenses' enter the record as real-time transactions (each grocery run a withdrawal against a budget line) or only as the declared 33-day budget lines?** → Declared lines only in r.001 (FIN-06); actual spend logged as withdrawals against a line is FIN-06's stretch and waits for feedback. — _The sheet's outcome is 'Cash On Hand'; the pilot needs the person to use it before the second transaction kind is designed, and one more kind now is one more class of failure to enumerate._
- **The personal quarter is 99 days (3 × 33) and the business quarter is 91 days (4-4-5 = 28+28+35); which does the word 'quarter' mean on a screen a business person and an individual both read?** → Q99 on the personal surface, Q91 on the business surface, each labelled with its day count (a fin.* key 'Q99' / 'Q91'), never a bare Q. — _The two grids never coincide (99 ≠ 91) and by year end the personal Q4 ends 32 days after the business Q4; the mismatch must be named before business is tested or the first shared report will disagree with itself._
- **Is the surface's default MoT the literal 30.333 the operator typed (SRC.mot.payMotDays, 43,679.52 min) or 91 ÷ 3 (43,680 min, what MOT_PAY_MONTH_DAYS and the gates use)?** → 91 ÷ 3 behind the scenes, printed as 30.333 on the glass; the deposit form parses '30.333' to 91/3 when it matches the pay MoT to three decimals. — _The gap is 28.8 s and one part per million on the rate, harmless to money but a permanent disagreement between the glass and the gate that a replay hash will one day expose._

**MoT law, as this lens read it.** A MoT (Measure of Time) is ONE WHOLE and a moment inside it is its elapsed fraction in Base-3600 — the same convention as UCRS-2525 where HU 0 is perihelion and HU 3600 is a full orbit (3600.3600..3600). For the pay period of 30.333 days (91 ÷ 3, exactly 2,620,800 s): A = one 3600th of the period = 728 s (12 min 8 s), B = one 3600th of an A = 0.2022 s, C = one 3600th of a B = 56.2 µs. The deposit instant reads 0.0000..0000; +3 h (the hold) reads 14.3006..2136; +1 day reads 118.2452..2690; +7 days 830.2769..0831; half way 1800.0000..0000; the end reads 3600.3600..3600, and the sheet's three writings 3600.0000..0000 · 3600.3600..3600 · 3599.3599..3599 are all FULL (isFull). That is what '30.33 converts to 3600.3600..3600' means: the length is the whole, so the whole reads FULL, not a day count. The length itself is written as the sheet writes durations, 0000.00.30_08.00..00 (30 d 8 h), and the day-based abc-3600 form 30.1200..0000 (format(91/3)) is a reveal, never the axis. Earth LTU conversion: seconds = (A + B/3600 + C/3600²) × 728; minutes = that ÷ 60; D/W/M/Q/Y = seconds ÷ 86,400 ÷ {1, 7, 33, 99, 365}; money follows the same fraction, $3,604.49 × fraction = $0.0013753/s = $0.08252/min = $4.9512/h = $118.83/d, so $14.85 has been released at 14.3006..2136. The financial year is its own MoT (perihelion to perihelion; FY2026 is 364.39 d, one A = 8,745 s) and its A.B..C is the celestial orbit position. Mars: a 30-sol MoT is 2,663,257 s, one A = 739.8 s, and a quarter of it reads 900.0000..0000 exactly as on Earth; only the LTU row (sol = 88,775.244 s) changes, and $/min stays per SI minute.

**Chart.** axes: x = elapsed time inside the focused deposit's MoT, labelled by default in Earth LTU at 0 · ¼ · ½ · ¾ · full (for 30.333 d: 0 h 0 min · 7 d 14 h · 15 d 4 h · 22 d 18 h · 30 d 8 h), switching to the Base-3600 A scale (0 · 900 · 1800 · 2700 · 3600) on the A.B..C reveal; y = dollars from 0 to the deposit amount ($3,604.49) with 0 · half · full ticks; a secondary hairline strip under x shows where NOW sits in the 33-day frame and in the perihelion year (day n/364, Q, or DOWN). · series: released — chartreuse (C.abundance), normal stroke, linear from 0 at the deposit stamp to the amount at MoT end · withdrawable — C.consciousness, normal stroke, flat 0 until the 3-hour hold then equal to released · available — withdrawable minus withdrawn, stepping down at every withdrawal (NOT drawn today; add it, it is the number a person acts on) · escrowed — C.intelligence, hairline, the mirror of released descending to 0 · live rate — a numeric readout above the chart, $/min · $/h · $/day · $/s, summed over every deposit still releasing · marks: the 3-hour hold: a short vertical tick at x(deposit + 3 h) in C.temporal · NOW: a full-height hairline in C.blank that moves every second while inside the MoT · each withdrawal: a top tick in C.evolution at its stamp · the MoT end and the next expected deposit: a dotted mark at 30.333 d so a gap (rate 0) is visible, not hidden · the perihelion / year boundary and any DOWN day inside the window as a labelled hairline · the A.B..C position of NOW printed beside the title on reveal (e.g. 14.3006..2136) · states: pending — before the deposit stamp: every series flat at 0, header reads 'pending from 2026.10.01_07.00..00 CST' → escrow / releasing-held — 0 to 3 h: released rises, withdrawable stays 0, hold tick ahead of NOW, withdraw button refuses HOLD by name → releasing-withdrawable — 3 h to MoT end: released and withdrawable rise together, available steps down at each withdrawal, INSUFFICIENT refused by name → released — at MoT end: escrowed 0, released = amount, rate reads $0/min until the next deposit (the gap is shown) → overlap — two deposits releasing: the rate readout is the sum and both MoTs are selectable as the focus → declared — a year outside the sourced perihelion table or a truncated Q4 prints DECLARED beside the year strip · cadence: One 1 Hz clock (the surface gate asserts a single setInterval(…,1000)): balances, rate and the NOW line tick every second; the curves are re-sampled from series() at 120 points per MoT (≈ 6 h 4 min per point on 30.333 d), so the hold tick and withdrawals are drawn as marks rather than read from the curve; no network, no randomness, every number through balanceAt(). · phone: viewBox 360×150 at width 100%, legible at 390 px with the balance rows (escrowed · released · withdrawable · withdrawn · available) directly above; one accent; strokes only on the black ground; the A.B..C toggle is a single button, LTU is the default (operator addendum 8); the three fields of a deposit ($ · stamp · MoT) are the only inputs.

**Invariants.**
- Money released is never more than money deposited, however long after. — `tests/financial-accrual.test.mjs (releasedCents at +40 d = amount)`
- A withdrawal never makes the available balance negative and is refused by name (HOLD · INSUFFICIENT · AMOUNT). — `tests/financial-accrual.test.mjs (validateWithdrawal cases)`
- The rate is amount ÷ MoT-minutes and nothing else: $3,604.49 over 30.333 days is 8.252 ¢/min. — `tests/financial-accrual.test.mjs + tests/financial-mot.test.mjs (perMin)`
- A transaction is $, the deposit day and time, and the MoT — always the three fields, and the worked example accrues from those alone. — `tests/financial-accrual.test.mjs (FIN-03)`
- The record is append-only and chain-hashed: an id is never written twice, a changed amount breaks the chain at that revision, and the record replays to any revision. — `tests/financial-accrual.test.mjs (verify · replay · idempotent append)`
- The start of a MoT reads 0.0000..0000, the end 3600.3600..3600, and the sheet's three Equal writings all read FULL. — `tests/financial-mot.test.mjs (motABC · isFull)`
- A.B..C converts to Earth LTU and back within one second over a 30.333-day MoT, and a Martian 30-sol MoT reads the same figure at the same fraction. — `tests/financial-mot.test.mjs (round-trip loop · Mars case)`
- 07:00 CST is 13:00 UTC in every month of the year; a stamp parses back to the same instant and garbage is null, never a date. — `tests/financial-mot.test.mjs (cstMs · parseStampCST)`
- The year resets at perihelion: the perihelion instant is day 1 · Q1 · 0.0000..0000, day 364 closes Q4, day 365 is DOWN, and a year outside the sourced table is DECLARED and says so. — `tests/financial-mot.test.mjs (positionInYear) — TO EXTEND with the FY2028 short-year case (363.24 d) and FY2027 long-year case (367.41 d): financial-year-fit`
- The 9,999 웃 cap is the pod's YUG_CEILING, read from lib/pod-yug.ts, and no second constant exists anywhere that this surface can read. — `tests/financial-crs.test.mjs line 65 (presence only) — TO STRENGTHEN: financial-cap-one-source asserts the Financial import comes from pod-yug and that pod-clock.ts re-exports rather than redeclares`
- One perihelion table in the repo: the SoI calendar and the Financial calendar name the same instant for every shared year. — `NEW: financial-perihelion-one-source (soi-calendar imports PERIHELION from lib/financial-2525/calendar or the reverse; the test asserts equality year by year)`
- A gap between two MoTs reads $0/min on the glass and an overlap reads the sum; neither is ever smoothed. — `tests/financial-accrual.test.mjs (overlap sums today) — TO EXTEND with a gap case: series() between MoT end and the next deposit reads ratePerMin 0`
- One 1 Hz clock, nothing random, the chart is strokes on the black ground in the 13 colours, the R-CORE badge is last, and the record says where it lives and whether its chain held. — `tests/financial-surface.test.mjs`
- The generated views (CRS · README · decisions · domain.gen.ts) never disagree with the one editable source, and the handoff hashes byte for byte. — `scripts/financial-render.mjs --check inside tests/financial-crs.test.mjs`
- The twelve-lens review named on the record exists as a file before its revision is called reviewed. — `NEW line in tests/financial-crs.test.mjs: fs.existsSync(reviews[n].record) whenever status is not PENDING`
- Every word a person reads is in all 33 languages; a key added after the last fill is staged and listed, never silent. — `tests/financial-i18n.test.mjs + tests/lexicon-coverage.test.mjs`

**Risks.**
- The perihelion jitters: on the shipped table FY2028 runs 363.24 days (2028-01-05 12:28 → 2029-01-02 18:13), shorter than the 4×91 = 364 grid, so 'day 364 closes Q4' fails that year; FY2027 and FY2024 run 367.4–367.5 days (three-plus DOWN days). The gate asserts only 2026/2027 and the glass has no word for a truncated quarter.
- CST standard is an hour off the Austin wall clock for about eight months a year: the record's 07:00 is 08:00 CDT on 1 October; without the zone printed on every stamp the operator will report the release 'starting late' and the fix will be argued at the instance.
- Pay cadence vs MoT: monthly paydays are 28–31 days apart, the MoT is 30.333; October→November leaves ~23 h at $0/min, February overlaps and doubles the rate; 12 MoTs = 364 days so the chain drifts a day a year — exactly the DOWN day, which is elegant only if it is declared.
- Two YUG_CEILING constants already exist (lib/pod-yug.ts:40, lib/pod-clock.ts:44) and FD-09 says 'never a second constant'; the FIN-10 gate only greps for the string, so a third copy on the Financial surface would pass.
- Two perihelion tables (soi-calendar.ts 17:16Z vs financial calendar.ts 17:15Z for 2026, and different 2028 values) — a NO-REWORK breach in embryo; in a year one will be 'corrected' and the other will not.
- The record is device-only localStorage with no eviction path (pod-store evicts the oldest pod; record.ts saveRecord just returns false) and no cloud half (FD-07 declares the wait on wiring the login into Supabase): a phone reset or a full device loses a year of transactions — the three-dead-ends class.
- The chain hash is FNV-1a (two 32-bit lanes), tamper-evident on one phone and nothing more; adequate for a personal pilot, unfit for a business record with two parties, and the JSON must say so before the business frame is tested.
- Mars: motABC is planet-neutral but MIN_PER_DAY = 1440, LTU_DAYS, cstParts and fmtDurationDays are Earth-bound; FIN-13 proves only the fraction. A sol needs a per-planet LTU row (UCRS PLANETS.rotDays) or the first Martian $/min will silently use an Earth day.
- Tokenization temptation: the obvious wiring divides the paycheck by a minimum wage to mint 웃; pod-yug forbids a currency in the mint. The only lawful route is POD-witnessed hours → 웃 → settle onto the record at the stamped rate, and that route has no call site yet.
- Personal Q99 and business Q91 share the word 'quarter' and never coincide (33+33+33 ≠ 28+28+35); the first report a person and their employer both read will disagree with itself unless the day count is on the label.
- The chart samples 120 points per MoT (~6 h per point): the released curve near NOW lags up to six hours behind the 1 Hz balance figures; harmless as strokes, misleading if anyone reads the curve instead of the number.
- FIN-09 is 'approved' while docs/financial-2525/reviews/r001_twelve_lenses.md does not exist and no gate checks the named record file; a review that lives only in conversation is the AAR 2026-08-28 failure again.
- The glass defaults the MoT input to the literal '30.333' (SRC.mot.payMotDays) while the laws and gates use 91/3 — 28.8 s and one ppm apart, a permanent glass-vs-gate disagreement a replay hash will surface.
- Three handoff hashes in one day (handoffHistory) and the ledger's rev 1 quotes the middle one while project.handoffSha256 is the last: readable, but a reader must be told which hash r.001 shipped against (7dc5…) and which the source now pins (fe47…).
- Escrow and a 3-hour withdrawal describe a money transmitter; the pilot is a record of typed figures and the disclaimer says so — the business frame must not inherit the words 'escrow' and 'withdraw' without a declared boundary between record and rail.

**The three things revision 0.001 must do first.**
- Revision 0.001 is already shipped (39f44c1; ledger rev 3), so the first act of its line is to PERSIST the twelve-lens round 1: write docs/financial-2525/reviews/r001_twelve_lenses.md (every lens's record, this one included), append revisions[] 0.002 kind 'decision' that flips reviews[0] from PENDING, append ledger rev 4, and add the gate line to tests/financial-crs.test.mjs that a named review record must exist on disk — FIN-09's own metric, never satisfied by a conversation.
- Rebuild the surface INSIDE the ◬ ♡ 웃 Session shell (addendum 7; ledger rev 3 names it r.002's first item): mount the existing FinancialCommandUX1 panels in /soi-session's chrome — SoITrinity header with the globe and Trinity glyphs, PodPhaseRail, the hhmmss big clock, the phone strip — reusing components, never repainting; keep MotChart's LTU default and A.B..C reveal (addendum 8, already on the glass) and add the 'available' series and the gap/overlap state so the chart shows the rate falling to $0/min between MoTs.
- Close the two one-source breaches and the calendar hole before a year passes: make lib/financial-2525/calendar.ts PERIHELION the single table (soi-calendar.ts imports it; gate financial-perihelion-one-source), make lib/pod-clock.ts re-export pod-yug's YUG_CEILING and have the Financial surface import it (gate financial-cap-one-source), and extend tests/financial-mot.test.mjs with the FY2028 short year (363.24 d: Q4 truncated is DECLARED on the glass) and the FY2027 long year (367.41 d: extra DOWN days) — the invariant the operator's own words imply and the year that will break first.

### Pangu — cutting-edge innovation: what the real-time $/min chart can be that no banking app is (read-only review; nothing edited)

**Readiness:** B

**One sentence.** The notes were enough to ship v.000_r.001 and its 531 green assertions prove it, but the one thing the operator drew that no banking app has — money as a line that breathes at $/sec, with the hold, the available step-downs, the planned-spend slope and the orbit position all on one phone screen — is still a sketch on sheet 3, and the thirteen uncommitted files holding r.002 must be persisted before another line of it is designed.
