**Ambiguities → declared default.**
- **Is a "monthly" paycheck's MoT 30.333 days (91 ÷ 3, the operator's example line) or the sheets' 33-day month ("1×/mth → 0000.00.33")? 30.333 × 12 = 364 fits the grid; 33 × 11 = 363 does not.** → The MoT is typed on each deposit; the paycheck carries 30.333 (FD-04, shipped); 33 is the personal ANALYSIS month (M33) and the chart's frame, never the deposit's length. — _The operator wrote 30.333 on the transaction and M = 33D on the analysis sheet; keeping them apart lets the record accrue at the operator's number while the frame reads the sheet's._
- **Which instant is the deposit "day and time": the bank's posted date 09/29/2026 or the operator's example line 2026-10-01 07:00?** → 2026.10.01_07.00..00 is the deposit instant (shipped); 09/29 is carried as the funding memo, and the chart should show a FUNDED mark two days before RELEASE begins. — _The operator gave the 07:00 line as the transaction; treating the bank post as a separate funding event keeps the pilot honest about money already in his account while escrow reads $0 released._
- **Is a real paycheck on a calendar month (Oct 1, Nov 1: 31 days) with a 30.333 MoT: does the gap (0.667 d) read $0/min and does an early next deposit overlap and sum?** → Overlaps sum (balanceAt already sums rates), gaps read $0/min; the next deposit's date is whatever is posted, never derived. — _The record is data, never invented; the law says the rate is amount ÷ MoT-minutes and nothing else._
- **CST standard is UTC−6 all year, but the operator's Austin phone shows CDT (UTC−5) on 2026-10-01: his 07:00 CDT is 06:00 on the surface.** → Keep CST fixed (FD-02, the operator's explicit rule); print "CST" on every stamp and, while the phone's zone differs, one line "your phone reads CDT (+1 h)". — _The rule is his and it is what makes the instants universal; the one-hour surprise is the risk, so it is labelled, not silently converted._
- **Day boundaries: do they fall every 24 h from the perihelion INSTANT (17:15 UTC = 11:15 CST in 2026, shipped) or at CST midnight of the perihelion day?** → The instant (shipped in calendar.ts); the surface prints the CST stamp beside the day number. — _Only the instant survives the trip to Mars; midnight is a local name for a day, not the day._
- **The headline money unit: the sheet says "Baseline: $/Day · Unlock $/min", the message says "$/min of an individuals financials".** → $/min is the headline rate on the glass with $/hour · $/day · $/sec on the ladder line beneath (shipped); $/day is the budget's column. — _The operator's own stated purpose is $/min; the sheet's baseline names the budget's unit, not the live rate's._
- **What is a "withdrawal" in the personal pilot when the money is already in his bank — a spend against a budget line, or a transfer to another account?** → Any outflow; r.003 adds an optional `category` (Home · Auto · …) on the withdrawal so the sheet's Swings (actual vs plan) can be drawn; a withdrawal without a category is still legal. — _Sheet 3's outcome is "Cash On Hand" and its chart is Swings; without a category the budget stays seed data and the swings cannot exist._
- **The business "2 weeks" escrow: 14 days or the sheet's 16.5 days (2×/mth = 0000.00.16_12.00..00 = half of 33)?** → TEST LATER (addendum 5); when tested, the MoT is typed on the deposit and the sheet's 16.5 is recorded as the sheet's figure, not a rule. — _The business frame is declared, not built, until the personal framework has received feedback._
- **What is tokenized under the 9,999 웃 cap — is a dollar deposit ever 웃?** → Nothing in r.001–r.002 (FIN-10 draft); 웃 comes only from POD-witnessed hours via lib/pod-yug.ts mint = hours × M; a paycheck has no hours and mints nothing. — _The mint is currency-free by operator ruling 2026-09-10; letting dollars mint 웃 would reopen the arbitrage the pod closed._
- **"$/Balance · $/End of Mth · $ Target · 11 · 22 · 33D" and "$/D: 144 → 138 → 137": are these three headline numbers and a target ladder the chart must show?** → Yes as the r.003 chart: balance now, projected cash-on-hand at the MoT's end (released − planned spend − withdrawn), and a $/D target with marks at 11 · 22 · 33 D; not shipped yet. — _It is the only part of the notes that describes the chart itself, and it is the part no banking app draws._
- **Undefined sheet tokens: "LSU 3600 – 3600.3600 · 3", "Charge to 2026_01_11–13.11..11", "◬ = ATX = AI", "Idea + | • · – | •• · ∞ | •••", "Analysis: Backward · MoT ↔ MoT", "⑤ ⑦ M.o.T. ①".** → Recorded as sheet text on the register, implemented nowhere; ATX read as Austin, Texas (the CST anchor) only if the operator confirms. — _A figure or glyph the operator has not explained is data to carry, never a rule to invent._
- **Does the pilot need the record on more than one device (phone + laptop) now?** → No (FD-07): device-local under the login's key, the surface saying so; cloud isolation waits on the login reaching Supabase. — _An honest device record beats a cloud half that would use a bearer key and claim more than it is._
- **Display precision: released money is stored in whole cents and the cent rolls only every ~7.3 s at $0.001375/s, so a 1 Hz number sits still for seven ticks.** → The record stays integer cents; the live released number shows four decimals ($14.8523) so it moves every second; every stored figure stays cents. — _The operator asked for $/sec; a number that visibly breathes each second is the heartbeat that makes escrow real, and the ledger stays exact._

**MoT law, as this lens read it.** MoT in A.B..C is the elapsed FRACTION of one whole in Base-3600 (FD-01, shipped in /home/user/eXeL-AI-Polling/frontend/lib/financial-2525/mot.ts over lib/abc-3600.ts toABC/fmtABC/abcToValue, mirroring ucrs-2525 FULL_ORBIT where HU 0 = perihelion and 3600 = a full orbit). For the pay period of 30.333 days (91 ÷ 3 = 2,620,800 s = 43,680 min): A = 3600ths of the MoT, so ONE A = 2,620,800 ÷ 3600 = 728 s (12 min 8 s); B = 3600ths of an A = 0.2022 s; C = 3600ths of a B = 56.2 µs. The start of the period reads 0.0000..0000, the end reads 3600.3600..3600, and the sheet's three Equal writings (3600.0000..0000 · 3600.3600..3600 · 3599.3599..3599) all read FULL (isFull). \"30.33 converts to 3600.3600..3600\" therefore means: the whole 30.333-day period IS the full unit — 30.333 d ↔ 3600 A — and the number 30.33 never appears inside the A.B..C; it is the Earth-LTU length that FULL stands for, which is exactly why the same FULL converts to Mars (a 30-sol MoT = 2,663,257 s, one A = 739.8 s; a quarter reads 900.0000..0000 on either planet). Conversion to Earth LTU: seconds = (A + B/3600 + C/3600²) ÷ 3600 × 2,620,800 (ltuOfMotABC, round-trips within 1 s in the gate); e.g. +3 h = 10,800 s reads 14.3006..2136, and the whole is written as a duration 0000.00.30_08.00..00. Money rides the same fraction: $3,604.49 ÷ 43,680 min = $0.08252/min · $4.951/h · $118.83/D · $0.001375/s; per LTU unit (D 1 · W 7 · M 33 · Q 99 · Y 365): $831.81/W · $3,921.37/M33 · $11,764/Q99 · $43,373/Y365; at +3 h $14.85 is released and withdrawable. One A of this paycheck releases $3,604.49 ÷ 3600 = $1.0012 — for this deposit the A axis is, to a tenth of a percent, a dollar axis (a coincidence of the figure, not a law). The year is its own MoT: perihelion to perihelion (2026-01-03 17:15 UTC → 2027-01-03 02:33 UTC = 364.39 d, one A = 2.43 h); 2026-10-01 07:00 CST is day 270 · Q3 day 88 · 33-day frame 9 day 6 · orbit ≈ 2665.xxxx..xxxx; day 365 and the remaining fraction are DOWN (no quarter owns them). CST standard is only how a day is NAMED (UTC−6 fixed, tzFromLon's −6); the instants are universal.

**Chart.** axes: x = elapsed time INSIDE the deposit's MoT, 0 → 30.333 d, labelled by default in Earth LTU (0 · 7 d 14 h · 15 d 4 h · 22 d 18 h · 30 d 8 h) with A.B..C on reveal (0 · 900 · 1800 · 2700 · 3600) — behind the glass every x is the fraction; y = dollars 0 → the deposit amount ($3,604.49), and because one A of this paycheck is $1.0012 the reveal may print the y rail in A-dollars. A second, zoomed window (last 24 h, and "this hour") shares the same series so the NOW cursor is seen to move: at MoT scale one pixel is ~2 h and the line is a map; at one hour one pixel is ~10 s and the line is a pulse. · series: escrowed — falling hairline from the amount to 0 (C.intelligence, shipped) · released — rising normal stroke, amount ÷ MoT-minutes per minute (C.abundance, shipped) · withdrawable — rising from the +3 h hold, 0 before it (C.consciousness, shipped) · available — withdrawable minus withdrawn: the step-down line the person actually acts on (NOT drawn today; series() already computes it) · planned spend — the budget's expense $/min as a slope from the same origin (sum of Fixed + Variable ÷ M33-minutes), so released − planned − withdrawn is the sheet's Cash On Hand projection to the MoT's end (r.003) · $/D target ladder — the sheet's 144 → 138 → 137 as a stepped stroke with marks at 11 · 22 · 33 D (r.003) · marks: deposit instant at x = 0 with its CST stamp (and a FUNDED mark two days earlier for the 09/29 post) · the 3-hour hold tick (C.temporal, shipped) — the moment withdrawable leaves zero · NOW — a vertical hairline that must be a SAMPLE POINT of the series, not interpolated between 6-hour samples · every withdrawal as a tick with its amount, and a step down on the available line · the MoT end (FULL, 3600.3600..3600) where escrow reaches zero · calendar marks when the MoT crosses one: a 33-day frame edge, a 91-day quarter edge, day 365 (DOWN), the perihelion · the year ring beneath the chart: the orbit position (celestial A.B..C) drawn as an arc with the same stroke language — where in the year this money sits · states: pending — before the deposit instant: flat 0, "pending from <stamp>" with a countdown; escrow not yet funded → releasing · held — 0 to +3 h: released rises, withdrawable stays 0, the hold countdown ticks hh:mm:ss; a withdrawal is refused HOLD by name → releasing · withdrawable — after +3 h: released and withdrawable rise together, available steps down at each withdrawal; a withdrawal above available is refused INSUFFICIENT by name → released — fraction = 1: escrow 0, both lines flat at the amount, phase RECORD 웃; the record shows the chain held → stacked — two or more deposits releasing at once: the clock number is the SUM of rates and the chart must say "this deposit" or draw the sum, never disagree with the clock · cadence: One 1 Hz clock feeds `now` (shipped, null until mounted); the big released number moves every second at four decimals ($0.001375/s), the cent rolls every ~7.3 s, the hold countdown at 1 Hz, the polyline recomputed at 1 Hz from 120 samples plus the forced sample points (hold instant, NOW, each withdrawal, MoT end); the year position at 1 Hz (its B changes every 2.4 s). No network on the tick; the record is appended only on a press. · phone: At 390 px the ACTIVE clock block is the hero (the Session's big mono number, shipped), the chart sits under it full-width at viewBox 360×150 with five 10 px mono axis labels (≈ 60 px each — the ceiling for "22 d 18 h"), the year ring is a thin arc not a second chart, and the bottom strip carries released · available · phase · Withdraw (shipped). Strokes only on the app's card ground, one accent, the 13 colours, no fill and no gradient (vector-law gate). A person should be able to read three things in one glance: what has released, what they may take now, and where in the year they are.

**Invariants.**
- Money released is never more than money deposited, however long after. — `tests/financial-accrual.test.mjs (shipped: "released money never exceeds the deposit")`
- A withdrawal never makes the available balance negative; above what is withdrawable it is refused INSUFFICIENT by name. — `tests/financial-accrual.test.mjs (shipped)`
- The rate is amount ÷ MoT-minutes and nothing else — $3,604.49 over 30.333 days is $0.0825/min. — `tests/financial-mot.test.mjs + tests/financial-accrual.test.mjs (shipped)`
- 3 hours after the deposit the individual can withdraw; a minute earlier the money is visible but held (HOLD). — `tests/financial-accrual.test.mjs (shipped); scripts/financial-render.mjs assertSource holdHours === 3`
- Each transaction gets amount, deposit day and time, and MoT length of time — always the three fields, and the worked example accrues from them alone. — `tests/financial-accrual.test.mjs (FinTx shape, shipped)`
- The record is append-only and chain-hashed: an id is never written twice, a changed amount breaks the chain at that revision, and it replays to any revision. — `tests/financial-accrual.test.mjs (verify/tamper, shipped)`
- MoT = 30.33 converted to 3600.3600..3600: the start reads 0.0000..0000, the end FULL, the three Equal writings all read FULL, and A.B..C ↔ LTU round-trips within 1 s; a Martian MoT reads the same A.B..C. — `tests/financial-mot.test.mjs (shipped)`
- Day resets at perihelion, named in CST standard (UTC−6, no daylight shift); 91 days a quarter, 364 the grid, day 365 DOWN; frames 33 / 66 / 99. — `tests/financial-mot.test.mjs + tests/financial-crs.test.mjs (91 · 364 · 365; shipped)`
- Behind the scenes is A.B..C; the chart's glass defaults to day · hour · minute and A.B..C is a reveal. — `tests/financial-surface.test.mjs (axis regex, shipped)`
- The 9,999 HI token cap is the pod's YUG_CEILING, never a second ceiling on this surface. — `tests/financial-crs.test.mjs (shipped) — PROPOSED addition: fail on any literal 9999 in frontend/lib/financial-2525/`
- Personal first; the business frame is declared and tested only after feedback — no business surface exists before FIN-12 leaves draft. — `tests/financial-crs.test.mjs (tenant/business status, shipped) — PROPOSED addition: no components/financial-2525/business* while FIN-12 is draft`
- The UI/UX starts from the ◬ ♡ 웃 Session shell — imported, never redrawn, and the Session's own JSX untouched. — `tests/financial-surface.test.mjs (Session classes, r.002) + tests/pod-invariant.test.mjs`
- Figures are data, never invented: the worked example is read from the domain source and the ask file hashes byte for byte. — `tests/financial-surface.test.mjs (EXAMPLE from SRC) + tests/financial-crs.test.mjs (handoffSha256)`
- A new edition is an append, never an edit: revisions strictly increase, a release cites a real commit, generated views never drift from the one source. — `tests/financial-crs.test.mjs + scripts/financial-render.mjs --check (shipped)`
- Every word a person reads is in all 33 languages, staged never silent. — `tests/financial-i18n.test.mjs + tests/lexicon-coverage.test.mjs (shipped)`
- The chart and the clock never disagree: the hold instant, NOW and every withdrawal are sample points of the drawn series, and with stacked deposits the chart draws the same sum the clock prints or says "this deposit". — `PROPOSED tests/financial-chart.test.mjs (pure: series() sampled at holdUntilMs, now and each withdrawal; sum-vs-focus parity)`
- A recorded deposit is never lost to a full phone: an append that the device refuses is kept somewhere the reader is told about. — `PROPOSED in tests/financial-accrual.test.mjs (saveRecord under a throwing localStorage still preserves the entry — the AAR 2026-09-09 class)`

**Risks.**
- PERSISTENCE: r.002 exists only as thirteen modified, uncommitted files (JSON, ledger, command-ux1, pod-phase-rail, pod-phases, lexicon-data, domain.gen, financial-ledger.gen, three tests) — the exact class AAR 2026-08-28 names; a container reset erases the edition. The JSON's revision 0.002 cites d5d2b72, the addendum-8 ASK commit, and needs a real release commit once shipped.
- Chart geometry lies about the hold: series is sampled at len/120 = every 6 h on a 30-day MoT, so the +3 h step of withdrawable is interpolated across a 6 h segment and NOW is never a sample point; at 340 px the NOW cursor moves one pixel per ~2 h, so nothing on the chart is seen to move — only the number.
- Chart vs clock divergence: the chart draws the newest deposit, the clock prints the SUM of all releasing deposits; with two overlapping paychecks (or a business escrow plus a personal deposit) the two numbers disagree on one screen.
- Available is not drawn: the one line the person acts on (withdrawable − withdrawn) exists in series() but the chart shows withdrawals only as ticks.
- A full phone loses a deposit: saveRecord returns false on quota with no eviction and no fallback; the entry lives in React state until reload — the THREE DEAD ENDS class (AAR 2026-09-09) that the pod store already solved with evictOldest.
- Two perihelion tables now live in the repo (soi-calendar.ts seed 2024–2028 vs financial calendar.ts sourced 2024–2035) with three recorded disagreements (2024 00:39/00:38 · 2026 17:16/17:15 · 2028 12:11/12:28); the SoI calendar and the financial year can name a different day 1.
- CST-fixed vs the phone's CDT: on 2026-10-01 the surface's 07:00 is 08:00 on his phone; every stamp must say CST or the pilot's first feedback will be "the time is wrong".
- Posted 09/29 vs deposit 10/01 07:00: for two days the operator's bank shows the money while the surface shows $0 released and "pending" — a felt contradiction unless FUNDED is a visible mark.
- FIN-09's metric ("a review record exists") is unmet: docs/financial-2525/reviews/ does not exist, the JSON says PENDING, and the gate only checks that the record is NAMED — the twelve returns must be persisted first, with a hash.
- FIN-06 is marked implemented but SHEET_BUDGET is static seed data with no editing surface and withdrawals carry no category, so the sheet's Swings / Cash On Hand outcome cannot yet be drawn; the status overstates the DTM ("the operator's own lines").
- Device-only record keyed by Auth0 user.sub: phone and laptop diverge silently (FD-07 is honest about it, the glass must keep saying so); an unsigned visitor correctly sees only the example.
- Tokenization vocabulary: the RECORD phase carries 웃 and the Trinity fold reads 웃 M_33, while nothing mints — the surface must not imply a paycheck earns 웃 (the mint is currency-free).
- Sheet slips (47,620 / 4,762) are recorded not fixed — correct — but a reader of the rendered README may copy 4,762; the corrected figures should sit beside the slips wherever the slips are printed.
- Lexicon: 89 fin.* keys are staged in AFTER_FILL, not yet filled ×32 — the whole-app 33-language rule is met by staging today, not by translation.

### Sofia — multi-perspective analysis: the individual on the phone, the employer / escrow payer, the business, the auditor — what each of the four needs on the record, and where the record as written serves one and fails another.

**Readiness:** B- — enough to build a truthful personal RECORD pilot from (it was: v.000_r.001 shipped from these notes in one day with five gates green), not enough to build anything that moves money, witnesses a deposit, or keeps the 91-day/day-365 calendar honest every year.

**One sentence.** The notes are enough to build a truthful personal RECORD of one man's paycheck as time — and they did, in a day, with five gates — but through four pairs of eyes the record still lacks its witness, its survival on a full phone, and a calendar that admits the orbit does not deliver day 365 every year; close those before a second person, a business, or a single 웃 touches it.

**Ambiguities → declared default.**
- **Which reading of A.B..C is the MoT law — the elapsed FRACTION of one whole (FD-01), or lib/abc-3600.ts's native tokenomics grammar where A is hours?** → Fraction of ONE WHOLE (FD-01): start 0.0000..0000, completion 3600.3600..3600. On the reveal, print the words 'of MoT' beside the A.B..C so 14.3006..2136 is never read as 14 hours. — _It is the only reading under which the operator's sentence is true: in the hours grammar 30.333 days is 728.0000..0000 and 3600.3600..3600 is 3,601 h = 150.04 days. Both grammars print through the same fmtABC, so the unit must be on the glass._
- **Which 'month' is a MoT — 30.333 (the paycheck example, = 91 ÷ 3 = soi91.monthDays), 33 (sheet 3's M and the 11/22/33 iconology), or the Gregorian month the bank actually pays on?** → No global month. The MoT is a field on each transaction (the operator's third field); the deposit form defaults to 30.333 as he wrote it; the ANALYSIS frames are 33 / 66 / 99 days (FD-04). — _He gave both numbers in different roles — pay LENGTH vs analysis FRAME — and the record shape already carries the length per deposit, so no default has to be invented._
- **A monthly paycheck with a fixed 30.333-day MoT leaves a 16-hour gap ($0/min) from 2026.10.31_15.00 to 2026.11.01_07.00 CST and an 8-hour OVERLAP (double rate) on Dec 1, a 2.3-day overlap in March. Intended?** → Yes — each deposit is independent and linear from its own instant (FD-05); the surface NAMES the states 'between MoTs' and 'overlapping' rather than showing $0.0000/min or 2× silently. — _The alternative (MoT = until the next deposit) makes A.B..C depend on a future event, which breaks the universal scale the operator asked for; honesty on the glass is the fix, not a different law._
- **Does the 3-hour hold run from the deposit day-and-time the operator wrote (Oct 1 07:00 CST) or from the bank's posted/pending date (Sep 29)?** → From the deposit stamp: the hold lifts at 2026.10.01_10.00..00 CST (FD-03); the posted date is a memo on the record. — _His sentence '3 hrs later individual can withdraw' follows 'Deposit day and time' directly; the bank's posting is the payer's evidence, not the clock._
- **Is the hold once per deposit, or does every released minute wait its own 3 hours?** → Once per deposit: at +3 h everything released so far ($14.85) becomes withdrawable at once, and thereafter released and withdrawable coincide (FD-03 as shipped). — _'3 hrs later' names one event; a rolling hold would make withdrawable a second curve nobody asked for and the payer's stake harder to read._
- **What is 'escrow' in the personal pilot, where the person types their own deposit and no payer exists on the record?** → A self-recorded, labelled claim — 'a record of what a person typed, never a bank' — whose only witness is the bank's posted date and memo; a counter-party (the employer's message, a bank export) becomes the witness in the business frame (FIN-03 / FIN-04 stretch). — _The POD's own law is 'witnessed, not claimed'; the personal pilot cannot witness a deposit, so the surface must say so rather than let escrow states look like a rail._
- **'day resets at perihelion … CST standard': are financial days 24-hour periods from the perihelion INSTANT (11:15 CST on 2026-01-03) or CST civil days rolling at midnight?** → From the instant (as shipped in calendar.ts, 'CST-independent'); CST only NAMES the day (FD-02). The year panel prints the boundary time once so a person is not surprised that Q2 opens mid-morning. — _Instants are universal — the Mars conversion and the year's exact A.B..C depend on it; civil days are a business-frame concern and 'test later'._
- **'91 days for a quarter with down day 365': the perihelion-to-perihelion year runs 363.24 d (FY2028 — no day 365 at all, Q4 loses its last 18 h) to 367.54 d (FY2024 — 3.5 down days) because the perihelion instant jitters ±1.5 days. Which is the year?** → Perihelion-to-perihelion, the operator's 'resets everywhere at perihelion'; the down period is 'day 365 to the next perihelion' and may be none or several days, shown with its true length; the business 4-4-5 grid (test later) anchors to a smoothed year (mean anomalistic 365.2596 d) or a civil rule, declared then. — _The orbit is the truth and the grid is a name; hiding a −0.76-day down period behind the word 'day 365' would be a silent lie on the calendar, and the shipped test already records 2026 as 364.39 days._
- **Two perihelion tables now exist — lib/soi-calendar.ts PERIHELION_UTC (2024–2028 seed) and lib/financial-2525/calendar.ts PERIHELION (2024–2035 sourced) — disagreeing on 2024 (00:39 / 00:38), 2026 (17:16 / 17:15) and 2028 (12:11 / 12:28). Which is the one source?** → The sourced twelve-year table with provenance; soi-calendar.ts reads from it or a gate asserts they agree. — _Three copies drifting is defect 27's class; a financial year that starts a minute apart on two surfaces is the same drift in money._
- **'each user gets their own log in': is the record per login across devices, or per login on this device only?** → Per login on the device (key exel-fin:<sub>), cloud isolation deferred until the app's login is wired into Supabase, said on the glass in the person's words (FD-07). — _Honest and shipped; the only correction is the wording — the person must not read a vendor's name (see the signer-voice risk)._
- **What does a withdrawal DO in the pilot?** → It records an intent and reduces the available balance; nothing moves; the surface says 'recorded', never 'sent'. — _'never a bank' — a rail before a witness would let a typed deposit become real money._
- **Several sheet-1 lines are glyph-only: 'LSU 3600 – 3600.3600 · 3', 'Unlock 🔒 $/min System · Baseline: $/Day', 'Idea + | • · – | •• · ∞ | •••', 'Analysis: Backward · MoT ↔ MoT', '◬ = ATX = AI', 'Charge to 2026_01_11–13.11..11'. Requirements?** → Carried verbatim in the ask; NOT requirements and no CRS row until the operator glosses them. — _A FIN row must be 'a statement a reader can pass or fail' (the crs gate's own rule); a line nobody can pass or fail cannot be gated._
- **How does money become 웃 — sheet 1 says '웃 = × Min Wage', pod-yug says 웃 = M × hours and $ = 웃 × stamped rate. Is a paycheck converted at $ ÷ the stamped local minimum wage?** → Declared only (FIN-10 draft), not wired until the personal framework has feedback; when wired it is the inverse of pod-yug settle() at a STAMPED rate, and 9,999 is YUG_CEILING on PAYOUT per year, never a second constant. — _Addendum 4 asks for tracking under the cap; addendum 5 says personal feedback first; derived on his figures the paycheck is ≈497 웃 per MoT at the Texas floor, ≈5,966 웃 a year — under the cap, so nothing binds in the pilot._
- **Addendum 9 puts HI at the top of the Trinity wheel, AI tokens bottom-left, SI minutes bottom-right; sheet 1 maps ♡ → M_LTU, 웃 → M_33, ◬ → M_SS, and r.002's folded Trinity is labelled [M_SS, M_33, M_LTU] with glyphs [◬, 웃, ♡]. Which glyph sits where?** → 웃 (HI) top, ◬ (AI tokens) bottom-left, ♡ (SI minutes contribution) bottom-right, each carrying its sheet-1 M_* label; recorded as a new FD before r.002 ships. — _Addendum 9 is the later and more specific word and arrived during this review (HEAD e7b257a); it is not yet in the JSON's decisions, so r.002's wheel may contradict it._
- **Is the sheets' 11-day example range (2026.01.11_13.11..11 → 2026.01.22_13.11..11) a second worked example?** → Yes, as a gate case only — an 11-day MoT with the stamp round-trip and 0 → 3600.3600..3600 — no surface seed. — _It is the operator's own data and the 11/22/33 iconology, costs one test line, and proves the law is not tuned to 30.333._

**MoT law, as this lens read it.** A MoT is ONE WHOLE — here the pay period. A.B..C is the elapsed FRACTION of that whole in Base-3600, exactly the celestial UCRS-2525 convention (HU 0 = perihelion, HU 3600 = one orbit = FULL_ORBIT '3600.3600..3600'): A = 3600ths of the whole (0..3600), B = 3600ths of an A, C = 3600ths of a B, so a position's value is (A + B/3600 + C/3600²) ÷ 3600 of the whole. For the pay MoT of 30.333 days (91 ÷ 3, which is already soi91.monthDays): the whole is 728 h = 43,680 min = 2,620,800 s, so ONE A = 728 s (12 min 8 s), ONE B = 0.2022 s, ONE C = 56.2 µs. Computed from lib/financial-2525/mot.ts: start 0.0000..0000 · +1 min 0.0296..2532 · +1 h 4.3402..0712 · the 3-hour hold lifts at 14.3006..2136 · +1 day 118.2452..2690 · +7 d 830.2769..0831 · half-way (15 d 4 h) 1800.0000..0000 · +30 d 3560.1582..1503 · the end (30 d 8 h, 2026.10.31_15.00..00 CST) 3600.3600..3600 — which the sheet's 'Equal' makes identical to 3600.0000..0000 and 3599.3599..3599 (isFull). So '30.33 converts to 3600.3600..3600' means: 30.33 days is the whole's LENGTH in Earth LTU, and every whole — 30.333 d, 14 d, 33 d, a 30-sol Martian period — reads 3600.3600..3600 at its own completion; the scale never names a planet. Converting back to Earth LTU multiplies the fraction by the whole's LTU length: seconds = fraction × 2,620,800 (gated to round-trip within 1 s at every tested instant); then D · W · M · Q · Y through the sheet's 1 · 7 · 33 · 99 · 365 days and h · min · s through 24 · 60 · 60; a stamp reads YYYY.MM.DD_HH.MM..SS in CST standard, UTC−6 with no daylight shift (2026.10.01_07.00..00 = 13:00 UTC), and a duration 0000.00.DD_HH.MM..SS (30.333 d = 0000.00.30_08.00..00). Money rides the same fraction: released = $3,604.49 × fraction = $0.0825/min = $4.95/h = $118.83/day = $0.001375/s, and one A of THIS MoT releases $1.0012 (3,604.49 ÷ 3,600) — by the operator's own figure a pay-A is a dollar, a natural tick for the chart. What the law is NOT: lib/abc-3600.ts's native tokenomics grammar (A = hours) would write 30.333 d as 728.0000..0000 and read 3600.3600..3600 as 3,601 h = 150.04 days, so the operator's sentence is true only under the fraction-of-whole reading (FD-01); because both grammars print through the same fmtABC, the revealed A.B..C must carry the words 'of MoT'.

**Chart.** axes: x = time inside the current MoT, 0 → the whole, labelled by default in Earth LTU (0 · 7 d 14 h · 15 d 4 h · 22 d 18 h · 30 d 8 h) and in A.B..C (0 · 900 · 1800 · 2700 · 3600 'of MoT') only on reveal; y = dollars 0 → the deposit amount, the live $/min being the slope. Because the 3-hour hold sits at 0.41% of a 30-day axis (1.4 px on a 360-px chart) the chart needs TWO x-scales: 'first day' (0 → 24 h, hold at one eighth) until the hold lifts and the first day passes, then 'whole MoT'; the surface picks by phase, the person can toggle. · series: released = amount × fraction — what the escrow has let go (abundance stroke) · available = withdrawable − withdrawn — the individual's ONE number, what I can take now; primary stroke, stepping down at each withdrawal (r.001 draws withdrawable, not available) · escrowed = amount − released — the payer's remaining stake (hairline) · budget burn = expenses $/min × elapsed (sheet 3: 3,400 per 33 d ≈ 7.16 ¢/min) so cash-on-hand = released − withdrawn − burn is on the chart — the sheet's 'Outcome: Cash On Hand' and its ± 'Swings' step per day · live $/min — summed over overlapping deposits — shown as the ticking number, not a line · marks: NOW as a vertical hairline · the 3-hour hold at +3 h (temporal colour) with the h:mm:ss countdown until it lifts · each withdrawal as a tick at its stamp (evolution colour) with its amount · the MoT end (RECORD) and, when declared, the next expected deposit — so a gap or overlap is visible before it happens · the latest entry's 8-character hash as the receipt (the payer's and the auditor's proof) · 33 / 66 / 99-day frame boundaries in the frame view · the year: day N of the financial year, quarter, the orbit's A.B..C, and the down period's TRUE length (including 'none' in FY2028) · states: PENDING — before the deposit stamp: dashed outline, nothing released, 'from 2026.10.01_07.00..00' → HELD — 0 → 3 h: released climbs, available flat at $0, countdown running → RELEASING — 3 h → end: released and available coincide until a withdrawal → WITHDRAWN — available steps down at the withdrawal's stamp; released unchanged → RECORD — at 3600.3600..3600: escrow $0, available = amount − withdrawn → BETWEEN MoTs — no deposit releasing: '$0/min · between pay periods' named, balance held (the 16-h gap Oct 31 15:00 → Nov 1 07:00 CST) → OVERLAPPING — two deposits releasing: rate = the sum, both drawn → REFUSED — a withdrawal refused HOLD / INSUFFICIENT / AMOUNT by name; nothing recorded → UNSAVED — the device would not keep the entry: it stays on screen with a 'take the file' action, never silently dropped → CHAIN BROKEN — the stored record failed verify(): shown empty and said in the person's words, never repaired silently · cadence: The big number ticks at 1 Hz (the operator's 'real-time'; at $0.001375/s the cents change every ~7 s). The polyline re-samples once a minute (120 samples across the MoT; NOW moves 0.00013 px/s on a 30-day axis, so a faster redraw costs battery for nothing — today MotChart recomputes series() on every 1-Hz render). series() is pure, so a replay at any instant draws the same picture. · phone: At 390 px: the number first, the chart second, the ladder third; the phase in one look on the Session's rail (DEPOSIT ◬ · HOLD ♡ · RELEASE ♡ · WITHDRAW 웃 · RECORD 웃) and on the phone strip (released · available · phase · Withdraw, 44-px targets); strokes only on the card, the 13 colours, no fill; the hold mark must be visible, hence the first-day scale; A.B..C is a toggle, never the default; no word on the glass names a database, a vendor or a browser store.

**Invariants.**

- The rate is amount ÷ MoT-minutes and nothing else; money released is never more than money deposited. — `frontend/tests/financial-accrual.test.mjs (FIN-04) — shipped`
- Released money is visible at once and withdrawable only from 3 hours after the deposit; a withdrawal never makes the balance negative and is refused by name (HOLD · INSUFFICIENT · AMOUNT). — `frontend/tests/financial-accrual.test.mjs §2 — shipped`
- A MoT starts at 0.0000..0000 and ends at 3600.3600..3600; the three Equal writings all read FULL; A.B..C ↔ Earth LTU round-trips within 1 s; a 30-sol Martian MoT reads the same position. — `frontend/tests/financial-mot.test.mjs (FIN-01, FIN-13) — shipped`
- Behind the scenes is A.B..C; the glass reads day · hour · minute by default and A.B..C only on reveal, and the reveal says 'of MoT'. — `frontend/tests/financial-surface.test.mjs (addendum-8 line, in the r.002 working tree) — the 'of MoT' label is a proposed addition to the same gate`

**Risks.**
- The orbit does not honour the grid: perihelion-to-perihelion years run 363.24 d (FY2028 — no day 365, Q4 truncated by ~18 h) to 367.54 d (FY2024 — 3.5 down days); the notes' '4 × 91 + down day 365' is a name, not a count, and the business 4-4-5 frame will break on it unless anchored to a smoothed year.
- Two perihelion tables disagree (2024, 2026, 2028) between lib/soi-calendar.ts and lib/financial-2525/calendar.ts — defect 27's class, now in the calendar money is counted on.
- A recorded deposit can be lost on a full or private phone: record.ts saveRecord tries once, no eviction, no retry, no take-the-file; the AAR 2026-09-09 'three dead ends' class, this time with money.
- Machine words on the person's glass: fin.device_only names Supabase; 'chain' and 'hash' read as the machine's; the signer-voice law is not extended to fin.* and its gate would fail on the shipped string.
- Every deposit is an unwitnessed, self-typed claim; the escrow → release → withdraw states simulate a payer who does not exist on the record — right for a pilot that says so, fatal if a 웃 conversion or any rail is wired before a payer or bank export witnesses the deposit.
- A.B..C has two readings that print identically: the 웃 tokenomics grammar (A = hours) and the MoT grammar (A = 3600ths of the whole); without 'of MoT' on the reveal, 14.3006..2136 reads as 14 hours.
- Fixed 30.333-day MoTs against Gregorian-monthly pay produce a 16-h $0/min gap and 8-h to 2.3-day double-rate overlaps every month, shown silently today.
- The 3-hour hold mark — the individual's most important mark — is 1.4 px from the axis at phone width on a whole-MoT scale.
- The chart draws withdrawable, not available, and no budget burn: the sheet's outcome 'Cash On Hand' and its Swings are not on the chart.
- The ask file is a living document: four hashes in one day (5880… → 7dc5… → fe47… → d2d9…), addendum 9 landed during this review; r.001 shipped against 7dc5…; the operator's 'hash he can check' moves under him — handoffHistory must stay the auditor's index and every edition must be committed before it is cited.
- r.002 is uncommitted across 13 files with a 348-line reworked surface while r.001's twelve-lens review runs, and the JSON already reads revision 0.002 — 'one commit → verify → next' is stretched; the ledger's rev 1 quotes an earlier edition's hash (correct as an append, confusing to a reader).
- FIN-09's review record file does not exist; the row is 'approved' with its metric unmet.
- The ×32 lexicon fill for 89 fin.* keys is owed: 32 languages read English on the pilot.
- Addendum 9's Trinity orientation (HI top, AI tokens bottom-left, SI minutes bottom-right) is not yet a decision on the record and r.002's folded Trinity order may contradict it.
- Derived on the operator's own figures: ≈497 웃 per MoT and ≈5,966 웃 a year at the Texas floor, under the 9,999 cap — nothing binds in the pilot, so any cap logic shipped now is untestable on real data.
- MotChart recomputes series() (121 balanceAt calls) on every 1-Hz render — harmless CPU today, a battery smell on a phone left open all day.

SHIPPED (financial-mot 41 · financial-accrual 28). ### Thoth — data and analytics deep dive: every figure on the three sheets, in the messages, in the bank screen and in the shipped r.001/r.002 laws recomputed and cross-checked (read-only; no file written)

**Readiness:** B

**One sentence.** The notes are arithmetically sound where it counts — every rate on the sheets reproduces from 144 $/D = 0.1 $/min except two propagated slips, and the shipped r.001/r.002 laws compute the operator's $3,604.49 paycheck to the cent ($118.83/day · $4.95/h · $0.0825/min · $0.001375/s · $14.85 at the 3-hour hold) — but the calendar's “day 365 down day” is a 365.27-day average that fails three of eleven sourced years, the glass and the gate carry two MoTs 28.8 s apart, the chart samples past its own hold, and the escrow law against the operator's own budget releases his fixed bills only by day 23, so revision 0.003 must persist this round, pin one MoT, gate every year's length and resample the chart before the personal framework is handed to anyone for feedback — and the business frame stays “test later”, as he said.

Per ELAPSED hour the same paycheck is $4.95/h = 0.683 × the floor — the two bases must never be blended (pod-clock k18).

**Ambiguities → declared default.**
- **Is the pay MoT the typed decimal 30.333 or 91 ÷ 3 exactly? The domain example (SRC.example.motDays = 30.333, what the glass computes) and the code constant MOT_PAY_MONTH_DAYS = 91/3 (what the gate computes) differ.** → The record stores what the person typed — 30.333 — per the domain's own disclaimer (“a RECORD of what a person typed”); the “⅓ quarter” preset fills the string 30.333, and MOT_PAY_MONTH_DAYS becomes 30.333 so glass and gate agree byte-for-byte; 91 ÷ 3 is printed as the derivation note. — _Today the surface shows 8.25213 ¢/min and the gate proves 8.25204 ¢/min with period ends 28.8 s apart; financial-mot's 1e-3 tolerance cannot see it. One number, or two truths._
- **Which “month”: M33 (sheet analysis unit, LTU_DAYS.M) or 30.333 (the pay MoT, one third of Q91)?** → Both, always named: M33 is the analysis unit for the ladder ($/M33), 30.333 is a pay MoT; the glass never prints a bare “month” or “/mo”. — _The sheet itself carries both (4,752 $/M33 beside frames of 30.3/60.6/90.9); collapsing them silently changes every per-month figure by 8.8 %._
- **Which “quarter”: Q91 (calendar, 4 × 91 = 364, the operator's first sentence) or Q99 (analysis, 3 × 33, sheet 3)?** → Q91 positions the year (quarter, dayInQuarter/91 — as built); Q99 exists only as the 3-frame analysis window and is always printed with its day count (“99 D”). — _4 × 99 = 396 ≠ 365; a person reading “Q” without the number will be told two different quarter boundaries._
- **When does a Financial-2525 day roll: at the perihelion instant's clock time (11:15 CST in FY2026, 20:33 CST FY2027, 06:28 CST FY2028) as built, or at CST midnight?** → At the perihelion instant (as built), and the year panel prints the roll time. — _The sheet's own 11-day range runs 13:11:11 → 13:11:11 (instant-aligned), every day stays exactly 1,440 min so 144 $/D = 0.1 $/min holds to the digit, and CST standard has no DST anyway; but an unprinted 11:15 roll will read as a broken day counter._
- **The 3-hour hold: 3 h after the deposit (FD-03, as built) or a rolling 3-hour lag on each released cent?** → 3 h after the deposit instant. — _“3 hrs later individual can withdraw” names one event; the rolling reading changes only which $14.85 is visible-but-held at any moment and doubles the chart's states. Revisit when business pays two-week blocks._
- **A business “2 weeks” deposit: 14 days (the message; the gate's dep2) or 16.5 days (the sheet's 2×/mth = 0000.00.16_12.00..00 = 33 ÷ 2)?** → No hidden default: presets 14 · 16.5 · 30.333 · 33, and the record stores the one chosen. — _Both figures are the operator's own; 14 vs 16.5 moves the rate by 17.9 %._
- **Which income line is the pilot's? Three appear: the bank's $3,604.49 per 30.333 d ($118.83/D), the sheet budget's Income 3,200 F per M33 ($96.97/D), and the sheet ladder's 144 $/D (4,752/M33).** → The bank figure is the pilot's Income line; the sheet's two figures stay on the surface only as labelled seed examples (“sheet”), never beside the real paycheck as if they were his budget. — _Figures are data, never invented: the paycheck is real; the sheet budget nets −$200 per M33 and, shown as his, would say the operator runs a deficit he never stated._
- **Does the 2026-10-01 07:00 deposit cover the month before (arrears) or after (advance)? The bank posted it 09/29.** → The MoT runs forward from the typed deposit instant (FD-05); the 09/29 posted date is memo. — _Escrow “releases” forward by definition and the operator typed 10-01 07:00 as the start; but see the bills risk — this is the single largest consequence of the law._
- **What is A in A.B..C for a MoT: 3600ths of the whole period (FD-01, shipped), days (30.333 d = 30.1198..2880; 91/3 = 30.1200..0000), or hours as abc-3600 documents for 웃 (727.3571..0720; 91/3 = 728.0000..0000)?** → 3600ths of the whole (shipped). The day- and hour-based scalars are printed on the A.B..C reveal as derived lengths, not as the position. — _Only the position reading makes “30.33 converted to 3600.3600..3600” literally true and mirrors FULL_ORBIT (HU 0 = perihelion, 3600 = full orbit); 728.0000..0000 is a pleasing identity (728 = 8 × 91) but it names a length, and the operator's sentence names a whole._
- **What happens when an orbit-year is SHORTER than the 364-day grid — FY2028 (363.24 d), FY2031 (363.35 d), FY2034 (363.84 d) — so Q4's day 91 is cut by 18.25 h / 15.62 h / 3.88 h and there is no day 365 at all?** → The perihelion instant wins (“day resets everywhere at perihelion”): the year flips, Q4 is short, and the year panel badges “SHORT YEAR −18.25 h”; the down gap is a signed, variable figure printed for every year, never a silent absorption into Q4 or Q1. — _The operator's primary law is the reset; 91-day quarters are secondary. The mean interval is 365.27 d but individual years swing 363.24–367.54 (the Earth–Moon barycentre wobble), so “day 365 is the down day” is true on average and false in particular._
- **The sheet's “Δ” swings and “60 D”: is the 60-day horizon a second analysis window, and do the ÷24/÷6 column headers apply to both rows (they only reproduce row 1)?** → Row 2 is recorded as 4,762 ÷ 60 then ÷ 6 (the sheet's own arithmetic); the ÷24/÷6 headers are row 1's; no 60-day frame is built until the operator confirms it. — _79.36 cannot be derived from ÷24 (4,762 ÷ 24 = 198.4); inventing a rule that fits both rows would be making up math._
- **How precise should the live number be? At 8.252 ¢/min a cent changes every 7.27 s, so the 2-decimal big number stands still for seven ticks of the 1 Hz clock.** → Keep the big number to the cent (the figure a bank would show) and tick the ¢/s digits (4 decimals of $, already computed as usd4) beside it every second. — _“Real-time” that does not move reads as broken; a number that moves in fractions of a cent reads as a meter, which is what the sheet drew._
- **The $/sec display: usd4 prints $0.0014 for $0.001375 (+1.8 %); the sheet's own “0.0016 s” is the same truncation of 0.001667.** → Print $/s with 6 decimals, or as ¢/s (0.1375 ¢/s). — _A rate off by 1.8 % on the glass while the gate proves it to 6 places is two truths again._

**MoT law, as this lens read it.** A MoT (Measure of Time) is ONE WHOLE — the pay period, the budget month, the financial year — and A.B..C is a POSITION inside that whole, uniform Base-3600 at every level (the celestial UCRS-2525 convention: HU 0 = perihelion, 3600 = a full orbit written 3600.3600..3600). A = 3600ths of the MoT (0 at the deposit, 3600 at completion), B = 3600ths of an A, C = 3600ths of a B. So for the operator's pay period of 30.333 days: the whole is 30.333 × 86,400 = 2,620,771.2 s (91 ÷ 3 exactly: 2,620,800 s); one A = 727.992 s = 12 min 8.0 s (91 ÷ 3: exactly 728.000 s — 728 = 8 × 91); one B = 0.20222 s; one C = 56.17 µs. The deposit instant reads 0.0000..0000; +1 s reads 0.0004..3402; +1 min reads 0.0296..2532; +3 h (the hold) reads 14.3006..2136 (30.333: 14.3007..0649); +1 day reads 118.2452..2690; half the period reads 1800.0000..0000; the end reads 3600.3600..3600. “30.33 converts to 3600.3600..3600” therefore means: the pay period IS the orbit — the deposit is its perihelion and the period's completion is its full orbit; 30.33 is the LTU length of the whole, 3600.3600..3600 is the whole's position at completion. The sheet's three writings 3600.0000..0000 · 3600.3600..3600 · 3599.3599..3599 are “Equal” as the boundary (under plain abc-3600 arithmetic they are 3600, 3601.000278 and 3599.99999992), so FULL is a sentinel exactly like FULL_UNIT_ABC / FULL_ORBIT, and mot.ts isFull() carries it. Earth LTU conversion is ucrsAt's own rule (ltu = orbit fraction × period seconds): seconds = (A + B/3600 + C/3600²) ÷ 3600 × motSec (ltuOfMotABC); minutes = s ÷ 60; hours = s ÷ 3,600; D = s ÷ 86,400; W = D ÷ 7; M33 = D ÷ 33 (analysis) or D ÷ 30.333 (pay); Q = D ÷ 91 (calendar) or D ÷ 99 (analysis); Y = D ÷ 365 (analysis; the orbit-year itself is 363.24–367.54 d). Money rides the same fraction: released(t) = amount × fraction; $/min = amount ÷ (days × 1,440) — spreadPerMin(total, days) verbatim from lib/soi-calendar.ts — so $3,604.49 ÷ 43,679.52 min = $0.0825213/min, × 60 = $4.95128/h, × 1,440 = $118.83/day, ÷ 60 = $0.001375/s (91 ÷ 3: $0.0825204/min, $4.95122/h, $118.8293/day). Mars and later planets change only motSec (a 30-sol MoT = 30 × 88,775.244 s): a quarter of it reads 900.0000..0000 identically — the scale never names a planet (gated in financial-mot). The rejected readings, kept for the reveal as derived scalars: A = days gives 30.1198..2880 (30.333) or 30.1200..0000 (91 ÷ 3); A = hours (abc-3600's whole for 웃) gives 727.3571..0720 or 728.0000..0000. Timestamps (2026.10.01_07.00..00) and durations (0000.00.33_00.00..00) are the sheet's calendar grammar, base-60 clock fields with the double dot before seconds — a different grammar from A.B..C that happens to look alike; the code keeps them apart (fmtStampCST / fmtDurationDays vs motABC).

**Chart.** axes: x = elapsed time inside the focused deposit's MoT, from the deposit instant to deposit + MoT, labelled at 0 · ¼ · ½ · ¾ · 1 in Earth LTU by default (“7 d 14 h”, “15 d 4 h”, …; “3 h 0 min” under a day) and 0 · 900 · 1800 · 2700 · 3600 on the A.B..C reveal (shipped). y = DOLLARS, 0 → the deposit amount, with tick labels at 0 · ¼ · ½ · ¾ · amount ($0 · $901.12 · $1,802.25 · $2,703.37 · $3,604.49) — the shipped chart normalises to a 0..1 fraction and prints no y labels, so $14.85 at +3 h is a 0.4 % rise nobody can read; absolute dollars fix that. · series: released — the ramp 0 → amount at $/min (abundance stroke, normal width): the escrow's release, monotonic, clamped at the amount · withdrawable — 0 until +3 h, then equal to released (consciousness stroke): what the hold has let go · available — withdrawable minus every withdrawal so far (the line the person acts on; stepped down at each withdrawal; NOT yet drawn — the number exists in balanceAt) · escrowed — the mirror ramp amount → 0 (hairline, intelligence stroke) · cash on hand — available minus the budget burn since the deposit at the budget's own $/min (the sheet's stated Outcome and its “Swings” + / − step chart; not yet built — it is the only series that turns the paycheck and the budget into one picture) · the live rate — printed, not drawn: $/min · $/h · $/day · $/s (ticking), with the A.B..C position on the reveal · marks: the 3-hour hold: a tick at x(deposit + 3 h) (temporal colour) — and, once sampling is fixed, the point where the withdrawable line first rises · NOW: a full-height hairline at x(now) while inside the window (shipped) · one tick per withdrawal at its instant, from the top edge (evolution colour, shipped); tap → the record row · the day-roll ticks every 24 h from the deposit (so a person sees why $118.83 is a day), the 33-day frame boundary, and the perihelion / day-365 line whenever it falls inside the window · the deposit stamp, amount and MoT as the chart's subtitle (shipped: 2026.10.01_07.00..00 · $3,604.49 · 30.333) · states: PENDING — before the deposit instant: everything in escrow, released 0, nothing withdrawable; the guide says “pending from <stamp>” → HELD — 0 → +3 h: released rises (visible, $14.85 by the end of it) but withdrawable stays 0; the guide shows the countdown to the hold (shipped as hhmmss) → RELEASING — +3 h → MoT end: released ramps, withdrawable = released, available = withdrawable − withdrawn; Withdraw is the person's turn → RELEASED — at and after the MoT end: escrow 0, released = amount, the A.B..C reads 3600.3600..3600; the phase rail lands on RECORD → WITHDRAWN — a count and a stepped-down available line; every refusal by name: HOLD (before 3 h), INSUFFICIENT (above available), AMOUNT (zero), a malformed stamp → SHORT YEAR / DOWN DAY — a badge on the year panel when the window crosses a perihelion (FY2028/2031/2034 short; FY2025's down day lasts 3.8 h) · cadence: The number: 1 Hz (shipped setInterval; null until mounted). At 8.252 ¢/min one cent lands every 7.27 s, so the ¢/s digits (already computed) must tick beside the 2-decimal figure or seven consecutive ticks show the same value. The polyline: resample only when the record changes and at most once per minute — on a 340 px-wide 30-day window the ramp moves under one pixel per 6 hours, so re-running series() (120 × balanceAt) every second buys nothing. Sampling: the shipped step is MoT ÷ 120 = 6.067 h, so the withdrawable line first rises at sample 1 = 6.07 h while the hold mark stands at 3 h; sample hourly (728 points) and add explicit samples at the deposit instant, +3 h, each withdrawal instant and now, so every mark sits on a vertex. · phone: Verified at 375 px: the 360 × 150 viewBox at width 100 % inside max-w-3xl px-4 leaves the 16 px gutter with no horizontal scroll; the big number is text-2xl mono tabular (fits “$3,604.49”); the six-item legend wraps to two rows — acceptable; the phone strip (fixed, bottom-14, sm:hidden) carries released · available · phase · Withdraw, which is the whole state in one glance. Two fixes: the A.B..C toggle is px-2 py-1 text-xs (under the 44 px target every other Session control keeps), and the axis labels are text-[10px] — legible but at the floor; the y-axis dollar ticks should be 11 px mono like the subtitle.

**Invariants.**
- Money released is never more than money deposited, and escrow + released = deposited to the cent, at every instant. — `tests/financial-accrual.test.mjs (shipped: clamp at the amount; escrowed = deposited − released)`
- A withdrawal never makes what is available negative, and a refusal always says why: HOLD · INSUFFICIENT · AMOUNT. — `tests/financial-accrual.test.mjs (shipped) — extend: a record with an over-withdrawal appended from elsewhere is reported by name, not hidden by max(0, …)`
- The rate is the amount divided by the MoT's minutes and nothing else — and the glass and the gate use the same MoT to the byte. — `tests/financial-mot.test.mjs — ADD: SRC.example.motDays === MOT_PAY_MONTH_DAYS (strict equality; the present 1e-3 tolerance hides a 28.8 s / 9.1e-7 $/min drift)`
- A transaction is three fields — amount, deposit day and time, MoT — and once recorded it is on the record for good; a correction is a new entry that names what it corrects. — `tests/financial-accrual.test.mjs (shipped: chain verify() names brokenAt; append is idempotent by id) — ADD: two deposits with equal amount and stamp are both kept (ids must not collide)`
- The year resets at the perihelion instant; every year in the table prints its length and its down gap, and a year shorter than 364 days is badged SHORT, never hidden. — `tests/financial-mot.test.mjs — ADD: loop every consecutive PERIHELION pair, assert positionInYear flips at the instant, print length and gap, and assert FY2028 / FY2031 / FY2034 carry the SHORT badge`
- A day is 1,440 minutes everywhere on this surface, so 144 $/D = 0.1 $/min holds exactly and no daylight shift ever enters the arithmetic. — `tests/financial-mot.test.mjs (shipped: MIN_PER_DAY = 1440; CST_OFFSET_MIN fixed at −360) + a source grep that no Date local-time getter (getHours, getTimezoneOffset) appears in lib/financial-2525`
- Elapsed minutes are never worked hours: the $/min economy and 웃 = M × hours are printed under their own names and never blended (the paycheck is 0.683 × the floor per elapsed hour and 2.868 × per worked hour). — `tests/financial-surface.test.mjs — ADD: no 웃 figure on the surface is derived from ratePerMinCents; FIN-10 stays draft until the mint's hours have a witness`
- Behind the glass is A.B..C; day · hour · minute is the default and A.B..C is a reveal. — `tests/financial-surface.test.mjs (shipped: useState(false) toggle; ltuLabel default)`
- The three writings 3600.0000..0000 · 3600.3600..3600 · 3599.3599..3599 read FULL, and nothing short of them does. — `tests/financial-mot.test.mjs (shipped: isFull, and 3599.3599..3598 is not full)`
- Every rate printed on the glass is within 0.1 % of the pure law's value — the $/s figure included. — `tests/financial-surface.test.mjs — ADD: per_sec is formatted with ≥ 6 decimals or as ¢/s (usd4 prints $0.0014 for $0.001375, +1.8 %)`
- The 3-hour hold on the chart is exactly where the withdrawable line first rises. — `tests/financial-accrual.test.mjs — ADD: series() with explicit samples at deposit, +3 h, each withdrawal and now; assert withdrawable = 0 at (+3 h − 1 ms) and > 0 at +3 h`
- A new edition is an append: revisions strictly increase, the ledger's last rev equals project.revision, every release cites a real commit, and the round's twelve-lens record exists before FIN-09 is called verified. — `scripts/financial-render.mjs --check + tests/financial-crs.test.mjs (shipped) — ADD: docs/financial-2525/reviews/r001_twelve_lenses.md exists with twelve lens sections and its hash is on the ledger`

**Risks.**
- CALENDAR: “day 365 is the down day” is a mean, not a law. From the shipped SOURCED table, three of eleven orbit-years are shorter than the 364-day grid — FY2028 363.24 d (Q4 day 91 cut by 18.25 h), FY2031 363.35 d (−15.62 h), FY2034 363.84 d (−3.88 h) — with no down day at all; FY2025's down day lasts 3.8 h, FY2026's 9.3 h, and FY2024/2027/2032 hold 3+ down days. positionInYear survives it (the year flips at the instant) but the gate asserts the grid only for FY2026, and a business quarter drawn on this will be wrong one year in four.
- TWO MoTs ON ONE SURFACE: the glass computes the example at 30.333 d (8.25213 ¢/min, ends 2026-10-31 14:59:31 CST) and the gate proves 91/3 (8.25204 ¢/min, ends 28.8 s later); the 1e-3 tolerance in financial-mot cannot see the difference.
- THE LAW AGAINST THE OPERATOR'S OWN SHEET: with forward release, the sheet's fixed bills ($2,750 = 76.3 % of $3,604.49) are only fully released by day 23.14 of the MoT; Home $700 by day 5.89, Car $1,800 by day 15.15, all sheet expenses ($3,400 = 94.3 %) by day 28.61; at +3 h only $14.85 is withdrawable. A monthly-paid person cannot pay a first-of-month rent from escrow. This is arithmetic, not a defect — but it must be on the record before business testing, and it is the first thing feedback will say.
- CHART SAMPLING: step = MoT ÷ 120 = 6.067 h; the withdrawable line first rises at 6.07 h while the hold mark is drawn at 3 h — the chart contradicts its own mark.
- $/s PRECISION: usd4 prints $0.0014 for $0.001375 (+1.8 %) — the sheet's own “0.0016” truncation, now in code.
- THE STILL NUMBER: at 8.252 ¢/min the 2-decimal big number changes every 7.27 s under a 1 Hz clock; six of every seven ticks show no movement.
- ID COLLISION: deposit ids are `d-${when}-${cents}`; two deposits of equal amount at one stamp collapse silently (append is idempotent by id) and the glass says nothing — a business paying two people, or a person twice, loses one.
- availableCents = max(0, withdrawable − withdrawn) hides an over-withdrawal appended from another device; verify() proves the chain, not the balance law.
- ONE DEPOSIT CHARTED, ALL DEPOSITS SUMMED: with two overlapping deposits the number (balanceAt over all) and the chart (focus = newest) disagree.
- DAY ROLL AT 11:15 CST (FY2026), 20:33 (FY2027), 06:28 (FY2028): instant-aligned days are right by the sheet's own range, but the roll time is not printed and will read as a broken counter.
- THREE INCOMES IN ONE ASK: $3,604.49/30.333 d, sheet Income 3,200/M33 (nets −$200), sheet ladder 144 $/D (4,752/M33); the budget panel today shows the sheet seed beside the real paycheck without saying which is his.
- FIN-09 is “approved” with the metric “a review record exists for the revision”, yet reviews[0] is PENDING and docs/financial-2525/reviews/ does not exist; the twelve-lens record must be persisted (hash on the ledger) before that row can be verified.
- TWO PERIHELION TABLES: lib/soi-calendar.ts PERIHELION_UTC (2024 00:39 · 2026 17:16 · 2028 12:11) vs lib/financial-2525/calendar.ts (00:38 · 17:15 · 12:28); the domain records only the 2028 discrepancy.
- EFFICIENCY: series() (120 × balanceAt × deposits) re-runs on every 1 Hz render though it depends only on the record — negligible for one person, wasteful for a business roster.
- TOUCH TARGET: the A.B..C toggle (px-2 py-1 text-xs) is under the 44 px minimum the rest of the Session shell keeps.
- $/Y at 365 (LTU_DAYS.Y) is an analysis convention; the calendar year is 363.24–367.54 d, so an annual figure is off by up to 0.7 % from the orbit it claims — label it as the sheet's convention.

Then r.003's decisions FD-13… settle the thirteen ambiguities above by name.

### Thor — risk and security stress testing: a person's real money and bank data, privacy, per-user isolation (Auth0 is not wired into Supabase; the app's only cloud isolation is a bearer owner_key), what must never be shown or stored, escrow fraud

**Readiness:** B-

**One sentence.** Financial-2525's time law is now right and gated at r.003, but its money law is a diary that trusts the phone's clock, the payee's keyboard and a clamped balance, so nothing may ever pay out from it until the hold is measured by a clock the person does not own, the deposit is stamped by the payer, a withdrawal is checked at every instant on the record, and the operator's real paycheck stays public only by consent written on the record.

**Ambiguities → declared default.**
- **Whose clock measures the 3-hour hold and the $/min release — the person's phone or the platform?** → The platform's witnessed instant (the pod-clock law, lib/pod-clock.ts measure/witnessed), never the device; until a platform clock exists, every balance carries the caveat 'as this phone tells the time' and no rail, message or business escrow reads availableCents. — _Executed against the shipped law: with the device clock set +3 h at the deposit instant, 1,485 cents become withdrawable. Harmless in a diary; the fraud the moment money moves on that number._
- **Who stamps a deposit's day, time and amount — the payer or the payee?** → Personal pilot: the person types it and every typed row is badged SELF-RECORDED (it records a bank fact, it is not a claim on anyone's escrow). Business frame: the payer's message stamps it (FIN-04's stretch) and a payee-typed deposit is refused. — _Executed: a deposit typed 40 days in the past is 100 % withdrawable at entry (360,449 of 360,449 cents)._
- **May a withdrawal be dated in the future, or earlier than an existing later entry?** → No: a withdrawal's instant is at most now and at least the latest entry on the record, and validation runs at the frontier so availableCents is never clamped up from a negative. — _Executed: a withdrawal dated after the MoT for the whole amount validates (checked against the future balance); a $10 withdrawal at +3 h then also validates; at +32 d the record is over-withdrawn by 1,000 cents and Math.max(0, …) shows available 0 — the invariant 'never negative' is hidden, not held._
- **Is the operator's real paycheck ($3,604.49, posted 2026-09-29) and his budget meant to be shown to every unsigned visitor of the public site and kept in the public repository?** → Yes, as his own worked example, but recorded as consent: example.consent = 'operator 2026-09-30, addendum 2' in the domain JSON (absent today), EXAMPLE.payer stays 'example', and a gate refuses any user's row ever becoming the example. — _The repository answers 200 unauthenticated and domain.gen.ts ships the figure to every visitor before sign-in; consent is inferred from the ask, not written on the record._
- **What does 'withdraw to their account' mean in the pilot — a rail or a record?** → A record of what the person moved at their own bank; the surface says so in one sentence; no payout rail is built until the business frame and until the clock and stamper questions above are answered. — _Money never moves from a number the person's own device computed; there is no payout code today and that is the safest state._
- **What is the cloud half and how is one person's record isolated from another's?** → No cloud half until Auth0 is wired into Supabase (third-party auth so auth.uid() is real) and a financial table has ONLY an auth.uid() policy; any interim mirror uses migration 030's SECURITY DEFINER RPC pattern with the CSPRNG owner key and never a direct table policy; no financial migration exists today and none should be written under owner_key. — _Migration 030 in its own words: 'possession of the owner_key UUID is the authorisation' — a money table on a bearer key is readable and appendable by whoever holds a key that lives in localStorage._
- **What may a memo hold, and what must never be stored or shown?** → A memo refuses eight or more consecutive digits and anything shaped like an account, routing or card number; the record has no field for an account identifier; the bank screenshot never enters the repository (already the rule). — _The memo is free text and the person will paste the bank line; the ask already carries the masked descriptor, and an unmasked one would render in the roster and ledger and sync to any future cloud half._
- **When a device record fails its chain, what happens to the bytes?** → The failed store is moved aside under exel-fin:<owner>:broken:<stamp> before any write; the person is told; nothing is overwritten. — _Today loadRecord returns an empty record on a broken chain and the next deposit's saveRecord writes the fresh record to the same key — the person's damaged-but-present history is destroyed by the act of recording again, while fin.chain_broken promises 'never repaired silently'._
- **Which revolution length does a MoT LENGTH use — the mean anomalistic year (365.2596 d) or the sourced perihelion interval of the year it falls in (364.39 d for 2026)?** → The same interval the instant uses (the year the deposit falls in), so a MoT's span in A equals its end position minus its start position; the mean only when no year is known. — _As coded, the chart's reveal reads the axis marks from positionInYear (364.39 d) but the elapsed span from spanABC (365.26 d): a 30.333-day MoT spans 299.7 A on the axis and 298.97 A in the label — a 0.7 A (about 1.7 h) discrepancy a careful reader will see as two answers._
- **What does Financial-2525 tokenize under the 9,999 웃 cap?** → Nothing in the personal pilot mints or settles 웃; the cap is read from lib/pod-yug.ts and shown only when a POD's witnessed hour settles onto the record (FIN-10 stays draft). — _The mint is currency-free by a published ruling and this surface is a currency record; blending them is the exact class of error pod-yug's header documents._
- **Two deposits with the same stamp and amount — one record or two?** → Two: the id carries the record's next rev as a nonce, and when an append is a no-op the surface says 'already recorded' instead of clearing the form. — _id = d-<when>-<cents> collapses identical rows silently; a real second paycheck could vanish with no message._
- **Is the DECLARED perihelion fallback (Jan 3 12:00 UTC) acceptable outside 2024–2035, and which of the two perihelion tables is the one source?** → Yes, shown as DECLARED (already coded); and ONE table — fold calendar.ts's sourced rows into lib/soi-calendar.ts PERIHELION_UTC so the 2028 disagreement (12:28 vs 12:11 UTC) is resolved, not merely recorded. — _The year boundary decides which 33-day frame and quarter a deposit falls in; two tables are two answers._

**MoT law, as this lens read it.** A.B..C is the Earth's revolution (addendum 10; on the record as FD-13 and FIN-01.01 at r.003, commit 13028c0): one perihelion-to-perihelion revolution — the financial year — is 3600 A; each A is 3600 B; each B is 3600 C (1 A = 12,960,000 C). On Earth one A is about 8,766 s (2.435 h), one B about 2.435 s, one C about 0.68 ms. A pay period of 30.333 days (91 ÷ 3) is a LENGTH, so it converts INTO the scale: 30.3333 ÷ 365.259636 × 3600 ≈ 298.965 A, so A = 298, B = 3475, C ≈ 1826, written 298.3475..1826 (the gate pins A = 298 and the round trip back to 30.333 d; B and C follow from the anomalistic-year constant). 'MoT = 30.33 converted to 3600.3600..3600' therefore means: expressed on the scale whose FULL reading is one whole revolution, 30.333 days is about 298.97 of the 3600 A-units — it does not mean the pay period itself runs 0 → 3600.3600..3600; that was the r.001–r.002 reading, now superseded on the record, and the sheet's three Equal writings (3600.0000..0000 · 3600.3600..3600 · 3599.3599..3599) all read FULL = one revolution. An INSTANT's A.B..C is its position in the current revolution measured from the sourced perihelion: the worked deposit 2026.10.01_07.00..00 CST = 13:00 UTC is about 270.82 d after the 2026 perihelion (2026-01-03 17:15 UTC) in a 364.39-d revolution → about 2675.22xx; day 271, Q3 day 89 of 91, 33-day frame 9 day 7; its MoT ends about day 301 (Q4 day 28) near 2975.1xxx. Converting to Earth LTU: days = (A + B/3600 + C/3600²) ÷ 3600 × revolution-days, so 30.333 d = 728 h = 43,680 min = 2,620,800 s; the sheet's analysis units stay D 1 · W 7 · M 33 · Q 99 · Y 365 (the pay MoT 30.333 and the analysis month 33 coexist by declaration FD-04). Money on the scale: $3,604.49 ÷ 43,680 min = $0.08252/min = $4.951/h = $118.83/day = $0.001375/s, about $12.06 per A-unit. CST standard (UTC−6, no daylight shift) only NAMES the day; the stored instants are universal, and the record correctly stores the UTC instant and derives A.B..C on read — an A.B..C written into a record would change if a DECLARED perihelion later became SOURCED. Mars: the same functions with sols and 668.5991 sols per revolution (a 30-sol MoT is 161 A). One inconsistency to close: an instant uses the sourced interval (364.39 d in 2026) while a length uses the mean (365.26 d), so the same MoT reads 299.7 A across the chart's axis and 298.97 A in its label.

**Chart.** axes: x = time across the focused deposit's MoT, from the deposit instant (2026.10.01_07.00..00 CST) to deposit + MoT (30.333 d), five marks labelled in Earth LTU by default (0 · 7 d 14 h · 15 d 4 h · 22 d 18 h · 30 d 8 h) and, on the A.B..C reveal, the revolution's coordinate at each mark (about 2675.22xx → 2975.1xxx); y = dollars from $0 to the deposit amount ($3,604.49) — the y axis is this deposit, never the sum of deposits. Under the chart the big mono number is money released so far, with the ladder $/h · $/day · $/s and the elapsed time in h:mm:ss and in A-units. · series: released — the linear ramp $0 → $3,604.49 at $0.0825/min, abundance stroke, normal width (the one that moves) · withdrawable — $0 until +3 h, then it joins the released line, consciousness stroke, normal width · escrowed — the mirror ramp $3,604.49 → $0, intelligence stroke, hairline · available (withdrawable minus withdrawn) is not a line; it is the number on the phone strip and it must never be shown clamped from a negative · marks: the 3-hour hold: a tick on the baseline at deposit + 3 h (temporal colour) · NOW: a vertical hairline while inside the MoT · each withdrawal: a tick from the top at its instant (evolution colour), amount in the ledger panel · the phase pill on the Session rail: DEPOSIT → HOLD (h:mm:ss countdown) → RELEASE → WITHDRAW → RECORD · Thor adds two marks: a SELF-RECORDED badge on every row the person typed (PAYER-STAMPED once a payer message exists) and a one-line caveat 'as this phone tells the time' until the hold is measured by a platform clock · states: pending — before the deposit instant: the curves are flat at 0; guide says 'releases from <stamp>' → escrowed · releasing · held — 0 to 3 h: released is visible and rising, withdrawable is 0, the rail counts the hold down → releasing · withdrawable — from +3 h to the end of the MoT: withdrawable = released, Withdraw enabled on the phone strip when available > 0 ($14.85 at exactly +3 h) → released — MoT complete: escrow 0, whole amount released, phase RECORD → overlays the person must see plainly: EXAMPLE (unsigned; the operator's paycheck as data), ON THIS DEVICE ONLY, CHAIN BROKEN (moved aside, never overwritten), SAVE FAILED (kept in memory, said so) · cadence: One 1 Hz clock (setInterval 1000) feeds every pure function; the big number changes by $0.0014 each second (visible at four decimals, every ~12 s at cents). Today every tick recomputes balanceAt for 120 series samples × all transactions; declared default: memoize the series per sample bucket (len/120 ≈ 6 h) and tick only the number and the NOW line, so a business ledger of thousands of rows does not make a phone recompute a quadratic every second. · phone: 360×150 viewBox at 100 % width, legible at 390 px; the fixed bottom strip carries released · available · phase · Withdraw (44 px targets); the legend wraps; the A.B..C reveal should not swap the axis text under the thumb — make it a details row or long-press; the Withdraw button is never enabled without the SELF-RECORDED badge and the phone-time caveat in view; no screen the person reaches names Supabase, a login wiring, a hash or a chain — it speaks of their money and their next action.

**Invariants.**
- Money released is never more than money deposited. — `financial-accrual (exists: 'released money never exceeds the deposit, however long after')`
- A withdrawal never takes more than is withdrawable, and the balance never goes negative — at every instant on the record, not only at the instant that was typed. — `financial-invariant (new, the pod-invariant pattern): refuses a withdrawal dated after now or before the latest entry, re-validates at the frontier, and asserts availableCents is never clamped up from a negative`
- The hold is three hours after the deposit, measured by a clock the person does not own. — `financial-invariant: source-level — no payout path reads Date.now(); the surface carries the phone-time caveat until a platform clock (pod-clock measure) stamps the record`
- A deposit's day and time is stamped by whoever put the money in; the person may record their own bank fact, badged SELF-RECORDED, and may never assign a deposit to someone else's escrow. — `financial-invariant + financial-surface: every typed row renders the badge; the business frame refuses a payee-typed deposit`
- A transaction is amount · deposit day and time · MoT — always the three fields, never a fourth that names a bank account. — `financial-voice (new, the sign-lexicon-voice pattern): the memo refuses account-shaped digit runs; no field is named account, routing, IBAN or card`
- The record is append-only; a correction is a new entry; nothing on the record is ever overwritten — including a record whose chain failed. — `financial-invariant: a broken chain is moved aside under a :broken:<stamp> key before any write; financial-accrual already pins append/replay/verify/idempotence`
- Each user their own login and their own record; no screen ever shows another person's money. — `financial-surface (exists: forms only for the signed-in owner, record keyed to user.sub) + financial-rls (new, when a table lands): only an auth.uid() policy, no bearer policy on money`
- The 9,999 웃 cap is the pod's, read from lib/pod-yug.ts, and nothing on this surface mints or settles. — `financial-crs (exists: YUG_CEILING reused) + financial-invariant: no mint( or settle( call site on the financial surface until FIN-10 is approved`
- A.B..C is the revolution; the glass reads day · hour · minute; the record stores the instant and derives A.B..C on read. — `financial-mot + financial-surface (exist, r.003)`
- The year resets at perihelion, CST standard names the day, and a year outside the sourced table says DECLARED. — `financial-mot (exists)`
- A new edition is an append: the JSON revisions[], the traceability ledger and the rendered views move together, and the render check is green before the commit. — `financial-crs (exists: --check, strictly increasing revisions, handoff hash byte-for-byte)`
- The operator's figures are data on the record with his written consent, and a user's figures are never an example. — `financial-crs (new assertion): example.consent present; EXAMPLE.payer === 'example'; no user record path feeds the example`

- frontend/lib/innovation-store.ts (ownerKeyOrNull, fail-closed CSPRNG key) + supabase/migrations/030_innovation_state_rls_hardening.sql — the only acceptable interim cloud pattern (SECURITY DEFINER RPC by exact key), and the migration path to auth.uid()
